P033-002 §1 This standard defines the DDR5 SDRAM specification, including features, functionalities, AC and DC characteristics, packages, and ball/signal assignments.
P033-003 §1 The purpose of this Standard is to define the minimum set of requirements for JEDEC compliant 8 Gb through 32 Gb for x4, x8, and x16 DDR5 SDRAM devices.
P033-004 §1 This standard was created based on the DDR4 standards (JESD79-4) and some aspects of the DDR, DDR2, DDR3, and LPDDR4 standards (JESD79, JESD79-2, JESD79-3, and JESD209-4).
P033-005 §1.1 JEDEC publication JM7 provides examples and directives for the use of verbal forms (e.g., ‘shall’ compared with ‘should’ and ‘may’ compared with ‘can’).
P034-001 §2.1 The DDR5 SDRAM x4/x8 component shall have 13 electrical rows of balls.
P034-002 §2.1 Electrical is defined as rows that contain signal ball or power/ground balls.
P034-003 §2.1 There may be additional rows of inactive balls for mechanical support.
P034-004 §2.2 The DDR5 SDRAM component shall use a ball pitch of 0.8mm by 0.8mm.
P034-005 §2.2 The number of fully depopulated columns is 3.
P034-006 §2.3 The DDR5 SDRAM x4/x8 component shall have 6 electrical columns of balls in 2 sets of 3 columns.
P034-007 §2.3 There shall be columns between the electrical columns where there are no balls populated.
P034-008 §2.3 The number of these is 3.
P034-009 §2.3 Electrical is defined as columns that contain signal ball or power/ground balls.
P034-010 §2.3 There may be additional columns of inactive balls for mechanical support.
P035-017 §2.4 NOTE 1 DQ4-DQ7 are higher order DQ pins and are not connected for the x4 configuration.
P035-018 §2.4 NOTE 2 TDQS_t is not valid for the x4 configuration
P035-019 §2.4 NOTE 3 TDQS_c is not available for the x4 configuration4 - DM_n not valid for the x4 configuration
P035-020 §2.4 MO-210-AL (x4/x8) MO-210-AN (x4/x8) with support balls
P035-025 §2.4 1. Additional columns and rows of inactive balls in MO-210 Terminal Pattern AN (x4/x8) with support balls are for mechanical support only, and should not be tied to either electrically high or low.
P035-026 §2.4 2. Some of the additional support balls can be selectively populated under the supplier’s discretion.
P035-027 §2.4 Refer to supplier’s datasheet.
P036-039 §2.5 MO-210-AT (x16) MO-210-AU (x16) with support balls
P036-045 §2.5 1. Additional columns and rows of inactive balls in MO-210 Terminal Pattern AU (x16) with support balls are for mechanical support only, and should not be tied to either electrically high or low.
P036-046 §2.5 2. Some of the additional support balls can be selectively populated under the supplier’s discretion.
P036-047 §2.5 Refer to supplier’s datasheet.
P039-025 §2.7 NOTE 1 For non-binary memory densities, a quarter of the row address space is invalid.
P039-026 §2.7 When the MSB address bit is “HIGH”, the MSB-1 address bit shall be “LOW”.
P041-001 §3.1 This Simplified State Diagram is intended to provide an overview of the possible state transitions and the commands to control them.
P041-002 §3.1 In particular, situations involving more than one bank, the enabling or disabling of on-die termination, and some other events are not captured in full detail.
P041-045 §3.1 PDA Select ID Mode
P042-001 §3.2 The DDR5 SDRAM is a high-speed dynamic random-access memory.
P042-002 §3.2 To ease transition from DDR4 to DDR5, the introductory density (8Gb) shall be internally configured as 16-bank, 8 bank group with 2 banks for each bank group for x4/x8 and 8-bank, 4 bank group with 2 banks for each bank group for x16 DRAM.
P042-003 §3.2 When the industry transitions to higher densities (=>16Gb), it doubles the bank resources and internally be configured as 32-bank, 8 bank group with 4 banks for each bank group for x4/x8 and 16-bank, 4 bank group with 4 banks for each bank group for x16 DRAM.
P042-005 §3.2 The 16n prefetch architecture is combined with an interface designed to transfer two data words per clock cycle at the I/O pins.
P042-006 §3.2 A single read or write operation for the DDR5 SDRAM consists of a single 16n-bit wide, eight clock data transfer at the internal DRAM core and sixteen corresponding n-bit wide, one-half clock cycle data transfers at the I/O pins.
P042-007 §3.2 Read and write operation to the DDR5 SDRAM are burst oriented, start at a selected location, and continue for a burst length of sixteen or a ‘chopped’ burst of eight in a programmed sequence.
P042-008 §3.2 Operation begins with the registration of an ACTIVATE Command, which is then followed by a Read or Write command.
P042-009 §3.2 The address bits registered with the ACTIVATE Command are used to select the bank and row to be activated (i.e. in a 16Gb part, BG0-BG2 in a x4/8 and BG0-BG1 in x16 select the bank group; BA0-BA1 select the bank; R0-R17 select the row; refer to “DDR5 SDRAM Addressing” for specific requirements).
P042-010 §3.2 The address bits registered with the Read or Write command are used to select the starting column location for the burst operation, determine if the auto precharge command is to be issued (CA10=L), and select BC8 on-the-fly (OTF), fixed BL16, fixed BL32 (optional), or BL32 OTF (optional) mode if enabled in the mode register.
P042-011 §3.2 Prior to normal operation, the DDR5 SDRAM must be powered up and initialized in a predefined manner.
P042-012 §3.2 The following clauses provide detailed information covering device reset and initialization, register definition, command descriptions, and device operation.
P043-001 §3.3 For power-up and reset initialization, in order to prevent DRAM from functioning improperly, default values for the following MR settings need to be defined.
P043-025 §3.3.1 The following sequence shall be used to power up the DDR5 device.
P043-026 §3.3.1 Unless specified otherwise, these steps are mandatory.
P043-027 §3.3.1 1. While applying power (after Ta), RESET_n is recommended to be LOW (≤0.2 x VDDQ) and all other inputs may be undefined.
P043-028 §3.3.1 The device outputs remain disabled while RESET_n is held LOW.
P043-029 §3.3.1 Power supply voltage ramp requirements are provided in Table 10.
P043-030 §3.3.1 VPP shall ramp at the same time or earlier than VDD.
P043-034 §3.3.1 1) Ta is the point when any power supply first reaches 300mV
P043-035 §3.3.1 2) Voltage ramp conditions in the table above apply between (Ta) and Power-off (controlled or uncontrolled).
P043-036 §3.3.1 3) Tb is the point at which all supply and reference voltages are within their defined ranges.
P043-037 §3.3.1 4) Power ramp duration (Tb-Ta) shall not exceed tINIT0.
P043-038 §3.3.1 2. Following the completion of the voltage ramp (Tb), RESET_n shall be maintained LOW.
P043-039 §3.3.1 DQ, DQS_t, DQS_c, voltage levels shall be between VSS and VDDQ to avoid latch-up.
P043-040 §3.3.1 CS_n, CK_t, CK_c, and CA input levels shall be between VSS and VDDQ to avoid latch-up.
P043-041 §3.3.1 3. Beginning at Tb, RESET_n shall be maintained LOW for a minimum of tINIT1 (Tb to Tc), after which RESET_n may be deasserted to HIGH (Tc).
P043-042 §3.3.1 At least tINIT2 before RESET_n de-assertion, CS_n is required to be set LOW.
P043-043 §3.3.1 All other input signals are “Don’t Care”.
P043-044 §3.3.1 The DRAM shall support the ability for RESET_n to be held indefinitely.
P044-004 §3.3.1 Power Ramp Reset Initialization Configuration ODT Async On CS CMOS Registration &
P044-094 §3.3.1 1. From time point (Td) until (Te), the command/address (CA) bus shall be held high in a NOP encoded state.
P044-095 §3.3.1 2. From time point (Te) until (Tf), NOP commands shall be applied on the command/address (CA) bus.
P044-096 §3.3.1 3. From time point (Tf) until (Tk), DES commands must be applied between legal commands (MRR, MRW, MPC, VREFCS & VREFCA).
P044-097 §3.3.1 MRR and MRW command, while legal, may not execute properly until CS and CA training routines are completed.
P044-098 §3.3.1 4. Prior to ZQcal completion at (Tk), MPC commands shall be Multi-Cycle as described in the MPC command Timings section.
P044-099 §3.3.1 5. From time point (Tk) until (Tl) all MPC, VREFCS and VREFCA commands prior to CS and CA training successfully completing, shall be Multi-Cycle (MR2:OP[4]=0) as described in the MPC, VREFCS, and VREFCA timing sections.
P044-100 §3.3.1 6. At time point (Tl), with successful completion of CS and CA training, an MRW command setting MR2:OP[4] =1 is recommended and allows for MPC, VREFCS, VREFCA commands to be single-cycle, improving training duration.
P044-101 §3.3.1 7. From time point (Tk) until (Tz), DES commands shall be applied between legal commands (MRR, MRW, MPC, VREFCS & VREFCA).
P044-102 §3.3.1 8. Starting at TI, MRW Commands shall be issued to all Mode Registers that require defined settings.
P044-103 §3.3.1 9. Default ODT tolerances are wider prior to ZQ calibration.
P044-104 §3.3.1 10, All MPC/MRW/MRR to MPC/MRW/MRR commands shall meet the timing restrictions required.
P044-105 §3.3.1 4. After RESET_n is de-asserted (Tc), wait at least tINIT3 before driving CS_n high.
P044-106 §3.3.1 5. After setting CS_n high (Td), wait a minimum of tINIT4 to allow the DRAM CMOS based receiver to register the exit and allow the CS_n, CK, CA, DQ and DQS ODT to go to the defined strap or MRS state (Te).
P044-107 §3.3.1 Clock (CK_t, CK_c) is required to be started and stabilized for tCKSRX before exit of tINIT4 (Te).
P044-108 §3.3.1 Upon the completion of Te, all ODT states (CA, CS_n, CK, DQ and DQS ODT) should be valid and the DRAM’s CS_n receiver should no longer be in its CMOS based mode.
P044-109 §3.3.1 ODT termination states will be uncalibrated until completion of ZQcal at (Tj)
P045-001 §3.3.1 6. Upon (Te), NOP commands shall be issued for a minimum of tINIT5 to conclude exit of initialization process and start tXPR timer at (Tf).
P045-002 §3.3.1 The system shall wait at least tXPR before issuing any legal configuration commands (Tg).
P045-003 §3.3.1 During early configuration steps (Tg to Tk), only MRR, MRW, MPC, VREFCS and VREFCA commands are legal.
P045-004 §3.3.1 MRR and MRW command, while legal, may not execute properly until CS and CA training routines are completed.
P045-005 §3.3.1 7. Between (Tg to Tj), the following initial configuration modes shall be completed prior to other training modes: - MPC for setting MR13 (tCCD_L*/tDLLK) shall be issued before the MPC command to reset the DLL. - MPC to execute DLL RESET shall be issued before ZQCal Start - MPC to execute ZQCal Start followed by ZQCal Latch shall be issued before any other training modes such as VrefCS, VrefCA, CS and CA Training.
P045-006 §3.3.1 8. Between (Tk to Tz), any number of legal configuration commands are allowed.
P045-007 §3.3.1 Many training based commands are optional and may be done at the system architect’s discretion and may vary depending on the systems, though proper setting of certain registers, such as those related to Write Leveling Training, is required.
P045-008 §3.3.1 The host shall follow Multi-Cycle MPC timing requirements in cases where the alignment between CS_n, CA, and CK is unknown, (ie., prior to successfully completing VREFCS, VREFCA, CS, and CA training commands or host provided successful training solutions).
P045-009 §3.3.1 MRW and MRR commands can be used once the alignment between CS_n, CA and CK are known.
P045-010 §3.3.1 Single-cycle CS_n assertion are allowed after setting MR2:OP[4]=1.
P045-011 §3.3.1 9. After (Tz), and the completion of any training or calibration timing parameters (i.e. tZQLAT is satisfied), the DDR5 device is ready for normal operation and is able to accept any valid command.
P045-012 §3.3.1 Any additional mode registers that have not previously been set up for normal operation should be written at this time.
P045-013 §3.3.1 If host use writeback suppression mode, it should be set after the initial write process to prevent aliasing to 2-bit errors.
P045-014 §3.3.1 10. After all mode registers have been programmed for normal operation, optional MBIST mode can be entered by writing MR23:OP[4] to HIGH, followed by subsequent MR24 PPR guard keys, then DRAM will drive ALERT_n to LOW for a maximum of tSELFTEST time.
P045-015 §3.3.1 DRAM will drive ALERT_n to HIGH to indicate that this operation is completed.
P045-016 §3.3.1 After ALERT_n is driven high, the DRAM is immediately ready to receive valid commands.
P045-017 §3.3.1 The MBIST/mPPR transparency status shall subsequently be checked in MR22:OP[2:0] in order to determine whether mPPR should be performed.
P045-018 §3.3.1 Please refer to Ch.
P045-030 §3.3.1 NOTE 1 Min number of NOP commands issued after CS_n High (tINIT4).

P046-003 §3.3.2 RESET_n needs to be maintained for a minimum of tPW_RESET.
P046-004 §3.3.2 CS_n shall be pulled LOW at least tINIT2 before de-asserting RESET_n.

P046-113 §3.3.2 3. From time point (Tf) until (Tk), DES commands shall be applied between legal commands (MRR, MRW, MPC, VREFCS & VREFCA).

P047-018 §3.3.3 NOTE 2 1 Mhz bandwidth limited measurement
P047-019 §3.3.3 NOTE 3 VPP shall be equal to or greater than VDD/VDDQ at all times.
P047-020 §3.3.3 NOTE 4 During ramp up and ramp down [VDDQ-VDD] must be equal or less than 200mV.

P048-001 §3.4.1 The Mode Register Read (MRR) command is used to read configuration and status data from the DDR5-SDRAM registers.
P048-002 §3.4.1 The MRR command is initiated with CS_n and CA[13:0] in the proper state as defined by the Command Truth Table.
P048-003 §3.4.1 The mode register address operands (MA[7:0]) allow the user to select one of 256 registers.
P048-004 §3.4.1 The mode register contents are available on the second 8 UI’s of the burst and are repeated across all DQ’s after the CL following the MRR command.
P048-005 §3.4.1 To avoid a potentially worst-case pattern, every odd DQ bit (represented with !) shall have its contents inverted.
P048-006 §3.4.1 Data in the burst (BL0-7) shall be either “0” or “1”, with “1” indicating that the content of the later UI’s (BL8-15) are inverted.
P048-007 §3.4.1 MRR commands require all banks to be idle on the DRAM.
P048-008 §3.4.1 For 3DS, MRR commands require all banks to be idle on all logical ranks, The 3DS base die (CID[3:0]=0x0) is the targeted logical rank for most Mode Register Reads, however the CID setting of MR14:OP[3:0] designates the targeted logical rank for MRR commands to MR14-MR20, MR22 and MR54-MR57, and still requires all banks to be idle not only on the targeted logical rank but also on all other logical ranks.
P048-009 §3.4.1 DQS is toggled for the duration of the MRR burst.
P048-010 §3.4.1 The MRR has a command burst length 16 regardless of the MR0 setting, the training mode or the mode register address.
P048-011 §3.4.1 MRR termination control and ODT timings are the same as for the Read command.
P048-012 §3.4.1 The MRR operation must not be interrupted.
P048-013 §3.4.1 Non-Target ODT encoding is available for MRR, just like a normal Read.
P048-014 §3.4.1 MRR NT ODT termination control and ODT timings are the same as for the Read NT command.
P048-015 §3.4.1 In the case that CRC is enabled, MRR’s output will come with BL18 (BL16 plus 2 CRC-bit), but the host has the option to consider the 17th and 18th bits “don’t care” for MRR handling.
P048-016 §3.4.1 Regardless on if the host uses the 17th and 18th bits, while CRC is enabled, the strobe needs to toggle for BL18.
P048-042 §3.4.1 NOTE 1 The read pre-amble and post-amble of MRR are same as normal read.
P049-010 §3.4.1 NOTE 1 The read pre-amble and post-amble of MRR are same as normal read.
P049-028 §3.4.1 NOTE 1 The read pre-amble and post-amble of MRR are same as normal read.
P050-018 §3.4.1 NOTE 1 The read pre-amble and post-amble of MRR are same as normal read.
P050-019 §3.4.1 NOTE 2 Output of mode register data is only duplicated and inverted across the first 8 bits of a x16 device.
P050-038 §3.4.1 NOTE 1 The read pre-amble and post-amble of MRR are same as normal read.
P050-039 §3.4.1 NOTE 2 Output of mode register data is only duplicated and inverted across the first 8 bits of a x16 device when reading from a DFE register associated with a lower byte DQ or DML.
P050-040 §3.4.1 NOTE 3 Output map is ONLY for per bit DFE, DCA and VrefDQ mode registers (MR103 through MR255)
P051-018 §3.4.1 NOTE 1 The read pre-amble and post-amble of MRR are same as normal read.
P051-019 §3.4.1 NOTE 2 Output of mode register data is only duplicated and inverted across the last 8 bits of a x16 device when reading from a DFE register associated with an upper byte DQ or DMU.
P051-020 §3.4.1 NOTE 3 Output map is ONLY for per bit DFE, DCA and VrefDQ mode registers (MR103 through MR255)
P052-001 §3.4.2 The Mode Register Write (MRW) command is used to write configuration data to the mode registers.
P052-002 §3.4.2 The MRW command is initiated with CS_n and CA[13:0] in the proper state as defined by the Command Truth Table.
P052-003 §3.4.2 The mode register address and the data written to the mode registers is contained in CA[13:0] according to the Command Truth Table.
P052-004 §3.4.2 The MRW command period is defined by tMRW.
P052-005 §3.4.2 Mode Register Writes to read-only registers have no impact on the functionality of the device.
P052-006 §3.4.2 MRW commands require all banks to be idle on the DRAM.
P052-007 §3.4.2 For 3DS, MRW commands are broadcast across all logical ranks, requiring all banks to be idle on all logical ranks.
P052-008 §3.4.2 For 3DS, MBIST Enable (MR23:OP[4]=1) is only enabled on the target logical rank designated by CID[3:0] and programmed by MRW via MR14:OP[3:0], but still requires all banks to be idle on all logical ranks.
P052-029 §3.4.3 This Mode Register update timing parameter applies for MR112 (MA[7:0]=70H) thru MR248 (MA[7:0]=F8H) - Mode Registers for DFE including DFE Gain Bias, DFE Tap-1, DFE Tap-2, DFE Tap-3, DFE Tap-4 mode registers
P053-028 §3.4.4 tODTLoff_RD_DQS = CL-1-tRPRE-(Read DQS offset)
P053-032 §3.4.4 tODTLon_RD_DQS = CL+BL/2-0.5+tRPST-(Read DQS offset)
P053-060 §3.4.4 ODTLoff_RD_NT_offset Mode Register Control Window (shown with offset +3 used)
P053-061 §3.4.4 ODTLon_RD_NT_offset Mode Register Control Window (shown with offset -1 used)
P053-063 §3.4.4 Read DQS Offset programmed at 0.
P053-064 §3.4.4 2 - Timing constraints for 1-cycle MPC/VrefCS/VrefCA commands are affected by ODTLoff_RD_NT_Offset the same as MRW in the diagram.
P054-005 §3.4.4 NOTE 1 For 3DS, both MRR and MRW commands require all banks to be idle on all logical ranks.
P054-022 §3.4.4 NOTE 1 All data should be completed before entry into self refresh or power down.
P054-023 §3.4.4 NOTE 2 MRR can refer to both Target ODT MRR and Non-Target ODT MRR
P054-024 §3.4.4 NOTE 3 Minimum delay is 1 clock after the burst or until the ODT offset requirements for DQS/DQ are met.
P054-025 §3.4.4 NOTE 4 During Read Training, MRR to MRR command spacing can be tMRR_p
P056-001 §3.5 With DDR5, the utilization and programming method shall change from the traditional addressing scheme found in DDR3 and DDR4, and shall move to the method used by LPDDR, where the Mode Register Addresses (MRA) and Payload placed in Op Codes (OP) are all packeted in the command bus encoding method.
P056-002 §3.5 Please refer to the Command Truth Table 31 for Mode Register Read (MRR) and Mode Register Write (MRW) command protocol.
P056-003 §3.5 For DDR5, the SDRAM shall support up to 8 MRA’s, each with a byte-wide payload.
P056-006 §3.5.1 Each bit in a register byte (MR#) is denoted as "R" if it can be read but not written, "W" if it can be written but reads shall always produce a ZERO for those specific bits, and "R/W" if it can be read and written.
P056-007 §3.5.1 Additionally, a DRAM read-only bit combined with a Host write-only bit is denoted as a “SR/W” bit.
P056-008 §3.5.1 This bit allows the DRAM to return a defined status during a read of that bit (SR = Status Read), independent of what the Host may have written to the bit.
P056-009 §3.5.1 A defined register byte (MR#), is any MR# that has at least one of the bits defined.
P056-010 §3.5.1 When the entire MR# is marked RFU, then it is considered undefined and all the bits from the DRAM shall be don’t care for reads or writes.
P056-011 §3.5.1 These undefined mode registers (completely empty bytes, not individual bits of an MR) may not be supported in the DRAM.
P056-012 §3.5.1 When a defined register byte (MR#) contains an “RFU” bit, the host must write a ZERO for those specific bits and the DRAM does not guarantee any operation of those specific RFU bits.
P056-013 §3.5.1 When the host issues an MRR to a defined register (MR#) that contains RFU bits in it, those specific bits shall always produce a ZERO.
P056-014 §3.5.1 For cases in which a mode register is specific to a particular device configuration (x16, x8, x4) and/or density (32 Gb, 16 Gb, 8 Gb), the following rules shall be applied: - When the DRAM is configured as a x4/x8, an entire MR# used only for a x16 shall be considered RFU.
P056-015 §3.5.1 These bits are don’t care for reads and writes, and they may be unsupported. - When a bit field within a register is used by a different configuration or density than a given DRAM, the host may write/read programmed values to these fields, but DRAM operation will not be affected.
P056-016 §3.5.1 A Mode Register Read command is used to read a mode register.
P056-017 §3.5.1 A Mode Register Write command is used to write a mode register
P065-012 §3.5.4 NOTE 1 To enter WL Training Mode the MR field must be programmed to 1.
P065-013 §3.5.4 WL Training Mode is used when Internal Write Timing = 0 (External WL Training) and when Internal Write Timing = 1 (Internal WL Training).
P065-014 §3.5.4 NOTE 2 To exit WL Training Mode the MR field must be programmed to 0.
P065-015 §3.5.4 NOTE 3 MRR’s are not supported during Write Leveling.
P065-016 §3.5.4 NOTE 4 This mode register is programmed via an explicit MPC command only.
P065-017 §3.5.4 NOTE 5 This is set during WL Training, after the host DQS has been aligned to the ideal External WL timings.
P065-018 §3.5.4 The Internal Write Timing is enabled and the WL Internal Timing Alignment is set to ensure the internal Write Enable aligns within tDQS2CK of the external WL Trained location.
P065-019 §3.5.4 When Internal Write Timing is Disabled, the WL Internal Cycle Alignment setting does not change the behavior of the write timings
P066-006 §3.5.5 NOTE 1 This is set during WL Training, after the host DQS has been aligned to the ideal External WL timings.
P066-007 §3.5.5 The Internal Write Timing is enabled and the WL Internal Timing Alignment is set to ensure the internal Write Enable aligns within tDQS2CK of the external WL Trained location.
P066-008 §3.5.5 When Internal Write Timing is Disabled, the WL Internal Cycle Alignment setting does not change the behavior of the write timings.
P066-009 §3.5.5 NOTE 2 The DRAM implementation may optionally have the same behavior when the Internal Write Timing is enabled vs disabled.
P066-011 §3.5.5 The WL Internal Cycle Alignment setting must still support pulling the Internal WL Pulse earlier so that the same WL Training Flow will produce the correct result.
P066-012 §3.5.5 NOTE 3 Lower Byte WL Internal Cycle Alignment is intended for x4, x8, and x16 configurations.
P066-013 §3.5.5 NOTE 4 Upper Byte WL Internal Cycle Alignment is intended for x16 configuration only.
P066-014 §3.5.5 Although training of the Lower and Upper Bytes is independent, contact the DRAM vendor regarding recommendations for setting the WICA values to the same offset.
P066-015 §3.5.5 NOTE 5 Optional OPcode may be needed for certain speed bins.
P068-001 §3.5.6 NOTE 1 The minimum required refresh rate for each OP[2:0] setting applies to tREFI1 and tREFI2.
P068-002 §3.5.6 Each OP[2:0] setting specifies a nominal temperature range.
P068-003 §3.5.6 The ranges defined by OP[2:0] are determined by temperature thresholds used by the system for proper operation.
P068-004 §3.5.6 NOTE 2 When OP[5]=0, the four temperature thresholds are nominally at 80°C, 85°C, 90°C and 95°C.
P068-005 §3.5.6 The <80°C threshold has no minimum value specified and the >95°C threshold has no maximum temperature value specified.
P068-006 §3.5.6 When OP[5]=1, the six temperature thresholds are nominally at 75°C, 80°C, 85°C, 90°C, 95°C, and 100°C.
P068-007 §3.5.6 The <75°C threshold has no minimum value specified and the >100°C threshold has no maximum temperature value specified
P068-008 §3.5.6 NOTE 3 DRAM vendors must report all of the possible settings over the operating temperature range of the device.
P068-009 §3.5.6 Each vendor guarantees that their device will work at any temperature within the range when the system refresh interval follows there guidelines:
P068-010 §3.5.6 Threshold ≤ 85 °C: tREFI x1 (1x Refresh Rate) or faster may be used
P068-011 §3.5.6 Threshold > 85 °C: tREFI /2 (2x Refresh Rate) or faster is required
P068-012 §3.5.6 Data integrity at thresholds >95°C is not assured regardless of refresh rate
P068-013 §3.5.6 NOTE 4 The 2x Refresh Rate must be provided by the system before the DRAM Tj has gone up by more than 2°C (Temperature Margin) since the first report out of OP[2:0]=011B.
P068-014 §3.5.6 This condition is reset when OP[2:0] is equal to 010B.
P068-015 §3.5.6 NOTE 5 The device may not operate properly when OP[2:0]=101B, if the DRAM Tj has gone up by more than 2°C (Temperature Margin) since the first report out of OP[2:0]=101B.
P068-016 §3.5.6 This condition is reset when OP[2:0] is equal to 100B.
P068-017 §3.5.6 OP[2:0]=101B must be a temporary condition of the DRAM, to be addressed by immediately reducing the Tj of the DRAM by throttling its power, and/or the power of nearby devices.
P068-019 §3.5.6 OP[2:0] bits are valid after initialization sequence (Te).
P068-020 §3.5.6 NOTE 7 See the section on “Temperature Sensor” for information on the recommended frequency of reading MR4
P068-021 §3.5.6 NOTE 8 Support for wide range temperature sensor ranges does not indicate that the DDR5 DRAM device may operate properly at temperature ranges above 95°C – side effects may include loss of data integrity
P070-006 §3.5.8 NOTE 1 tWR,min is defined in the “Timing Parameters” tables (Table 323 - Table 325).
P070-007 §3.5.8 Host must operate with MR settings resulting in tCK * MR6:OP[3:0] >= tWR,min.
P070-008 §3.5.8 NOTE 2 tRTP, min is defined in the “Timing Parameters” tables (Table 323 - Table 325).
P070-009 §3.5.8 Host must operate with MR settings resulting in tCK * MR6:OP[7:4]>= tRTP,min.
P070-010 §3.5.8 NOTE 3 All nCK conversions require rounding algorithm consideration.

P082-013 §3.5.23 NOTE 4 The host can step through all possible combinations of MR bit allocation and choose the settings that is best optimized for the system based on the performance metric of interest.

P095-001 §3.5.39 This byte is setup to allow the host controller to push out or pull in the Write RTT enable time (tODTLon_WR) or the Write RTT disable time (tODTLoff_WR) outside of the default setting.

P099-001 §3.5.44 This byte is used for configuring DCA
P100-001 §3.5.44 NOTE 1 When “MRR (or Read) synchronized with IBCLK is blocked” is set by MR42 OP[3:2]=01b, DQs caused by MRR (or Read) synchronized with IBCLK are driven HIGH.
P100-002 §3.5.44 NOTE 2 When “MRR (or Read) synchronized with ICLK is blocked” is set by MR42 OP[3:2]=10b, DQs caused by MRR (or Read) synchronized with ICLK are driven HIGH.
P100-003 §3.5.44 NOTE 3 DQS_t/DQS_c output normal toggling waveforms meaning that DQS_t/DQS_c are not affected by the settings of DCA Assist Mode MR42 OP[3:2].
P100-004 §3.5.44 NOTE 4 The CRC function is not supported during DCA Training Assist Mode.
P100-005 §3.5.44 NOTE 5 DCA Training Assist Mode is only supported by DRAMs with DCAs that have 4-phase internal clocks
P100-006 §3.5.44 NOTE 6 If MR42:OP[3:2] is set to either 01B or 10B, odd-gap READ or odd-gap Mode Register Read Pattern commands should follow the tMRR timing spec.
P100-007 §3.5.44 NOTE 7 The MRR based mode of DCA Training Assist Mode II is enabled when the host sets MR42 OP[6:4]≠000 & OP[7]=0.
P100-008 §3.5.44 DRAM will provide selected predefined pattern when the host issues MRR to MR42 command to the DRAM.
P100-009 §3.5.44 For MRR, issuing any MRR other than to MR42 is invalid and may result in unexpected DQ behavior, requiring the host to issue a Reset to exit MRR based mode and restart the training with an MRR to MR42.The returned data will be a predefined pattern instead of the contents of a mode register.
P100-010 §3.5.44 The timing of the read data return is the same as for an MRR command, including the operation of the strobes (DQS_t, DQS_c).
P100-011 §3.5.44 The host can issue MRW to MR42:OP[6:4]≠000B to change data pattern during MRR based mode.
P100-013 §3.5.44 NOTE 8 Continuous burst output mode is available for DCA Training Assist Mode II and is enabled with MRW to MR42 OP[6:4]≠000B & OP[7]=1.
P100-014 §3.5.44 Once this mode is enabled, MRR to MR42 will start the pattern output and will automatically continue to output the appropriate pattern until it is stopped by either a system reset or issuing an MRW MR42 OP[7]=0 command that reverts it to the “MRR based (Default)” mode.
P100-015 §3.5.44 Issuing any MRR other than to MR42 is invalid and may result in unexpected DQ behavior, requiring the host to issue a Reset to exit continuous burst output mode and restart the training with an MRR to MR42.
P100-016 §3.5.44 Once the MR42 OP[7]=0 “MRR based (Default)” is registered by the DRAM, it will stop all pattern traffic by tCont_Exit.
P100-017 §3.5.44 Since there is no min time for tCont_Exit, the DRAM may stop the pattern prior to tCont_Exit, potentially truncating any current burst pattern.
P100-018 §3.5.44 To ensure that the DRAM’s state-machine doesn’t get into some meta-stability while turning off the output pattern, the host must issue a second MR42 OP[7]=0 “MRR based (Default)” after waiting tMRR, which will then start tCont_Exit_delay.
P100-019 §3.5.44 After tCont_Exit_delay has expired, any other valid command is then legal.
P100-020 §3.5.44 Issuing any MRW other than MRW to MR 42 OP[7]=0 during Continuous Burst Mode after predefined pattern output started is invalid and may result in unexpected DQ behavior, requiring the host to issue a Reset to exit continuous burst output mode and restart the training with an MRR to MR 42
P100-021 §3.5.44 NOTE 9 The DCA Training Assist Mode I and II can not be enabled at the same time by the host.
P100-022 §3.5.44 That is, while the DCA Training Assist Mode II is enabled, the DCA Training Assist Mode I shall be disabled by Host and vice versa.
P100-023 §3.5.44 NOTE 10 Depending on relationship of internal clocks and MRR command, DRAM may start outputting from the 3rd bit of data pattern.
P100-024 §3.5.44 The data pattern is defined with ICLK bases (1st=ICLK, 2nd=QCLK, 3rd=IBCLK and 4th=QBCLK), but MRR burst may start from IBCLK.
P101-001 §3.5.45 This byte is used for configuring TRR
P101-009 §3.5.45 These settings can only be applied if MR42:OP[1:0] =01B or 10B.
P101-010 §3.5.45 NOTE 1 These settings can only be applied if MR42:OP[1:0] =10B.
P102-001 §3.5.46 This byte is used for configuring TRR
P102-008 §3.5.46 NOTE 1 These settings can only be applied if MR42 OP[1:0]=10B.

P104-006 §3.5.48 The DRAM DQS Oscillator count value is used to train DQS to the DQ data valid window.
P104-007 §3.5.48 The value reported by the DRAM in this mode register can be used by the memory controller to periodically adjust the phase of DQS relative to DQ.

P105-013 §3.5.50 NOTE 2 Default is all zeros for OP[7:0]
P106-012 §3.5.51 NOTE 1 When at least one of the two write CRC enable bits is set to ‘1’ in x8, the timing of write CRC enable mode is applied to the entire device (i.e. both nibbles).
P106-013 §3.5.51 When write CRC is enabled in one nibble and disabled in the other nibble in x8, then the DRAM does not check CRC errors on the disabled nibble, and hence the ALERT_n signal and any internal status bit related to CRC error is not impacted by the disabled nibble.

P108-007 §3.5.54 NOTE 1 When Loopback is disabled, both LBDQS and LBDQ pins are either at HiZ or Termination Mode per MR36:OP[2:0].
P108-008 §3.5.54 Loopback Termination default value is 48-ohms
P108-009 §3.5.54 NOTE 2 When Loopback is enabled, both LBDQS and LBDQ pins are in driver mode using default RON of 34 ohms
P108-010 §3.5.54 NOTE 3 Phase A through D selects which bit in the multiplexer is being selected for Loopback output
P108-011 §3.5.54 NOTE 4 This configures the DRAM Loopback output to either send data out every time the DQS toggles in Normal Output Mode, or to only send data out when enabled by the Write command, so that only write burst data is send out via Loopback.
P109-001 §3.5.55 With hPPR, DDR5 can correct one Row address per Bank Group and the Electrical-fuse cannot be switched back to unfused states once it is programmed.
P109-002 §3.5.55 The controller should prevent unintended the hPPR mode entry and repair.
P109-004 §3.5.55 Entry into hPPR is through a register enable, ACT command is used to transmit the bank and row address of the row to be replaced in DRAM.
P109-005 §3.5.55 After tRCD time, a WR command is used to select the individual DRAM through the DQ bits and to transfer the repair address to the DRAM.
P109-006 §3.5.55 After program time, and PRE, the hPPR mode can be exited and normal operation can resume.
P109-020 §3.5.55 NOTE 3 MR14:OP[3:0] applies to CID[3:0] for 3DS-DDR5 and must be setup to indicate which slice in the 3DS DDR5 Stack is referenced in the MR54 through MR57 hPPR resource information.
P110-014 §3.5.56 NOTE 3 MR14:OP[3:0] applies to CID[3:0] for 3DS-DDR5 and must be setup to indicate which slice in the 3DS DDR5 Stack is referenced in the MR54 through MR57 hPPR resource information.
P111-014 §3.5.57 NOTE 3 MR14:OP[3:0] applies to CID[3:0] for 3DS-DDR5 and must be setup to indicate which slice in the 3DS DDR5 Stack is referenced in the MR54 through MR57 hPPR resource information.
P112-014 §3.5.58 NOTE 3 MR14:OP[3:0] applies to CID[3:0] for 3DS-DDR5 and must be setup to indicate which slice in the 3DS DDR5 Stack is referenced in the MR54 through MR57 hPPR resource information.
P113-007 §3.5.59 NOTE 1 Refresh Management settings are vendor specific by the MR settings.
P113-008 §3.5.59 NOTE 2 Only applicable if the Refresh Management Required bit is set to “1” (MR58 OP[0]=1)
P114-009 §3.5.60 NOTE 1 Refresh Management settings are vendor specific by the MR settings.

P116-001 §3.5.62 This MR is used for the characterization of the DRAM package.
P116-002 §3.5.62 Refer to the Package Output Driver Test Mode function for more details.
P118-001 §3.5.64 This MR is used by the host controller to read back Control Words from the RCD.
P118-002 §3.5.64 Control Words are the RCD equivalent of the DRAM MR registers.
P118-003 §3.5.64 The DRAM implements MR63 as a simple read/write register, writable via an MRW to address 3Fh, and readable via an MRR to address 3Fh.
P118-008 §3.5.64 NOTE 1 The contents of this register have no function in the DRAM.
P118-009 §3.5.64 Details for this function can be found in the DDR5 RCD01 Specification.
P118-010 §3.5.64 The following data is just for reference and is not part of the DRAM specification.
P118-011 §3.5.64.1 The method to read an RCD Control Word is as follows:
P118-012 §3.5.64.1 The host controller writes to the RCD’s CW Read Pointer, which selects the RCD control word to be read.
P118-013 §3.5.64.1 The host controller then does an MRW to DRAM MR63.
P118-014 §3.5.64.1 This MRW passes through the RCD to the DRAMs, but is modified by the RCD.
P118-015 §3.5.64.1 The RCD will detect this write to MR63 and replace the data from the host controller with the contents of the RCD register pointed to by the CW Read Pointer.
P118-016 §3.5.64.1 The host controller will then read the DRAM’s MR63, which now contains the value from the desired RCD control word.
P118-017 §3.5.64.1 All DRAMs in the rank will return this same value to the host controller
P118-018 §3.5.65 This MR is reserved for NVRAM paging.

P121-001 §3.5.71 The following mode registers are used to configure the Decision Feedback Equalization (DFE), Per Bit Duty Cycle Adjuster and Per Bit VrefDQ features of the DRAM.
P121-002 §3.5.71 The Mode Registers MA[7:0]=70-FFH are organized in a way such that mode registers for programming of DFE, DCA & VrefDQ configuration per DQ or DM are grouped together.
P121-005 §3.5.71 Looking further into the 8-bit binary encoding, MA[6:3] is defined as a direct mapping for DQL0 to DQU7, i.e., MA[7:0]=1000:0XXXb for DQ0, MA[7:0]=1000:1XXXb for DQ1, : MA[7:0]=1111:0XXXb for DQU6 MA[7:0]=1111:1XXXb for DQU7.
P126-010 §3.5.80 NOTE 1 This bit applies to all DM and DQ pins.

P149-002 §3.5.121 There are currently no plans to utilize these MR addresses.
P150-001 §4.1 (a) Notes 1, 2, and 14 apply to the entire Command truth table (b) To improve command decode time, the table has been optimized to orient all 1-cycle commands together and all 2-cycle commands together; allowing CA1 to be used to identify the difference between a 1-cycle and a 2-cycle command.
P151-008 §4.1 NOTE 1 V means H or L (a defined logic level).
P151-009 §4.1 X means don't care in which case the signal may be floated.
P151-010 §4.1 NOTE 2 Bank group addresses BG[2:0] and Bank addresses BA[1:0] determine which bank is to be operated upon in a specific bank group.
P151-011 §4.1 NOTE 3 The Refresh All and Refresh Management All commands are applied to all banks in all bank groups.
P151-012 §4.1 CA6 and CA7 are required to be valid (“V”)
P151-014 §4.1 The bank bits, BA0 and BA1 on CA6 and CA7, respectively, specify the bank within each bank group.
P151-015 §4.1 NOTE 5 The Precharge All command applies to all open banks in all bank groups.
P151-016 §4.1 NOTE 6 The Precharge Same Bank command applies to the same bank in all bank groups.
P151-017 §4.1 The bank bits specify the bank within each bank group.
P151-018 §4.1 NOTE 7 The Precharge command applies to a single bank as specified by bank address and bank group bits.
P151-023 §4.1 DRAM does not execute the command if it receives CS as LOW on 2nd cycle.
P151-024 §4.1 NOTE 12 WR command with WR_Partial (WR_P) = Low indicates a partial write command.
P151-025 §4.1 This is to help DRAM start an internal read for 'read modify write'.
P151-026 §4.1 NOTE 13 If CW=Low during the MRW command then DRAM should execute the command, Mode Register will be written.
P151-027 §4.1 If CW=HIGH then DRAM ignores the MRW command, and the Mode Register is not changed.
P151-028 §4.1 If CW=Low, DRAM should execute the MRR command.
P151-029 §4.1 If CW=High, DRAM may or may not execute MRR command.
P151-030 §4.1 NOTE 14 CID[3:0] bits are used for 3DS stacking support.
P151-031 §4.1 When CID[3:0] bits are not used, they are required to be Valid (“V”).
P151-032 §4.1 NOTE 15 If CA5:BL*=L, the command places the DRAM into the alternate Burst mode described by MR0[1:0] instead of the default Burst Length 16 mode.
P151-033 §4.1 NOTE 16 ODT=L is defined to allow On Die Termination (ODT) to persist when the device is in Power Down Mode.
P151-034 §4.1 NOTE 17 CID3/R17 is a multi-mode pin allowing for either 16H 3DS stacking with the CID3 bit usage or R17 for high bit density monolithic usage.
P151-035 §4.1 These usages are mutually exclusive.
P151-036 §4.1 NOTE 18 Write Pattern only supports BL16 and BL32.
P151-037 §4.1 NOTE 19 When CID3 is not used, its required to be Valid (“V”).
P151-038 §4.1 NOTE 20 In the case of a DRAM where the density or stacking doesn't require CA[13] the ball location for that function (considering the state of MIR) shall be connected to VDDQ, and the DRAM shall decode CA[13]=L so that the proper selection of die and RA is provided.
P151-040 §4.1 NOTE 22 When host issue MRR with CRC enabled, data comes out with CRC bit.
P151-041 §4.1 NOTE 23 If the Refresh Management Required bit is “0” (MR58 OP[0]=0), CA9 is only required to be valid (“V”) for a REF command, and the DRAM will treat a RFM command as a REF command.
P151-042 §4.1 If MR58 OP[0]=1, a REF command requires CA9=H.
P151-043 §4.1 NOTE 24 If the Refresh Interval Rate indicator bit is disabled (MR4:OP[3]=0) by the host, CA8 is only required to be valid (“V”).
P151-044 §4.1 If the Refresh Interval Rate indicator bit is enabled (MR4:OP[3]=1) by the host, the host is required to set CA8=H for REF commands issued at the 1x refresh interval rate and CA8=L for REF commands issued at the 2x refresh interval rate.
P151-045 §4.1 If the host issues 2x REF commands with CA8=L while MR4:OP[3]=1, but the Refresh rate requirement is 1x as determined internally by the DRAM, the DRAM may internally align to the 1x refresh rate.
P151-046 §4.1 NOTE 25 Command Truth Table bits such as BG2, BA1, R16, R17, C10, and CID[3:0] which are unused based on a device’s density, configuration width, and stacking options, the CA decode is defined as VALID when in these unused states.
P151-047 §4.1 NOTE 26 Unlike DES, NOP is considered a valid command, and timing from a preceding valid command must satisfy any associated command timings.
P152-001 §4.1.1 DDR5 DRAM commands ACT, WRP, WRPA and MRW are 2-cycle commands without associated ODT control requirements.
P152-002 §4.1.1 The DRAMwill not execute these2-cycle commands if the CS_n is LOW on the 2nd cycle (command cancel).
P153-001 §4.2 Accesses within a given burst is currently limited to only sequential, interleaved is not supported.
P153-002 §4.2 The ordering of accesses within a burst is determined by the burst length and the starting column address as shown in Table 35.
P153-003 §4.2 The burst length is defined by bits OP[1:0] of Mode Register MR0.
P153-004 §4.2 Burst length options include BC8 OTF, BL16, BL32 (optional) and BL32 OTF.
P153-020 §4.2 NOTE 1 T: Output driver for data and strobes are in RTT_PARK.
P153-023 §4.2.1 DDR5 DRAM supports an optional fixed BL32 mode and optional BL32 OTF (On the fly) mode for x4 devices only.
P154-006 §4.2.1 NOTE 1 In case of BL16 in BL32 OTF mode by setting MR0[OP1:0=11], the internal write operation starts eight cycles earlier than for BL32 mode.
P154-007 §4.2.1 This means that the starting point for tWR and tWTR shall be pulled in by eight clocks.
P154-008 §4.2.1 NOTE 2 T: Output driver for data and strobes are in high impedance.
P155-001 §4.3 The PRECHARGE command is used to deactivate the open row in a particular bank or the open row in all banks.
P155-002 §4.3 The bank(s) shall be available for a subsequent row activation a specified time (tRP) after the PRECHARGE command is issued.
P155-003 §4.3 Once a bank has been precharged, it is in the idle state and must be activated prior to any READ or WRITE commands being issued to that bank.
P155-004 §4.3 A PRECHARGE command is allowed if there is no open row in that bank (idle state) or if the previously open row is already in the process of precharging.
P155-005 §4.3 However, the precharge period shall be determined by the last PRECHARGE command issued to the bank.
P155-006 §4.3 If CA10 on the 2nd pulse of a Read or Write command is LOW, (shown as AP=L in the command truth table) then the auto-precharge function is engaged.
P155-007 §4.3 This feature allows the precharge operation to be partially or completely hidden during burst read cycles (dependent upon CAS latency) thus improving system performance for random data access.
P155-008 §4.3 The RAS lockout circuit internally delays the precharge operation until the array restore operation has been completed (tRAS satisfied) so that the auto precharge command may be issued with any read.
P155-009 §4.3 Auto-precharge is also implemented during Write commands.
P155-010 §4.3 The precharge operation engaged by the Auto precharge command shall not begin until the last data of the burst write sequence is properly stored in the memory array.
P155-011 §4.3 The bank shall be available for a subsequent row activation a specified time (tRP) after hidden PRECHARGE command (AutoPrecharge) is issued to that bank.
P155-012 §4.3 The precharge to precharge delay is defined by tPPD in the core timing tables.
P155-013 §4.3 tPPD applies to any combination of precharge commands (PREab, PREsb, PREpb).
P155-014 §4.3 tPPD also applies to any combination of precharge commands to a different die in a 3DS DDR5 SDRAM.
P155-015 §4.3.1 DDR5 supports three different types of precharge commands: Precharge, Precharge All and Precharge Same Bank The Precharge Command (PREpb) applies precharge to a specific bank defined by BA[1:0] {if applicable} in a specific bank group defined by BG[2:0], while a Precharge All (PREab) applies precharge to all banks in all bank groups and a Precharge Same Bank (PREsb) applies precharge to a specific bank defined by BA[1:0] in all bank groups.
P155-016 §4.3.1 In the case of a 3DS DDR5 SDRAM device, CID[3:0] shall also be selected to identify the target die.
P155-023 §4.3.1 NOTE See Command Truth Table for details
P156-001 §4.4 DDR5 shall support programmable preambles and postambles.
P156-002 §4.4.1 DDR5 supports a programmable read preamble and postamble.
P156-003 §4.4.1 Read Preamble is configured as 1tCK, 2tCK (two unique modes), 3tCK and 4tCK via MR8:OP[2:0].
P156-006 §4.4.1 Read Postamble is configured as 0.5tCK or 1.5tCK via MR8:OP[6]
P156-007 §4.4.1 NOTE: DQS shall have an option to drive early by x-tCK to accommodate different HOST receiver designs as controlled by the Read DQS Offset in MR40:OP[2:0].
P157-026 §4.4.1 Read DQS Offset MR40:OP[2:0] 3 tCK Offset Example
P157-027 §4.4.2 DDR5 supports a programmable write preamble and postamble.
P157-028 §4.4.2 Write Preamble is configured as 2tCK, 3tCK and 4tCK via MR8:OP[4:3] Write Postamble is configured as 0.5tCK or 1.5tCK via MR8:OP[7]
P158-001 §4.4.3 During Write operations, the input receiver strobe shall be aligned with the DQ according to the preamble and postamble settings, and the strobe shall meet the specified timing requirements to guarantee enough timing margin by setting the window for the strobe during the Write preamble and postamble time frame.
P158-002 §4.4.3 These timing requirements apply to all configurations of the Write preamble set by MR8 OP[4:3], which is 2tCK, 3tCK, and 4tCK Write preamble, for Write to Write operations as well as for normal Write operations.
P158-003 §4.4.3 These timing requirements apply to all configurations of the Write postamble set by MR8:OP[7], which is 0.5tCK and 1.5tCK WRITE postamble, for Write to Write operations as well as for normal Write operations.
P158-022 §4.4.3 tDQSH_pre and tDQSL_pre are shown, and apply to all toggles during the preamble.
P158-024 §4.4.3 2nd preamble during Write to Write operation shall follow the same requirement.
P159-013 §4.4.3 NOTE 1 The 2tCK Write Preamble is valid for the DDR5-3200/3600/4000/4400/4800 speed grades.
P159-014 §4.4.3 NOTE 2 The 3tCK Write Preamble is valid for the DDR5-4000/4400/4800/5200/5600/6000/6400 speed grades.
P159-015 §4.4.3 NOTE 3 The 4tCK Write Preamble is valid for the DDR5-5600/6000/6400/6800/7200/7600/8000/8400 speed grades.
P159-016 §4.4.3 NOTE 4 The 0.5tCK Write Postamble is valid for the DDR5-3200/3600/4000/4400/4800 speed grades.
P159-017 §4.4.3 NOTE 5 The 1.5tCK Write Postamble is valid for the DDR5-4000/4400/4800/5200/5600/6000/6400/6800/7200/7600/8000/8400 speed grades.
P159-018 §4.4.3 NOTE 6 The strobe preamble and postamble timing parameter values shall follow the operating frequency.
P160-013 §4.4.3 NOTE 1 The 2tCK Write Preamble is valid for the DDR5-3200/3600/4000/4400/4800 speed grades.
P160-014 §4.4.3 NOTE 2 The 3tCK Write Preamble is valid for the DDR5-4000/4400/4800/5200/5600/6000/6400 speed grades.
P160-015 §4.4.3 NOTE 3 The 4tCK Write Preamble is valid for the DDR5-5600/6000/6400/6800/7200/7600/8000/8400 speed grades.
P160-016 §4.4.3 NOTE 4 The 0.5tCK Write Postamble is valid for the DDR5-3200/3600/4000/4400/4800 speed grades.
P160-017 §4.4.3 NOTE 5 The 1.5tCK Write Postamble is valid for the DDR5-4000/4400/4800/5200/5600/6000/6400/6800/7200/7600/8000/8400 speed grades.
P160-018 §4.4.3 NOTE 6 The strobe preamble and postamble timing parameter values shall follow the operating frequency.
P161-001 §4.5 The DQS strobe for the device requires a preamble prior to the first latching edge (the rising edge of DQS_t with data valid), and it requires a postamble after the last latching edge.
P161-002 §4.5 The preamble and postamble options are set via Mode Register Write commands.
P161-003 §4.5 Additionally, the postamble and preamble configured size shall NOT force the HOST to add command gaps in the command interval just to satisfy postamble or preamble settings.
P161-004 §4.5 (i.e. Preamble=4tCK + Postamble=1.5tCK shall not force tCCD+5).
P161-005 §4.5.1 In Read to Read operations with tCCD=BL/2, postamble for 1st command and preamble for 2nd command shall disappear to create consecutive DQS latching edge for seamless burst operations.
P161-006 §4.5.1 In the case of Read to Read operations with command interval of tCCD+1, tCCD+2, etc., if the postamble and preambles overlap, the toggles take precedence over static preambles.
P161-012 §4.5.1 Pre = 1 shown Post = 0.5 shown
P163-001 §4.5.2 In Write to Write operations with tCCD=BL/2, postamble for 1st command and preamble for 2nd command shall disappear to create consecutive DQS latching edge for seamless burst operations.
P163-002 §4.5.2 In the case of Write to Write operations with command interval of tCCD+1, tCCD+2, etc., if the postamble and preambles overlap, the toggles take precedence over static preambles.
P163-008 §4.5.2 Pre = 2 shown Post = 0.5 shown
P163-020 §4.5.2 Postamble overlaps preamble by 3 4tCK preamble is TBD
P165-001 §4.6 The ACTIVATE command is used to open (or activate) a row in a particular bank for a subsequent access.
P165-002 §4.6 The value on the BG[2:0] in X4/8 and BG[1:0] in X16 select the bank group; BA[1:0] inputs selects the bank within the bankgroup, and the address provided on the appropriate CA pins for R[17:0] to select the row (see Table 40).
P165-003 §4.6 In the case of a 3DS DDR5 SDRAM device, the CID[3:0] shall also be selected to identify the correct die in the stack.
P165-004 §4.6 This row remains active (or open) for accesses until a precharge command is issued to that bank or a precharge all command is issued.
P165-005 §4.6 A bank must be precharged before opening a different row in the same bank.
P165-006 §4.6 Bank-to-bank command timing for ACTIVATE commands uses two different timing parameters, depending on whether the banks are in the same or different bank group.
P165-007 §4.6 tRRD_S (short) is used for timing between banks located in different bank groups.
P165-008 §4.6 tRRD_L (long) is used for timing between banks located in the same bank group.
P165-009 §4.6 Consecutive ACTIVATE commands, allowed to be issued at tRRDmin, are restricted to a maximum of four within the time period tFAW (four activate window).
P165-014 §4.6 NOTE See Command Truth Table for details
P165-015 §4.6.1 An ACT command with row address inputs which violate the 'MSB address bit “HIGH”, MSB-1 address bit “LOW”' non-binary density address restriction shall follow all timing and protocol rules as though the ACT were valid.
P165-016 §4.6.1 Any RD or RDA command following an ACT to the invalid address space within the same bank shall drive the DQS strobes with normaltiming but shall not output data on the DQs that can be used to learn about data stored in cells with valid addresses.
P165-017 §4.6.1 DQ data that coincidentally matches cell array data is permissible (for example: always sending the all 1s and cell data sometimes being all 1s).
P165-018 §4.6.1 Consistently exposing data from a previous read or previous activate is not permissible.
P165-019 §4.6.1 Any WR, WRA, WRP or WRPA command following an ACT to the invalid address space within the same bank shall not result in new data being written anywhere within the DRAM.
P165-020 §4.6.1 The DRAM will operate normally for Read and Write commands to banks which have pages open for valid rows.
P166-001 §4.7 The Read Operation causes the DRAM to retrieve and output data stored in its array.
P166-002 §4.7 The Read Operation is initiated by the Read Command during which the beginning column address and bank/group address for the data to be retrieved from the array is provided.
P166-003 §4.7 The data is driven by the DRAM on its DQ pins RL (CL) cycles after the Read Command along with the proper waveform on the DQS inputs.
P166-004 §4.7 Read Latency (RL or CL) is defined from the Read command to data and is not affected by the Read DQS offset timing (MR40 OP[2:0]).
P166-005 §4.7.1 During a READ or WRITE command, DDR5 shall support BC8, BL16, BL32 (optional) and BL32 OTF (optional) during the READ or WRITE.
P166-006 §4.7.1 MR0[1:0] is used to select burst operation mode.
P166-025 §4.7.1 2. DES commands are shown for ease of illustration; other commands may be valid at these times.
P166-026 §4.7.1 3. In this example, Read DQS Offset Timing is set to 0 Clocks.
P166-046 §4.7.1 2. DES commands are shown for ease of illustration; other commands may be valid at these times.
P166-047 §4.7.1 3. In this example, Read DQS Offset Timing is set to 0 Clocks
P167-004 §4.7.1 READ-0 DES DES DES DES DES DES DES DES DES DES DES DES DES DES DES DES DES DES DES DES DES DES DES DES DES DES CMD
P167-006 §4.7.1 Matched - Read DQS Offset Timing set to 0 Clock
P167-024 §4.7.1 Unmatched - Read DQS Offset Timing set to 1 Clock
P167-045 §4.7.1 2. DES commands are shown for ease of illustration; other commands may be valid at these times.
P167-046 §4.7.1 3. Two different examples are shown side by side, the top with the default setting for Read DQS Offset = 0 Clock, the lower with a 1 Clock setting.
P167-047 §4.7.1 In the lower case, the DQS is started 1 clock earlier than normal with respect to RL (CL).
P167-048 §4.7.1 4. In both cases, the Data does not move
P168-001 §4.7.2 The minimum external Read command to Precharge command spacing to the same bank is equal to tRTP with tRTP being the Internal Read Command to Precharge Command Delay.
P168-002 §4.7.2 Note that the minimum ACT to PRE timing, tRAS, must be satisfied as well.
P168-003 §4.7.2 The minimum value for the Internal Read Command to Precharge Command Delay is given by tRTP.min. A new bank active command may be issued to the same bank if the following two conditions are satisfied simultaneously:

P168-005 §4.7.2 2. The minimum RAS cycle time (tRC.MIN) from the previous bank activation has been satisfied.

P179-014 §4.8 The Write Operation stores data to the DRAM.
P179-015 §4.8 It is initiated by the Write command during which the beginning column address and bank/group address for the data to be written to the array is provided.
P179-016 §4.8 The data is provided to the DRAM on the DQ inputs CAS Write Latency (CWL) cycles after the Write command along with the proper waveform on the DQS inputs.
P179-017 §4.8 CAS Write Latency is defined and measured from final cycle of the Write command to the first effective rising DQS (excluding write preamble).
P179-018 §4.8.1 One write data mask (DM_n) pin for each byte data group is supported on x8 and x16 DDR5 SDRAMs.
P179-019 §4.8.1 The DM_n pin/function is enabled via mode register.
P179-020 §4.8.1 For the x4 configuration SDRAM, the DM mode register setting must be disabled.
P179-021 §4.8.1 The DM_n pin has identical timings and termination functionality on write operations as the DQ pins, as shown in Figure 43.
P179-022 §4.8.1 The DM_n pin is not used for read cycles and the pin should behave like a DQ pin driving high or be terminated to RTT_PARK.
P179-023 §4.8.1 When the DM function is disabled by MR, the DRAM disables the DM input and output receiver and does not expect nor drive any valid logic level.
P179-025 §4.8.1 The WR_Partial (WR_P) = Low as part of the write command must be used in conjunction with the DM_n data.
P179-026 §4.8.1 The WR_Partial (WR_P) = Low is to help DRAM start an internal read for 'read modify write' during masked writes.
P179-027 §4.8.1 If WR_Partial (WR_P) = High during write, then the mask data on DM_n must be high.
P179-028 §4.8.1 If DM is disabled, MR5 OP[5] = 0, WR_Partial (WR_P) must be “H”.
P179-029 §4.8.1 DM_n may be high or low.

P186-019 §4.8.7.1 Generally, if Write timing parameters are violated, a complete reset/initialization procedure has to be initiated to make sure that the DRAM works properly.
P186-020 §4.8.7.1 However, it is desirable, for certain violations as specified below, the DRAM is guaranteed to not “hang up” and that errors are limited to that particular operation.
P186-021 §4.8.7.1 For the following, it will be assumed that there are no timing violations with regards to the Write command itself (including ODT, etc.) and that it does satisfy all timing requirements not mentioned below.
P186-022 §4.8.7.2 Should the required data to strobe timing or voltage parameters be violated (Such as: tRx_RDQ_tMargin, tRx_DQS2DQ_Skew, VRx- _DQS, VRx_DQ, etc.), for any of the data/strobe timing edges or data/strobe voltage limits associated with a write burst data eye, then incorrect data might be written to the memory locations addressed with this WRITE command.
P186-023 §4.8.7.2 In the example, Figure X (add example timing diagram reference) the relevant strobe edges for a write burst are associated with the clock edges: Tn,Tn+0.5,Tn+1,...,Tn+8.5 Subsequent reads from that location might results in unpredictable read data, however the DRAM will work properly otherwise.
P187-001 §4.8.7.3 Should the strobe timing requirements (tWPRE, tWPST) or the strobe to clock timing requirements (tDQSS, tDQSoffset) be violated for any of the strobe edges associated with a Write burst, then wrong data might be written to the memory location addressed with the offending WRITE command.
P187-002 §4.8.7.3 Subsequent reads from that location might result in unpredictable read data, however the DRAM will work properly otherwise with the following constraints:
P187-003 §4.8.7.3 (1) Both Write CRC and data burst OTF are disabled; timing specifications other than tWPRE, tWPST, tDQSS, tDQSoffset are not violated.
P187-004 §4.8.7.3 (2) The offending write strobe (and preamble) arrive no earlier or later than six DQS transition edges from the Write-Latency position.
P187-005 §4.8.7.3 (3) A Read command following an offending Write command from any open bank is allowed.
P187-006 §4.8.7.3 (4) One or more subsequent WR or a subsequent WRA {to same bank as offending WR} may be issued tCCD_L later but incorrect data could be written; subsequent WR and WRA can be either offending or non-offending Writes.
P187-007 §4.8.7.3 Reads from these Writes may provide incorrect data.
P187-008 §4.8.7.3 (5) One or more subsequent WR or a subsequent WRA {to a different bank group} may be issued tCCD_S later but incorrect data could be written; subsequent WR and WRA can be either offending or non-offending Writes.
P187-009 §4.8.7.3 Reads from these Writes may provide incorrect data.
P187-010 §4.8.7.3 (6) Once one or more precharge commands (PREpb, PREsb, or PREab) are issued to DDR5 after offending WRITE command and all banks become precharged state (idle state), a subsequent, non-offending WR or WRA to any open bank shall be able to write correct data.
P187-011 §4.8.7.3 (7) DQS strobes including preamble must align to each Write commands data burst length configuration.
P187-012 §4.8.7.3 If the DRAM fails to capture or incorrectly de-serializes the incoming data stream because of misalignment or missing strobe edges, errors may occur.
P187-013 §4.8.7.3 These errors will propagate indefinitely until the DRAM is put into an idle state, i.e., all banks are in the precharged state with tRP satisfied.
P188-001 §4.8.8.1 The following specifies the relationship between the write enable timing window tWPRE_EN_ntck and the DRAM related DQS to CK drift window tDQSD as well as the system related DQS to CK drift window tDQSS around the final DQS to CK offset trained pass/fail point tDQS offset based on write leveling feedback in order to support n-tck pre-amble mode.
P188-002 §4.8.8.1 Functional operation requires that the following condition is met:
P189-009 §4.8.8.1 NOTE 1 Measured relative to the write leveling feedback, after write leveling training has been completed.
P189-010 §4.8.8.1 NOTE 2 Includes min DQS and CK timing terms TBD.
P189-011 §4.8.8.1 NOTE 3 When measuring the tDQSoffset, tWLS/H are reflected in the tDQSoffset result.
P189-012 §4.8.8.1 NOTE 4 DDR5-3200 timings apply for data rates <2933 MT/s.
P190-001 §4.9 The Self-Refresh command can be used to retain data in the DDR5 SDRAM, even if the rest of the system is powered down.
P190-003 §4.9 The DDR5 SDRAM device has a built-in timer to accommodate Self-Refresh operation.
P190-005 §4.9 The internal average periodic refresh interval adjustment (increasing, decreasing or staying constant) does not require any external control.
P190-006 §4.9 Self Refresh entry is command based (SRE), while the Self-Refresh Exit Command is defined by the transition of CS_n LOW to HIGH with a defined pulse width tCSH_SRexit, followed by three or more NOP commands (tCSL_SRexit) to ensure DRAM stability in recognizing the exit.
P190-007 §4.9 This is described below in more detail.
P190-008 §4.9 Before issuing the Self-Refresh-Entry command, the DDR5 SDRAM must be idle with all bank precharge state with tRP satisfied. ‘Idle state’ is defined as all banks are closed (tRP, etc. satisfied), no data bursts are in progress, and all timings from previous operations are satisfied (tMRD, tRFC, etc.).
P190-009 §4.9 A Deselect command must be registered on the last positive clock edge before issuing Self Refresh Entry command.
P190-010 §4.9 Once the Self Refresh Entry command is registered, Deselect commands must also be registered at the next positive clock edges until tCPDED is satisfied.
P190-011 §4.9 After tCPDED has been satisfied, CS_n must transition low.
P190-012 §4.9 After CS_n transitions low at the end of tCPDED, the CS_n shall stay low until exit.
P190-013 §4.9 The DDR5 SDRAM may switch to a CMOS based receiver to save more power and that transition should coincide with CS_n going low.
P190-014 §4.9 When the CS_n is held low, the DRAM automatically disables ODT termination and sets Hi-Z as termination state regardless RTT configuration for the duration of Self-Refresh mode.
P190-015 §4.9 Upon exiting Self-Refresh, DRAM automatically enables ODT termination and set RTT_PARK (for DQs) asynchronously during tXSDLL when RTT_PARK is enabled.
P190-016 §4.9 CA/CS/CK ODT shall revert to its strapped or its MR ODT Setting state if previously applied.
P190-017 §4.9 During normal operation (DLL on) the DLL is automatically disabled upon entering Self-Refresh and is automatically enabled (including a DLL-Reset) upon exiting Self-Refresh.
P190-018 §4.9 When the DDR5 SDRAM has entered Self-Refresh mode, all of the external control signals, except CS_n and RESET_n, are “don’t care.” For proper Self-Refresh operation, all power supply and reference pins (VDD, VDDQ, VSS, VSS and VPP) must be at valid levels.
P190-019 §4.9 DRAM internal VrefDQ and/or VrefCA generator circuitry may remain ON or turned OFF depending on DRAM design.
P190-020 §4.9 If DRAM internal VrefDQ and/or VrefCA circuitry is turned OFF in self refresh, when DRAM exits from self refresh state, it ensures that VrefDQ and/or VrefCA and generator circuitry is powered up and stable within tXS period.
P190-021 §4.9 First Write operation or first Write Leveling Activity may not occur earlier than tXS after exit from Self Refresh.
P190-023 §4.9 The clocks must stay on until tCKLCS but can be DON’T CARE after tCKLCS expires but it should be noted that shortly after tCPDED, the termination for the clocks will be off.
P190-024 §4.9 The clock is internally disabled (in the DRAM) during Self-Refresh Operation to save power.
P190-025 §4.9 The minimum time that the DDR5 SDRAM must remain in Self-Refresh mode is tCSL.
P190-026 §4.9 The user may change the external clock frequency or halt the external clock tCKLCS after Self-Refresh entry is registered, however, the clock must be restarted and stable tCKSRX before the device can exit Self-Refresh operation.
P190-027 §4.9 The procedure for exiting Self-Refresh requires a sequence of events.
P190-028 §4.9 Since the DRAM will switch to a CMOS based driver to save power, the DRAM will trigger Self-Refresh exit upon seeing the CS_n transition from low to high and stay high for tCSH_SRExit.
P190-029 §4.9 tCASRX prior to CS_n transitioning high, the CA bus must be driven high.
P190-030 §4.9 Once tCSH_SRExit is satisfied, three NOP commands must be issued, otherwise the DRAM could be put into an unknown state.
P190-031 §4.9 The clocks must be valid for tCKSRX prior to issuing the NOP commands that completes the Self Refresh exit sequence.
P190-032 §4.9 Once a Self-Refresh Exit is registered, the following timing delay must be satisfied:
P190-033 §4.9 1. Commands that do not require locked DLL: tXS - ACT, MPC, MRW, PDE, PDX, PRE(ab,sb,pb), REF(ab,sb), RFM(ab,sb), SRE, VREFCA & WRP
P190-034 §4.9 2. Commands that require locked DLL: tXS_DLL - RD, MRR & WR
P190-035 §4.9 Depending on the system environment and the amount of time spent in Self-Refresh, ZQ calibration commands may be required to compensate for the voltage and temperature drift as described in “ZQ Calibration Commands”.
P190-036 §4.9 To issue ZQ calibration commands, applicable timing requirements must be satisfied.
P191-002 §4.9 One additional refresh shall be issued in addition to refreshes normally scheduled.
P191-003 §4.9 This additional refresh is not counted toward the computation of the average refresh interval (tREFI).
P191-004 §4.9 If this refresh isn’t issued by tREFI after CS_n rises with tCSH_SRexit, then it counts toward the maximum number of refreshes which may be postponed.
P191-005 §4.9 This additional refresh does not replace the regularly periodic refresh that’s also scheduled at tREFI after CS_n rises with tCSH_SRexit (both of these refresh commands count toward the maximum number of refreshes which may be postponed).
P191-006 §4.9 The additional refresh consists of a single REFab command or n * REFsb, where n is the number of banks in a bank group.
P191-007 §4.9 If Self Refresh is to be re-entered and no regularly scheduled periodic refresh commands have been issued, the additional refresh shall be issued prior to Self Refresh re-entry.
P191-008 §4.9 Plus, FGR mode may require extra Refresh command(s), in addition the aforementioned additional Refresh, depending on the condition of the Self Refresh entry (refer to Clause 4.13.7 for more information)
P191-009 §4.9 The exit timing from self-refresh exit to first valid command not requiring a locked DLL is tXS.
P191-010 §4.9 The value of tXS is (tRFC).
P191-011 §4.9 This delay is to allow for any refreshes started by the DRAM to complete.
P191-012 §4.9 tRFC continues to grow with higher density devices so tXS will grow as well.
P191-032 §4.9 NOTES: 1 - While in 2N mode, tCSL_SRexit will not be statically held low (as shown above), as it will pulse for each 2 cycle period.
P191-033 §4.9 Refer to the 2N mode section for more details.
P191-034 §4.9 2 - Both tCSH_SRexit and tCSL_SRexit timings must be satisfied to guarantee DRAM operation.
P191-035 §4.9 3 - When tCSH_SRexit,min expires, the CA bus is allowed to transition from all bits High to any valid (V) level.
P191-036 §4.9 Prior to CS_n being registered Low at tc+1, the CA bus must transition to NOP conforming to the CAI state of the DRAM and complying with applicable DRAM input timing parameters.

P192-002 §4.9 Refer to the 2N mode section for more details.
