P193-001 §4.9.2 DDR5 DRAMs may contain an optional feature that disables refresh to selected segments in each bank when in self refresh.
P193-002 §4.9.2 The feature allows for lower self refresh power if portions of the DRAMs are not required to retain data.
P193-003 §4.9.2 Each bank is divided into six or eight segments based on the three highest row address bits supported by the DRAM device’s density.
P193-004 §4.9.2 Non-Binary density devices are divided into 6 segments as the 110 and 111 encodings of the PASR segment row bits are not used.
P193-005 §4.9.2 Binary densities are divided into 8 segments.
P193-006 §4.9.2 MR60 provides the segment mask for all banks, with one bit per segment.
P193-007 §4.9.2 A 0 (default) in the bit position provides normal refresh operation for the segment while a 1 masks that segment.
P193-008 §4.9.2 Masked segments are NOT refreshed during self refresh.
P193-010 §4.9.2 All segments are refreshed by refresh command when out of self refresh.
P193-011 §4.9.2 Segments which are masked are not guaranteed to retain their data if self refresh is entered.
P193-012 §4.9.2 ECS Transparency will not produce accurate results if any mask bit is set, but ECS scrubbing will still occur if enabled.
P193-013 §4.9.2 MR19 bit 7 is indicates whether the DRAM supports PASR.
P194-001 §4.10 DDR5’s Power-Down mode is new to the DDR family, as it no longer has a CKE pin to control entry and exit.
P194-004 §4.10 In PDE mode, it should be sampled on every edge.
P194-005 §4.10.1 Power-Down is entered when the command is registered.
P194-006 §4.10.1 Unlike Self Refresh Mode, CS_n will NOT be held low constantly while in Power-Down.
P194-007 §4.10.1 Timing diagrams are shown in Figure 60 with details for entry and exit of Power-Down.
P194-008 §4.10.1 The DLL should be in a locked state when Power-Down is entered for fastest Power-Down exit timing.
P194-009 §4.10.1 SDRAM design provides all AC and DC timing and voltage specification as well as proper DLL operation as long as DRAM controller complies with SDRAM specifications.
P194-010 §4.10.1 During Power-Down, if all banks are closed after any in-progress commands are completed, the device will be in Precharge Power-Down mode; if any bank is open after in-progress commands are completed, the device will be in active Power-Down mode.
P194-012 §4.10.1 If CA11=L during the PDE command, CA1 and CA4 will also be excluded, allowing the appropriate NT ODT command to be passed through and decoded by the non-target SDRAM while the target SDRAM remains in Power-Down (i.e. the SDRAM will monitor commands that utilize NT ODT via CA1 and CA4 and will not exit Power Down if a valid NT ODT command is registered).
P194-013 §4.10.1 If CA11=H during the PDE command, only the PDX command, qualified by CS_n, is legal during Power-Down.
P194-014 §4.10.1 If CA11=L during the PDE command, only NT ODT commands and PDX commands, qualified by CS_n, are legal during Power-Down.
P194-015 §4.10.1 Refer to the command truth table for more information.
P194-016 §4.10.1 MRR NT ODT commands during Power Down are not support with Burst on the fly (OTF) modes in MR0:OP[1:0].
P194-017 §4.10.1 When Power-Down is entered with ODT control enabled (CA11=L) the DRAM will continue to accept NT termination commands throughout the Power-Down process, including entry and exit.
P194-018 §4.10.1 Upon entry, during the tCPDED period, the DRAM will be switching from decoding all CA bus command bits to only decoding CA1 and CA4.
P194-019 §4.10.1 During this time all CA command bits must be valid when CS_n is asserted with the full Read or Write command, as the DRAM may still be decoding the full command.
P194-020 §4.10.1 Following tCPDED, only CA1 and CA4 need be valid as the DRAM will be ignoring the others.
P194-021 §4.10.1 Following the PDX command, all CA command bits must be valid for NT termination commands also, as the DRAM will be transitioning to decoding all bits.
P194-022 §4.10.1 It is only the time between tCPDED completion and tXP start that CA13:5, 3:2, and 0 need not be valid
P194-028 §4.10.1 CS used to trigger exit of PD
P194-047 §4.10.1 1. There is no specific PDX command.
P194-048 §4.10.1 In the case of systems with register using CAI, the encoding out of the register may be inverted from a NOP type command.
P194-049 §4.10.1 2. Diagram above is shown with a valid 2-cycle command after tXP for simplicity.
P194-050 §4.10.1 1-cycle valid commands are also legal.
P194-051 §4.10.1 3. CS_n shall be held HIGH, not toggled, during Power-Down, except Non-Target ODT command when PDE with CA11=L is asserted.
P195-001 §4.10.1 The DLL is kept enabled during Precharge Power-Down or Active Power-Down.
P195-002 §4.10.1 (If RESET_n goes low during Power-Down, the DRAM will be out of PD mode and into reset state).
P195-003 §4.10.1 Power-down duration is limited by tPD(max) of the device.
P195-017 §4.10.1 NOTE 1 Power-Down command can be sent while operations such as Activation, Precharge, Auto-Precharge or Refresh are in progress but IDD spec will not be applied until the operations are finished.
P195-018 §4.10.1 NOTE 2 WR is calculated from tWR by using the Rounding Algorithm.
P195-019 §4.10.1 Refer to the Rounding Definitions and Algorithm section for more information.
P195-020 §4.10.1 NOTE 3 WR in clock cycles as programmed in MR6.
P195-021 §4.10.1 NOTE 4 Read/Write/MRR can refer to both Target command and Non-Target command when CA11=H during PDE command.
P195-022 §4.10.1 NOTE 5 tMPC_delay is a valid timing parameter for all MPC commands except: a) Enter CS training Mode, Enter CA Training Mode, PDA Enumerate ID Program Mode because Power Down Entry is not supported for these MPC commands.
P195-023 §4.10.1 b) Apply VrefCA, VrefCS and RTT_CA/CS/CK because this MPC command requires waiting for VrefCA_time/ VREFCS_time.
P195-024 §4.10.1 NOTE 6 RBL: Read burst length associated with Read command a) RBL = 32 (36 w/ RCRC on) for fixed BL32 and BL32 in BL32 OTF mode b) RBL = 16 (18 w/ RCRC on) for fixed BL16 and BL16 in BL32 OTF mode c) RBL = 16 (18 w/ RCRC on) for BL16 in BC8 OTF mode and BC8 in BC8 OTF mode
P195-025 §4.10.1 NOTE 7 WBL: Write burst length associated with Write command a) WBL = 32 (36 w/ WCRC on) for fixed BL32 and BL32 in BL32 OTF mode b) WBL = 16 (18 w/ WCRC on) for fixed BL16 and BL16 in BL32 OTF mode c) WBL = 16 (18 w/ WCRC on) for BL16 in BC8 OTF mode and BC8 in BC8 OTF mode tPD(max) shall always be less than or equal to 5*tREFI1(max) during Normal Refresh Mode and less than or equal to
P195-026 §4.10.1 NOTE 8 9*tREFI2(max) during Fine Granularity Refresh Mode, and when using the rounding algorithms, nPD(max) shall always be less than or equal to 5*nREFI1(max) during Normal Refresh Mode and less than or equal to 9*nREFI2(max) during Fine Granularity Refresh Mode.
P196-006 §4.10.1 NOTE 1 MRR NT ODT commands during Power Down are not supported with Burst on the fly (OTF) modes in MR0:OP[1:0].
P196-007 §4.11 Once the DDR5 SDRAM is initialized, the DDR5 SDRAM requires the clock to be “stable” during almost all states of normal operation.
P196-008 §4.11 This means that, once the clock frequency has been set and is to be in the “stable state”, the clock period is not allowed to deviate except for what is allowed for by the clock jitter and SSC (spread spectrum clocking) specifications.
P196-009 §4.11 The input clock frequency can be changed from one stable clock rate to another stable clock rate under Self Refresh w/Frequency Change mode.
P196-010 §4.11 Outside Self-Refresh w/Frequency Change mode, it is illegal to change the clock frequency.
P196-011 §4.11 Prior to entering Self-Refresh w/ Frequency Change mode, the host must program tCCD_L/tCCD_L_WR/tCCD_L_WR2/tDLLK via MPC Command to update MR13 shadow register to the desired target frequency and configure VREFCA, VREFCS, RTT_CK, RTT_CS and RTT_CA (MR11, MR12, MR32 and MR33) shadow registers if needed.
P196-012 §4.11 Once the DDR5 SDRAM has been successfully placed into Self-Refresh w/Frequency Change mode and tCKLCS has been satisfied, the state of the clock becomes a don’t care.
P196-013 §4.11 Once a don’t care, changing the clock frequency is permissible, provided the new clock frequency is stable prior to tCKSRX.
P196-014 §4.11 During tCSL_FreqChg and prior to exiting Self-Refresh, the DRAM will automatically apply the changes to tCCD_L/tCCD_L_WR/tCCD_L_WR2/tDLLK, VREFCA, VREFCSRTT_CK, RTT_CS and RTT_CA.
P196-015 §4.11 When entering and exiting Self-Refresh mode for the sole purpose of changing the clock frequency, the Self-Refresh entry and exit specifications must still be met as outlined in Section 4.9 “Self-Refresh Operation”.
P196-016 §4.11 For the new clock frequency, Mode Registers may need to be configured (to program the appropriate CL, Preambles, Write Leveling Internal Cycle Alignment, DFE, DCA, etc.) prior to normal operation.
P196-017 §4.11 The DDR5 SDRAM input clock frequency is allowed to change only within the minimum and maximum operating frequency specified for the particular speed grade.
P196-018 §4.11.1 The following steps must be taken:
P196-019 §4.11.1 1. Prior to executing the SREF command, the shadow register of Mode Registers MR11/MR12/MR13/MR32/MR33 can be pre-loaded anytime if DRAM is in an IDLE state: 1a.
P196-020 §4.11.1 The host MUST program tCCD_L/tCCD_L_WR/tCCD_L_WR2/tDLLK via MR13:OP[3:0] to the desired target frequency.
P196-021 §4.11.1 During this stage, the values are stored in the MR13 shadow register and shall be applied to MR13 automatically when running SREF w/ CA9=”L” on the DRAM.
P196-022 §4.11.1 This change occurs during tCSL_FreqChg when exiting Self-Refresh with Frequency Change.
P196-024 §4.11.1 The host can configure the appropriate CS/CA/CK ODT settings via Mode Register (MR32 & MR33) if new values are needed for the new target frequency.
P196-025 §4.11.1 During this stage, the values are stored in the MR32 & MR33 shadow registers and shall be applied to MR32 & MR33 automatically when running SREF w/ CA9=”L” on the DRAM.
P196-026 §4.11.1 This change occurs during tCSL_FreqChg when exiting Self-Refresh w/Frequency Change.
P196-028 §4.11.1 The host can configure the VREFCA & VREF CS via the VREFCA or VREFCS command(s).
P196-029 §4.11.1 During this stage, the values are stored in the MR11 & MR12 shadow registers and shall be applied to MR11 & MR12 automatically when running SREF w/ CA9=”L” on the DRAM.
P196-030 §4.11.1 This change occurs during tCSL_FreqChg when exiting Self-Refresh w/ Frequency Change.
P196-032 §4.11.1 Entering and exiting Self-Refresh with the SREF command shall replace the values of MR11, MR12, MR13, MR32, and MR33 with their shadow register values.
P196-033 §4.11.1 2. Enter Self Refresh w/ Frequency Change by sending the SREF command (SRE w/ CA9=’L’).
P196-034 §4.11.1 3. After tCPDED(min), the host will transition CS_n low, indicating to the DRAM that the terminations are safe to turn off.
P197-001 §4.11.1 4. After tCKLCS, the host can turn the clocks off.
P197-003 §4.11.1 6. At this time, changing the clock frequency is permissible, provided the new clock frequency is stable prior to tCKSRX
P197-004 §4.11.1 7. Exiting Self-Refresh w/Frequency Change follows the same process as normal Self-Refresh exit.
P197-005 §4.11.1 8. After tXS, any additional mode registers requiring the DLL that are needed for the new frequency can be configured or other commands not requiring a DLL may be issued.
P197-007 §4.11.1 9. After tXS_DLL, normal operations resume and all commands are legal.
P197-011 §4.11.1 NOTE 1 See Command Truth Table for details
P197-059 §4.11.1 1. While in 2N mode, tCSL_SRexit will not be statically held low (as shown above), as it will pulse for each 2 cycle period.
P197-060 §4.11.1 Refer to the 2N mode section for more details.
P197-061 §4.11.1 2. Both tCSH_SRexit and tCSL_SRexit timings must be satisfied to guarantee DRAM operation.
P197-062 §4.11.1 3. Diagram above is shown with a valid 2-cycle command after tXS for simplicity.
P197-063 §4.11.1 1-cycle valid commands are also legal.
P197-064 §4.11.1 4. When tCSH_SRexit,min expires, the CA bus is allowed to transition from all bits High to any valid (V) level.
P197-065 §4.11.1 Prior to CS_n being registered Low at tc+1, the CA bus must transition to NOP conforming to the CAI state of the DRAM and complying with applicable DRAM input timing parameters.
P197-068 §4.11.1 NOTE 1 Since frequency can require VREFCA, VREFCS, and CA/CK/CS ODT Changes, the min time is longer than the traditional tCSL when the SRE command with CA9=L is used.
P198-001 §4.12 When Maximum Power Saving Mode is enabled by setting the MPSM enable (MR2:OP[3]) bit to ‘1’ using MRW command, the device enters Maximum Power Saving Mode Idle (MPSM Idle) state.
P198-002 §4.12 When Maximum Power Saving Mode for Device 15 is enabled by setting the Device 15 MPSM enable bit (MR2:OP[5]) to ‘1’ using MRW command, and the device’s PDA Enumerate ID (MR1 bits OP[3:0]) are equal to 15, the device enters Maximum Power Saving Mode Idle (MPSM Idle) state.
P198-003 §4.12 Setting the Device 15 MSPM enable bit to ‘1’ must be done after PDA device enumeration is complete.
P198-004 §4.12 Once the DRAM is placed into the MPSM Idle state, it can stay in that state indefinitely, or it can further enter either Maximum Power Saving Mode Power Down (MPSM Power Down) state or Maximum Power Saving Mode Self Refresh (MPSM Self Refresh) state.
P198-005 §4.12 Data retention is not guaranteed when DRAM is in any of MPSM states.
P198-006 §4.12 Mode register status and Soft PPR information is preserved.
P198-014 §4.12 Any command is allowed, but DRAM can ignore commands except MRW/SRE/PDE.
P198-026 §4.12 Data retention is not guaranteed
P198-032 §4.12.1 When DDR5 SDRAM is in this state, it ignores all types of commands except MRW, ODT, Power Down Entry (PDE) and Self Refresh Entry (SRE) commands.
P198-033 §4.12.1 MRW, ODT, PDE and SRE commands are executed normally.
P198-034 §4.12.1 DRAM shall not respond to any other command except these four command types.
P198-035 §4.12.1 DLL status is same as in normal idle state.
P198-036 §4.12.1 DRAM continues to drive CA ODT as programmed.
P198-037 §4.12.1 Normal command timing parameters are applied in this state, except that tREFI doesn’t need to be satisfied as Refresh command does not need to be issued in this state.
P199-001 §4.12.2 MPSM Power Down state is entered by Power Down Entry command from MPSM Idle state.
P199-002 §4.12.2 When DDR5 SDRAM is in this state, it responds to ODT command normally as it does in precharged power down state.
P199-003 §4.12.2 DLL status is same as in normal precharged power down state.
P199-004 §4.12.2 When the Power Down Exit command is issued, the DRAM goes back to the MPSM Idle state after tXP.
P199-005 §4.12.2 Normal Power Down command timings are applied in this state, except the tREFI requirement.
P199-006 §4.12.3 MPSM Deep Power Down (DPD) state is entered and exited by Self Refresh Entry and Exit commands from/to MPSM Idle state.
P199-007 §4.12.3 Input signal requirements to the DRAM in this state are same to those in the Self Refresh mode.
P199-008 §4.12.3 DRAM shall not execute any internal Refresh operation in this state.
P199-009 §4.12.3 When the Power Down Exit command is issued, the DRAM goes back to the MPSM Idle state after tXS.
P199-010 §4.12.3 tXS_DLL must be met prior to issuing any commands that require a locked DLL.
P199-011 §4.12.3 Normal Self Refresh command timings are applied in this state.
P199-012 §4.12.4 The device can exit from the MPSM Idle state by programming the MPSM enable (MR2:OP[1]) bit to ‘0’ using the MRW command.
P199-013 §4.12.4 MPSM exit to the first valid command delay is tMPSMX.
P200-001 §4.13 The Refresh command (REF) is used during normal operation of the DDR5 SDRAMs.
P200-002 §4.13 This command is non persistent, so it must be issued each time a refresh is required.
P200-003 §4.13 The DDR5 SDRAM requires Refresh cycles at an average periodic interval of tREFI.
P200-004 §4.13 There are three types of refresh operations supported by DDR5 SDRAMs.
P200-009 §4.13 For Normal Refresh and Fine Granularity Refresh operations, all banks of the SDRAM must be precharged and idle for a minimum of the precharge time tRP(min) before the All Bank Refresh command (REFab) can be issued.
P200-010 §4.13 The refresh addressing is generated by the internal refresh controller during the refresh cycle.
P200-011 §4.13 The external address bus is only required to be in a valid state once this cycle has started.
P200-012 §4.13 When the refresh cycle has completed, all banks of the SDRAM will be in the precharged (idle) state.
P200-013 §4.13 A delay between the Refresh command and the next valid command, except DES, PDE, NOP (PDX) and non-Target ODT commands, must be greater than or equal to the minimum Refresh cycle time tRFC(min) as shown in Figure 64 and Figure 65.
P200-014 §4.13 Note that the tRFC timing parameter depends on memory density and the refresh mode setting, which can be set to Normal Refresh mode or Fine Granularity Refresh (FGR) mode.
P200-031 §4.13 DRAM must be idle DRAM must be idle
P200-034 §4.13 Only DES, PDE, NOP (PDX) or non-Target ODT commands are allowed after Refresh command is issued until tRFC1(min) expires.
P200-035 §4.13 2. Time interval between two Refresh commands may be extended to a maximum of 5 x tREFI1.
P200-052 §4.13 DRAM must be idle DRAM must be idle
P200-055 §4.13 Only DES, PDE, NOP (PDX) or non-Target ODT commands are allowed after Refresh command is issued until tRFC2(min) expires.
P200-056 §4.13 2. Time interval between two Refresh commands may be extended to a maximum of 9 x tREFI2.
P201-001 §4.13.1 The DDR5 SDRAM has two different Refresh modes with two different refresh cycle time (tRFC) settings.
P201-002 §4.13.1 There is a Normal Refresh mode setting and a Fine Granularity Refresh (FGR) mode setting.
P201-003 §4.13.1 The FGR mode provides a shorter refresh cycle time (tRFC2) but also requires All Bank Refresh commands (REFab) to be provided twice as often (tREFI is divided by two, i.e., tREFI2 = tREFI1/2).
P201-004 §4.13.1 The Refresh mode setting is programed by MRW command as shown in Table 69.
P201-005 §4.13.1 The Refresh Modes are fixed until changed by MRW command to MR4 OP[4].
P201-006 §4.13.1 No on-the-fly Refresh mode change is supported.
P201-010 §4.13.2 If Refresh Mode is changed by MRW, the new tREFI and tRFC parameters would be applied from the moment of the rate change.
P201-011 §4.13.2 As shown in Figure 66, when an All Bank Refresh command is issued to the DRAM in Normal Refresh mode, then tRFC1 and tREFI1 are applied from the time that the command (REFab) was issued.
P201-012 §4.13.2 And when an All Bank Refresh command is issued in Fine Granularity Refresh (FGR) mode, then tRFC2 and tREFI2 should be satisfied.
P201-014 §4.13.2 NOTE: Refresh mode is Normal Refresh mode before the MRW and FGR mode after the MRW
P201-015 §4.13.2 The following conditions must be satisfied before the Refresh mode can be changed.
P201-016 §4.13.2 Otherwise, data retention of DDR5 SDRAM cannot be guaranteed.
P201-017 §4.13.2 1. In the Normal Refresh mode, the REFab command must complete and tRFC1 must be satisfied before issuing the MRW command to change the Refresh Mode.
P201-018 §4.13.2 2. If performing REFab commands in the Fine Granularity Refresh mode, it is recommended that an even number of REFab commands are issued to the DDR5 SDRAM since the last change of the Refresh mode with an MRW command before the Refresh mode is changed again by another MRW command.
P201-019 §4.13.2 If this condition is met, no additional Refresh commands are required upon the Refresh mode change.
P201-020 §4.13.2 If this condition is not met, one extra REFab command is required to be issued to the DDR5 SDRAM upon Refresh mode change.
P201-021 §4.13.2 This extra Refresh command is not counted toward the computation of the average refresh interval (tREFI1).
P201-022 §4.13.2 This extra Refresh counts toward the maximum number of refreshes which may be postponed.
P201-024 §4.13.2 3. If performing REFsb commands, it is recommended that all banks have received an even number of REFsb command since the last change of the Refresh mode with an MRW command before the Refresh mode is changed again by another MRW command, since a REFab command will reset the internal bank counter.
P201-025 §4.13.2 If this condition is met, no additional refresh commands are required upon the Refresh mode change.
P201-026 §4.13.2 If this condition is not met, one extra REFab command is required to be issued to the DDR5 SDRAM upon Refresh mode change.
P201-027 §4.13.2 These extra Refresh commands are not counted toward the computation of the average refresh interval (tREFI1).
P201-028 §4.13.2 These extra Refresh commands count toward the maximum number of refreshes which may be postponed.
P201-029 §4.13.2 See Figure 68 for 16Gb and higher density DRAM with 4 banks in a bank group example.
P202-020 §4.13.2 FGR 2x, even REFab count, to Normal 1x Refresh Mode (recommended)
P203-016 §4.13.2 FGR 2x, all banks received REFsb and even REF count, to Normal 1x Refresh Mode (recommended)
P204-001 §4.13.3 Same Bank Refresh command (REFsb) allows the DDR5 DRAM to apply the refresh process to a specific bank in each bank group unlike the All Bank Refresh command (REFab) which applies the refresh process to all banks in every bank group.
P204-002 §4.13.3 The determination whether a Same Bank Refresh or an All Bank Refresh is executed by the DRAM depends on whether REFsb or REFab command is issued, as shown in the command truth table.
P204-003 §4.13.3 The REFsb command is only allowed in FGR mode (MR4[OP4]=1).
P204-004 §4.13.3 The first Same Bank Refresh command (REFsb) to each bank increments an internal bank counter and once the bank counter equals the number of available banks in a bank group, it will reset and start over on the next subsequent REFsb.
P204-005 §4.13.3 Each time the internal bank counter resets and starts over on the next subsequent REFsb, the global refresh counter will also increment.
P204-006 §4.13.3 A REFsb command can be issued to any bank and in any bank order.
P204-007 §4.13.3 A subsequent REFsb command issued to the same bank prior to every bank receiving a REFsb command will repeat refreshing the same row since the SDRAM’s global refresh counter will not increment until all banks in a bank group receive a REFsb command.
P204-008 §4.13.3 The first REFsb command issued is the “Synchronization” REFsb command and the “Synchronization” count resets the internal bank counter to zero when either (a) every bank has received one REFsb command, (b) RESET is applied, (c) entering/exiting self refresh mode, or (d) REFab is issued.
P204-009 §4.13.3 The DRAM’s global refresh counter increments when either a REFab is issued or when all banks have received their one REFsb command and the “Synchronization” count reset to zero.
P204-010 §4.13.3 If a REFab command is issued when the bank counter is not zero, i.e. in the middle of same-bank refreshing, the SDRAM’s global refresh counter will not increment until the completion of REFab, effectively losing the credits for any REFsb commands issued prior to the REFab.
P204-011 §4.13.3 See the 16 Gb and Higher Density DRAM Bank and Refresh counter increment behavior table for details.
P205-001 §4.13.3 The REFsb command must not be issued to the device until the following conditions are met: -tRFC1 or tRFC2 has been satisfied after the prior 1x or 2x REFab command(s), respectively -tRFCsb has been satisfied after the prior REFsb command -tRP has been satisfied after the prior PRECHARGE command to that bank -tRRD_L has been satisfied after the prior ACTIVATE command (e.g. tRRD_L has to be met from ACTIVATE of a different bank in the same bank group to the REFsb targeted at the same bank group)
P205-002 §4.13.3 Additional restrictions for issuing the REFsb command: -tFAW has not been met (each REFsb counts as an ACTIVATE command for the four activate window timing restriction)
P205-003 §4.13.3 Once a REFsb is issued, the target banks (one in each Bank Group) are inaccessible during the same-bank refresh cycle time (tRFCsb); however, the other banks in each bank group are accessible and can be addressed during this same-bank refresh cycle.
P205-004 §4.13.3 When the same-bank refresh cycle has completed, the banks refreshed via the REFsb will be in idle state.
P205-005 §4.13.3 After issuing REFsb command, the following conditions must be met: -tRFCsb must be satisfied before issuing a REFab command -tRFCsb must be satisfied before issuing an ACTIVATE command to the same bank -tREFSBRD must be satisfied before issuing an ACTIVATE command to a different bank.
P205-017 §4.13.3 NOTE 1 MR4(OP[4]) set to Normal Refresh mode.
P205-018 §4.13.3 NOTE 2 MR4(OP[4]) set to FGR mode.
P205-019 §4.13.3 REFsb command is valid only in FGR mode.
P205-020 §4.13.3 Where n is the number of banks in a bank group, a single REFab command can be replaced with n REFsb commands for the purpose of scheduling postponed refresh commands.
P205-022 §4.13.4 When the refresh mode is set to Normal Refresh mode, REFab commands are issued (tRFC1), and Tcase<=85°C, the maximum average refresh interval (tREFI1) is tREFI.
P205-023 §4.13.4 When the refresh mode is set to FGR mode, REFab commands are issued (tRFC2) and Tcase<=85 °C, the maximum average refresh interval (tREFI2) is tREFI/2.
P205-024 §4.13.4 This same tREFI/2 interval value is also appropriate if the refresh mode is set to Normal Refresh mode and REFab commands are issued (tRFC1) but 85 °C<Tcase<=95 °C.
P205-025 §4.13.4 Finally, if the refresh mode is set to FGR mode, REFab commands are issued (tRFC2), and 85 °C<Tcase<=95 °C, the maximum average refresh interval (tREFI2) is tREFI/4.
P205-026 §4.13.4 The DDR5 SDRAM includes an optional method for the host to indicate when Refresh commands are being issued at the 2x (tREFI2) refresh interval rate.
P205-027 §4.13.4 The 2x Refresh Interval Rate indicator (MR4:OP[3]) alerts the DRAM if the host supports the refresh interval rate indication as part of the REF command using CA8.
P205-028 §4.13.4 If enabled (MR4:OP[3]=1), the host will issue 1x REF commands with CA8=H (Tcase<=85 °C), and the host will issue 2x REF commands with CA8=L (Tcase any allowable temperature).
P205-029 §4.13.4 MR4:OP[3] is a Status Read/Write “SR/W” MR bit which shows DDR5 SDRAM support of this optional feature.
P205-030 §4.13.4 Reading MR4:OP[3] will return a “1” if the 2x Refresh Interval Rate indicator is supported.
P205-031 §4.13.4 A “0” will be returned if not supported.
P205-032 §4.13.4 tREFI is based on the 8,192 refresh commands that need to be issued within the baseline tREF=32 ms refresh period on the DDR5 SDRAM.
P206-001 §4.13.5 Typical platforms are designed with the assumption that no more than one physical rank is refreshed at the same time.
P206-002 §4.13.5 In order to limit the maximum refresh current (IDD5B1) for a 3D stacked SDRAM, it will be required to stagger the refresh commands to each logical rank in a stack.
P206-003 §4.13.5 The tRFC time for a single logical rank is defined as tRFC_slr and is specified as the same value as for a monolithic DDR5 SDRAM of equivalent density.
P206-004 §4.13.5 The minimum amount of stagger between refresh commands sent to different logical ranks (tRFC_dlr) or physical ranks (tRFC_dpr) is specified to be approximately tRFC_slr/3 - Table 74.
P206-012 §4.13.5 NOTE 1 All 3D Stacked (3DS) devices follow the same requirements as the monolith die regardless of logical rank.
P206-014 §4.13.5 Future densities such as 24Gb or 32Gb could require different tREFI requirements.
P206-015 §4.13.5 NOTE 3 n is the number of banks in a bank group (e.g. 8G: n=2; 16G: n=4).
P206-029 §4.13.5 NOTE 1 All 3D Stacked (3DS) devices follow the same requirements as the monolith die for same logical ranks
P206-030 §4.13.5 NOTE 2 Parameter applies to dual-physical-rank (36 and 40 placement) 3DS-based DIMMs built with JESD301-1 “PMIC 50x0 Specification”, but may not apply to DIMMs built with higher current capacity PMICs.
P206-031 §4.13.5 NOTE 3 3DS tRFC parameters are to be rounded up to the nearest 1ns after the “tRFC*min”/3 calculation.
P207-006 §4.13.6 In general, a Refresh command needs to be issued to the DDR5 SDRAM regularly every tREFI interval.
P207-007 §4.13.6 To allow for improved efficiency in scheduling and switching between tasks, some flexibility in the absolute refresh interval is provided.
P207-008 §4.13.6 In Normal Refresh mode, a maximum of 4 REFab commands can be postponed, meaning that at no point in time more than a total of 4 Refresh commands are allowed to be postponed.
P207-009 §4.13.6 In case that 4 REFab commands are postponed in a row, the resulting maximum interval between the surrounding REFab commands is limited to 5 × tREFI1 (see Figure 69).
P207-010 §4.13.6 At any given time, a maximum of 5 REFab commands can be issued within 1 x tREFI1 window.
P207-011 §4.13.6 Self-refresh mode may be entered with a maximum of 4 REFab commands being postponed.
P207-012 §4.13.6 After exiting Self-Refresh mode with one or more REFab commands postponed, additional REFab commands may be postponed to the extent that the total number of postponed REFab commands (before and after the Self-Refresh) will never exceed 4.
P207-013 §4.13.6 During Self-Refresh Mode, the number of postponed REFab commands does not change.
P207-014 §4.13.6 An additional REFab command is required after Self-Refresh exit (refer to Clause 4.9 for more information).
P207-015 §4.13.6 In FGR Mode, the maximum REFab commands that may be postponed is 8, with the resulting maximum interval between the surrounding REFab commands limited to 9 x tREFI2 (see Figure 70).
P207-016 §4.13.6 At any given time, a maximum of 9 REFab commands can be issued within 1 x tREFI2 window.
P207-017 §4.13.6 The same maximum count of 8 applies to postponed REFab commands around self-refresh entry and exit.
P207-018 §4.13.6 An additional REFab command is required after Self-Refresh exit (refer to Clause 4.9 for more information).
P207-019 §4.13.6 Where n is the number of banks in a bank group, a single REFab command can be replaced with n REFsb commands for the purpose of scheduling postponed refresh commands.
P208-006 §4.13.7 DDR5 SDRAM can enter Self Refresh mode anytime in Normal Refresh and FGR mode without any restriction on the number of Refresh commands that have been issued during the mode before the Self Refresh entry.
P208-007 §4.13.7 However, upon Self Refresh exit, one additional Refresh must be issued in addition to the normally scheduled Refresh commands (refer to Clause 4.9 for more information).
P208-008 §4.13.7 Plus, extra Refresh command(s), in addition to the aforementioned additional Refresh, may also be required depending on the condition of the Self Refresh entry.
P208-009 §4.13.7 The conditions and requirements for the extra Refresh command(s) are defined as follows:
P208-010 §4.13.7 1. There are no special restrictions for the Normal Refresh mode.
P208-011 §4.13.7 2. If performing REFab commands in FGR mode, it is recommended that there should be an even number of REFab commands before entry into Self Refresh since the last Self Refresh exit or MRW command that set the FGR mode.
P208-012 §4.13.7 If this condition is met, no additional refresh commands are required upon Self Refresh exit.
P208-013 §4.13.7 If this condition is not met, one extra REFab command is required to be issued to the DDR5 SDRAM upon Self Refresh exit.
P208-014 §4.13.7 This extra Refresh command is not counted toward the computation of the average refresh interval (tREFI2).
P208-015 §4.13.7 This extra Refresh counts toward the maximum number of refreshes which may be postponed.
P208-016 §4.13.7 Refer to Clause 4.9 for more information.
P208-018 §4.13.7 3. If performing REFsb commands, it is recommended that all banks have received a REFsb command prior to entering Self Refresh, since entering and exiting Self Refresh will reset the internal bank counter.
P208-019 §4.13.7 If this condition is met, no additional refresh commands are required upon Self Refresh exit, and REFsb commands again can be issued to any bank in any bank order.
P208-020 §4.13.7 If this condition is not met, one extra REFab command or an extra REFsb command to each bank is required to be issued to the DDR5 SDRAM upon Self Refresh exit.
P208-021 §4.13.7 These extra Refresh commands are not counted toward the computation of the average refresh interval (16Gb and higher density DRAM with 4 banks per bank group example: tREFI2 - see Figure 72).
P208-022 §4.13.7 These extra Refresh commands count toward the maximum number of refreshes which may be postponed.
P208-023 §4.13.7 Refer to Clause 4.9 for more information.
P209-024 §4.13.7 FGR 2x, even REF count, to Self Refresh (recommended)
P210-026 §4.13.7 FGR 2x all banks received REFsb and even REF count, to Self Refresh (recommended)
P211-001 §4.14 DDR5 devices feature a temperature sensor whose status (MR4:OP[2:0]) can be read to determine the minimum required refresh rate.
P211-002 §4.14 Either the temperature sensor readout or the device TOPER may be used by the system to determine whether the refresh rate and operating temperature requirements are being met.
P211-003 §4.14 Additionally, the device’s temperature status (MR4:OP[2:0]) can be read by the system to determine the current approximate temperature range sensed by the device.
P211-004 §4.14 DDR5 devices shall monitor device temperature and update MR4 according to tTSI.
P211-005 §4.14 Upon completion of device initialization, the device temperature status bits shall be no older than tTSI.
P211-006 §4.14 MR4 will be updated even when device is in Self Refresh state.
P211-007 §4.14 When using the temperature sensor, the actual device case temperature may be higher than the TOPER specification that applies for the standard or elevated temperature ranges.
P211-008 §4.14 For example, TCASE may be above 85 °C when MR4:OP[2:0]=010B.
P211-009 §4.14 DDR5 devices shall allow for 2 °C temperature margin between the point at which the device updates the MR4 value and the point at which the controller reconfigures the system accordingly.
P211-010 §4.14 When MR4:OP[5]=0 (wide range temperature sensor not supported), the four thresholds of the temperature sensor will be nominally 80 °C, 85 °C, 90 °C and 95 °C.
P211-011 §4.14 The 2nd threshold (nominally 85°C) is used by the system to switch to 2x refresh.
P211-012 §4.14 The 4th threshold (nominally 95 °C) is used by the system to throttle activity to keep the DRAM at a safe operating temperature.
P211-013 §4.14 The 1st threshold (nominally 80 °C) allows the system to take actions which delay reaching the 2nd threshold.
P211-014 §4.14 The 3rd threshold (nominally 90 °C) allows the system to take actions which delay reaching the 4th threshold.
P211-015 §4.14 When MR4:OP[5]=1 (optional wide range temperature sensor range supported), the six thresholds of the temperature sensor will be nominally 75 °C, 80 °C, 85 °C, 90 °C, 95 °C, and 100 °C.
P211-016 §4.14 The 3rd threshold (nominally 85 °C) is used by the system to switch to 2x refresh.
P211-017 §4.14 The 5th threshold (nominally 95 °C) is used by the system to throttle activity to keep the DRAM at a safe operating temperature.
P211-018 §4.14 The 6th threshold (nominally 100 °C) is used by the system to perform emergency actions such as forcing the system to power down.
P211-019 §4.14 Support for temperature sensor readings above 95 °C does not imply the DDR5 DRAM device may operate properly above 95 °C - side effects may include loss of data integrity.
P211-020 §4.14 To ensure proper operation using the temperature sensor, applications should consider the following factors:
P211-021 §4.14 TempGradient is the maximum temperature gradient experienced by the memory device at the temperature of interest over a range of 2 °C, measured in the range of interest 80-100 °C.
P211-022 §4.14 ReadInterval is the time period between MR4 reads from the system.
P211-023 §4.14 TempSensorInterval (tTSI) is the maximum delay between internal updates of MR4.
P211-024 §4.14 SysRespDelay is the maximum time between a read of MR4 and the response by the system.
P211-025 §4.14 In order to determine the required frequency of polling MR4, the system shall use the maximum TempGradient and the maximum response time of the system using the following equation:
P211-038 §4.14 NOTE 1 Verified by design and characterization, and may not be subject to production test.
P211-039 §4.14 NOTE 2 Only the minimum (negative side) is specified for the second and fourth thresholds.
P211-040 §4.14 The DRAM vendor is responsible for guaranteeing correct operation of 1x refresh for MR4 <= 010b, and correct operation of 2x refresh for MR4 <= 100b.
P211-042 §4.14 NOTE 3 The 1st threshold is defined relative to the 2nd threshold.
P211-043 §4.14 NOTE 4 The 3rd threshold is defined relative to the 2nd and 4th thresholds.
P212-001 §4.14 For example, if TempGradient is 10 °C/s and the SysRespDelay is 100 ms: (10 °C/s) x (ReadInterval + 32 ms + 100 ms) ≤ 2 °C In this case, ReadInterval shall be no greater than 68 ms.
P212-004 §4.14 NOTE: MR4 encodings in Figure 73 are examples only and assume that MR4:OP[4]=0
P212-005 §4.14.1 In the case of 3D Stacked devices, the Refresh Rate (MR4) is related to the hottest die in the stack only.
P212-006 §4.14.2 The DDR5 DRAM provides temperature related information to the controller via an encoding on MR4:OP[2:0].
P212-007 §4.14.2 The encodings define the proper refresh rate expected by the DRAM to maintain data integrity.
P213-009 §4.14.3 NOTE 1 The refresh rate for each OP[2:0] setting applies to tREFI1 and tREFI2.
P213-010 §4.14.3 Each OP[2:0] setting specifies a nominal temperature range.
P213-011 §4.14.3 The five ranges defined by OP[2:0] are determined by four temperature thresholds.
P213-012 §4.14.3 NOTE 2 The four temperature thresholds are nominally at 80 °C, 85 °C, 90 °C, and 95 °C.
P213-013 §4.14.3 The 85 °C and 95 °C thresholds have a specified minimum temperature value, but the maximum temperature value is not specified.
P213-014 §4.14.3 NOTE 3 DRAM vendors must report all of the possible settings over the operating temperature range of the device.
P213-015 §4.14.3 Each vendor guarantees that their device will work at any temperature within the range using the refresh interval requested by their device.
P213-016 §4.14.3 NOTE 4 The 2x Refresh Rate must be provided by the system before the DRAM Tj has gone up by more than 2 °C (Temperature Margin) since the first report out of OP[2:0]=011B.
P213-017 §4.14.3 This condition is reset when OP[2:0] is equal to 010B.
P213-018 §4.14.3 NOTE 5 The device may not operate properly when OP[2:0]=101B, if the DRAM Tj has gone up by more than 2°C (Temperature Margin) since the first report out of OP[2:0]=101B.
P213-019 §4.14.3 This condition is reset when OP[2:0] is equal to 100B.
P213-020 §4.14.3 OP[2:0]=101B must be a temporary condition of the DRAM, to be addressed by immediately reducing the Tj of the DRAM by throttling its power, and/or the power of nearby devices.
P213-022 §4.14.3 OP[2:0] bits are valid after initialization sequence (Te).
P213-023 §4.14.3 NOTE 7 See the section on “Temperature Sensor” for information on the recommended frequency of reading MR4
P214-002 §4.15.1 The MPC command is initiated with CS_n, and CA[4:0] asserted to the proper state at the rising edge of CK, as defined by the Command Truth Table.
P214-003 §4.15.1 The MPC command has eight operands (OP[7:0]) that are decoded to execute specific commands in the SDRAM.
P214-004 §4.15.1 The MPC command uses an encoding that includes the command encoding and the opcode payload in a single clock cycle.
P214-005 §4.15.1 This enables the host to extend the setup and hold for the CA signals beyond the single cycle when the chip select asserts.
P214-006 §4.15.1 In addition, the MPC command will support multiple cycles of CS_n assertion.
P214-007 §4.15.1 The multiple cycles of CS_n assertion ensures the DRAM will capture the MPC command during at least one rising CK_t/CK_c edge.
P214-011 §4.15.1 NOTE See Command Truth Table for details
P215-001 §4.15.2 Table 81 specifies the opcode assignments for the MPC operations:
P215-004 §4.15.2 NOTE 1 See command truth table for more information.
P215-005 §4.15.2 NOTE 2 Refer to the CS Training Mode section for more information regarding CS Training Mode Entry and Exit.
P215-006 §4.15.2 NOTE 3 Refer to the CA Training Mode section for more information regarding CA Training Mode Entry.
P215-007 §4.15.2 NOTE 4 Refer to the ZQ Calibration section for more information regarding ZQCal Start and ZQCal Latch.
P215-008 §4.15.2 NOTE 5 Refer to the DQS Interval Oscillator section for more information regarding Start DQS Interval Oscillator and Stop DQS Interval Oscillator
P215-009 §4.15.2 NOTE 6 Refer to the Per DRAM Addressability section for more information regarding Enter PDA Mode and Exit PDA Mode.
P215-010 §4.15.2 NOTE 7 Refer to the CA_ODT Strap Operation section for more information regarding Group A and Group B configurations for RTT_CA, RTT_CS, and RTT_CK.
P215-011 §4.15.2 NOTE 8 “Apply VrefCA, VrefCS and RTT_CA/CS/CK” applies the settings previously sent with the VrefCA or VrefCS command and “MPC Set RTT_CA/CS/CK”.
P215-012 §4.15.2 Until this “MPC Apply VrefCA, VrefCS and RTT_CA/CS/CK” command is sent, the values are in a shadow register.
P215-013 §4.15.2 Any MRR to the VrefCA, VrefCS and RTT_CA/CS/CK settings should return only the applied value.
P215-014 §4.15.2 The shadow register shall retain the previously set value, so that any time the “MPC Apply VrefCA, VrefCS and RTT_CA/CS/CK” command is sent, there is no change in the applied value unless a new VrefCA , VrefCS or RTT_CA/CS/CK value was sent to the shadow register since the previous “MPC Apply VrefCA, VrefCS and RTT_CA/CS/CK” command.
P215-015 §4.15.2 NOTE 9 The PDA Enumerate ID and PDA Select ID opcodes include a 4-bit value, designated by xxxxB in the table.
P215-016 §4.15.2 This is the value that is programmed into the MR for these fields.
P215-017 §4.15.2 The PDA Enumerate ID can only be changed while in PDA Enumerate Programming Mode.
P215-018 §4.15.2 NOTE 10 For any MPC command that is associated with a Mode Register, the only way to program that Mode Register is via the MPC command.
P215-019 §4.15.2 Those Mode Registers will be read only and will not support MRW.
P215-020 §4.15.2 NOTE 11 MPC command “tDLLK/tCCD_L” sets the settings to the MR13 shadow registers and applies the settings to MR13 when the DRAM encounters MPC command “DLL RESET” or SRE with CA9=”L”.
P217-001 §4.15.3 As shown in Figure 74 and Figure 75, the MPC CMD timings are extended when Multi-Cycle CS assertion mode (MR2:OP[4]) is set to zero.
P217-002 §4.15.3 The CS_n is also asserted for multiple consecutive cycles, limited by tMPC_CS.
P217-003 §4.15.3 All timings will be relative to the final rising CK_t/CK_c within the CS_n assertion window.
P217-004 §4.15.3 The min delay from when the MPC command is captured to the next valid command is specified as tMPC_Delay.
P217-005 §4.15.3 Prior to VrefCS, VrefCA, CS and CA Training completing successfully, the CA will be driven with additional setup and hold beyond the CS_n assertion.
P217-006 §4.15.3 For the DRAM to latch the MPC command in cases where the alignment between CS_n, CA, and CK may be unknown, the CA inputs shall reach the proper command state and provide at least three cycles of setup prior to CS_n transitioning from high to low, CS_n shall remain low for tMPC_CS, and CA shall remain in the proper command state for and provide at least three cycles of hold after CS_n transitions from low to high.
P217-007 §4.15.3 This additional setup and hold is only needed when MR2:OP[4] is set to 0 (Multi-Cycle CS assertion duration mode).
P217-026 §4.15.3 The DDR5 DRAM will support a MR setting to indicate when a Multi-Cycle CS_n assertion may be used for the MPC, VrefCA and VrefCS commands.
P217-027 §4.15.3 MR2:OP[4] defaults to “0” upon a reset.
P217-028 §4.15.3 Once MR2:OP[4] =1 (single-cycle CS_n assertion) has been set, the host is required to change MR2:OP[4]=0 if any multi-cyle CS_n timings are used.
P217-048 §4.15.3 Default value for the CS Assertion Duration is 0, which allows for Multi-Cycle CS assertions during training.
P218-006 §4.15.3 NOTE 1 The minimum tMPC_CS constraint only applies when the CS Assertion Duration setting is 0.
P218-007 §4.15.3 The CS Assertion Duration MR setting shall be set (MR2:OP[4]=1B) to enable single cycle MPC commands.
P218-008 §4.15.3 The earliest time to set the CS Assertion Duration MR (MR2:OP[4]=1B) is after VrefCS, VrefCA, CS and CA training are complete, when MRW commands can be sent to the DRAM.
P218-009 §4.15.3 NOTE 2 This applies only to Multi-Cycle MPC commands when MR2:OP[4]=0B
P218-030 §4.15.3 NOTE 1 For 3DS, MPC commands for All Banks Idle cases require all banks to be idle on all logical ranks.
P218-036 §4.15.3 NOTE 1 RD/WR can refer to both target ODT RD/WR and non-target ODT RD/WR
P218-037 §4.15.3 NOTE 2 Selected MPC functions require all banks to be idle.
P218-038 §4.15.3 This includes all banks within 3DS logical ranks.
P218-039 §4.15.3 NOTE 3 Non-target rank commands are required to meet the minimum ODT requirements for DQ/DQS
P219-001 §4.16 DDR5 allows programmability of a given device on a rank.
P219-002 §4.16 As an example, this feature can be used to program different ODT or Vref values on DRAM devices on a given rank.
P219-003 §4.16 The Per DRAM Addressability (PDA) applies to MRW, MPC, VrefCA, and VrefCS commands.
P219-004 §4.16 Some per DRAM settings will be required prior to any training of the CA and CS timings and the DQ write timings.
P219-005 §4.16 The MPC, VrefCA, and VrefCS command timings with extended setup/hold and Multi-Cycle CS assertion may be used for PDA commands if the CA and CS timings have not yet been trained.
P219-006 §4.16 DDR5 introduces a CA interface-only method for Per DRAM Addressability, by having a unique PDA Enumerate ID in each DRAM, and the ability to Group A PDA Select ID in all DRAMs.
P219-007 §4.16 The unique PDA Enumerate ID requires the use of the DQ signals and a PDA Enumerate Programming Mode in the DRAM to program.
P219-008 §4.16 Once the PDA Enumerate ID has been programmed, subsequent commands must not use the DQ signals (Legacy PDA mode) to designate which DRAM is selected for the command.
P219-009 §4.16 The PDA Enumerate ID is a 4-bit field, and the PDA Select ID is also a 4-bit field.
P219-010 §4.16 When the PDA Select ID is the same as the PDA Enumerate ID or when the PDA Select ID is set to the “All DRAM” code of 1111B, the DRAM will apply the MPC, MRW, VrefCA, or VrefCS command.
P219-011 §4.16 There are a few MPC commands that do not use the PDA Select ID to determine if the command will be applied.
P219-012 §4.16 Among these MPC commands that do not use the PDA Select ID are the MPC opcodes to set the PDA Enumerate ID and the opcode to set the PDA Select ID.
P219-013 §4.16 During RESET procedure, the receive FIFO must be initialized with all ones in order to ensure that the PDA enumerate flow does not program an enumerate ID when the strobes are not toggling.
P219-014 §4.16 Table 87 summarizes which MPC commands are dependent on the PDA Select ID values.
P219-030 §4.16 NOTE 1 The PDA Enumerate ID is the only command that utilizes the PDA Enumerate ID Programming mode.
P220-001 §4.16 The following mode register fields are associated with Per DRAM Addressable operation:
P220-005 §4.16.1 1. PDA Enumerate Programming Mode is enabled by sending one or more MPC command cycles with OP[7:0]=0000 1011B.
P220-006 §4.16.1 Data Mask and PDA Enumerate are not supported for use at the same time.
P220-007 §4.16.1 Either data mask shall be disabled or DM_n driven high, while PDA Enumerate Programming Mode is enabled.
P220-008 §4.16.1 2. In the PDA Enumerate Programming Mode, only the MPC command with PDA Enumerate ID opcode is qualified with DQ0 for x4/ x8 and DQL0 for x16.
P220-009 §4.16.1 The DRAM captures DQ0 for x4/x8 and DQL0 for x16 by using DQS_c and DQS_t for x4/x8 DQSL_c and DQSL_t for x16 signals as shown in Figure 76, where DQ is driven low after the SET PDA Enumerate ID command, and DQS starts toggling at tPDA_DQS_DELAY, and DQ is held until after DQS stops toggling.
P220-010 §4.16.1 An alternate method is shown in Figure where DQ is driven low and DQS toggles continuously starting prior to the SET PDA Enumerate ID command, and remains toggling until the Exit PDA Enumerate Programming Mode command has finished.
P220-011 §4.16.1 If the value on DQ0 for x4/x8 or DQL0 for x16 is 0 then the DRAM executes the MPC command to set the PDA Enumerate ID.
P220-012 §4.16.1 The controller may choose to drive all the DQ bits.
P220-013 §4.16.1 Only the MPC command with PDA Enumerate ID opcodes will be supported in PDA Enumerate Programming Mode, and the MPC command to exit PDA Enumerate Programming Mode does not require a DQ qualification.
P220-014 §4.16.1 3. For the “don’t enumerate” case where the SDRAM ignores the PDA Enumerate ID MPC command in the PDA Enumerate Programming Mode, the DQS_t/DQS_c and DQ signals (DQSL_t/DQSL_c and DQL/DQU for x16) may be high (driven or due to RTT_PARK termination) prior to sending the MPC command to enter PDA Enumerate Programming Mode.
P220-015 §4.16.1 After entering PDA Enumerate Programming Mode, the DQS and DQ signals must remain high (driven or due to RTT_PARK termination) until exiting PDA Enumerate Programming Mode.
P220-016 §4.16.1 Holding the signals high will ensure that this DRAM is never set to a PDA Enumerate ID other than the default setting of 0xFH (15).
P220-017 §4.16.1 Timing diagram example shown in Clause 4.16.1.2.
P221-007 §4.16.1 NOTE 1 DQS_t/DQS_c are differential signals and small amounts of noise could appear as “toggling”, resulting in “Unknown” PDA Enumerate Results”.
P221-008 §4.16.1 NOTE 2 When the DQS signals are high, the DQs shall be held high as well.
P221-009 §4.16.1 NOTE 3 “Differentially Low” is defined as DQS_t low and DQS_c high (DQSL_t low and DQSL_c high for x16)
P221-010 §4.16.1 4. A minimum of one complete BL16 set of consecutive strobe edges (8 rising edges and 8 falling edges, plus 1 rising edge and 1 falling edge for the 2 tCK and 3 tCK preambles or 2 rising edges and 2 falling edges for the 4 tCK preamble) must be sent by the host within the beginning at tPDA_DQS_DELAY after the associated MPC command.
P221-011 §4.16.1 The DQ value is captured during any strobe edge during the valid low duration of the target DQ.
P221-012 §4.16.1 Valid low time is defined as the time between tPDA_S and tPDA_H.
P221-013 §4.16.1 If the DRAM captures a 0 on DQ0 (or DQL0 for x16 devices) at any strobe edge in the strobe sequence, the PDA Enumerate ID command shall be executed by the DRAM.
P221-014 §4.16.1 Since the write timings for the DQ bus have not been trained, the host must ensure a minimum of one complete BL16 set of consecutive strobe edges(8 rising edges and 8 falling edges, plus 1 rising edge and 1 falling edge for 2 tCK and 3 tCK preambles or 2 rising edges and 2 falling edges for 4 tCK preamble) occurs after a period of tPDA_DQS_Delay(min) after the associated MPC command.
P221-015 §4.16.1 The BC8 mode register setting in the DRAM is ignored while in PDA Enumerate Programming mode.
P221-017 §4.16.1 5. Prior to when the MPC command for PDA Enumerate Programming Mode entry is sent by the host, the host must drive DQS_t and DQS_c differentially low, other than when the burst of 16 strobe edges (including the preamble and postamble DQS_t/DQS_c toggles)is sent in association with the PDA Enumerate ID MPC command.
P221-018 §4.16.1 The host must send preamble and postamble DQS_t/ DQS_c toggles during the qualification of the PDA command.
P221-019 §4.16.1 Once PDA Enumerate Programming Mode is enabled in the DRAM, the host memory controller shall wait tMPC_Delay to the time the first PDA Enumerate ID MPC command is issued.
P221-020 §4.16.1 6. In the PDA Enumerate Programming Mode, only PDA Enumerate ID MPC commands and Exit PDA Enumerate Programming Mode MPC command are allowed.
P221-021 §4.16.1 7. In the PDA Enumerate Programming Mode, the default (or previously programmed) RTT_PARK value will be applied to the DQ signals.
P221-022 §4.16.1 8. The MPC PDA Enumerate ID command cycle time is defined as tPDA_DELAY.
P221-023 §4.16.1 This time is longer than the normal MPC_Delay and must be met in order to provide the DRAM time to latch the asserted DQ and complete the write operation to the PDA Enumerate ID mode register (MR1:OP[3:0], prior to the next MPC PDA Enumerate ID command shown in Figure 76.
P221-025 §4.16.1 The Exit PDA Enumerate Programming Mode MPC command is never qualified by the DQ settings and is applied to all DRAMs in the rank.
P221-026 §4.16.1 10. During the PDA Enumerate ID Programming mode, only one enumerate command is allowed to a device.
P221-027 §4.16.1 Once the PDA Enumerate ID is programmed, any change for the PDA Enumerate ID requires DRAM to enter into PDA Enumerate ID Programming mode.

P222-003 §4.16.1.1 For a multi-device PDA Enumerate ID operation, host may choose either reiterate single device enumeration as plotted in Figure 76 and Figure 77, or follow suggested multi-device enumeration sequences as plotted in Figure 78 through Figure 80.
