P231-001 §4.16.2 Once the PDA Enumerate ID’s have been programmed in all the DRAMs, the execution of future MPC/MRW/VrefCA/VrefCS commands depend on the value of the PDA Select ID and the type of MPC command.
P231-002 §4.16.2 If the PDA Select ID is set to 1111B, all DRAMs will execute the command.
P231-003 §4.16.2 For all MRW, VrefCA, and VrefCS commands, and some of the MPC commands (RTT_CA/CS/CK and RTT_PARK opcodes), the PDA Select ID will be compared to the PDA Enumerate ID to determine if the DRAM will execute the commands.
P231-004 §4.16.2 For all other MPC commands (i.e. not the RTT_CA/CS/CK and RTT_PARK opcodes), the DRAM will execute the command regardless of the PDA Select ID value.
P231-005 §4.16.2 As an example, the following sequence could be used to program unique MR fields per device:
P231-006 §4.16.2 1. Send MPC with ‘PDA Select ID’ opcode, with encoding 0000 included in the opcode
P231-008 §4.16.2 this can be any number of MRW’s
P231-009 §4.16.2 3. Send MPC with ‘PDA Select ID’ opcode, with encoding 0001 included in the opcode
P231-011 §4.16.2 this can be any number of MRW’s
P231-013 §4.16.2 6. Send MPC with ‘PDA Select ID’ opcode, with encoding 1111 included in the opcode to enable all DRAMs to execute all MRW, VrefCA, VrefCS, and MPC commands.
P231-014 §4.16.2 Timing diagram in Figure 89 shows an example sequencing of the programming of the PDA Select ID and MPC, VrefCA, VrefCS, or MRW commands.
P231-038 §4.16.2 NOTES: 1 - Commands used such as MPC, VREFCA, VREFCS or MRW have different command spacing requirements.
P231-039 §4.16.2 Please refer to those specific sections in the document for details.
P231-040 §4.16.2 2 - ANY Multi-Cycle MPC, VREFCA, or VREFCS command spacing is measured from the last valid command cycle to the first following valid command cycle, while standard command spacing goes from last valid command cycle to last valid command cycle.
P231-041 §4.16.2 See MRW command period diagrams for details.
P234-014 §4.16.2 NOTE 1 tPDA DQS_DELAY specifies when the beginning of a minimum of one complete BL16 set of consecutive strobe edges (8 rising edges and 8 falling edges, plus 1 rising edge and 1 falling edge for the 2tCK and 3tCK preambles or 2 rising edges and 2 falling edges for the 4tCK preamble) shall be sent by the host controller after the associated MPC command.
P235-001 §4.17.1 Training of the Memory Interface requires the ability to read a known pattern from the DRAM, prior to enabling writes into the DRAM.
P235-002 §4.17.1 Due to the increased frequencies supported by DDR5, a simple repeating pattern will not be sufficient for read training.
P235-003 §4.17.1 A Linear-Feedback Shift Register (LFSR) for a pattern generator will also be required.
P235-004 §4.17.1 The read training pattern is accessed when the host issues an MRR command to the MR31 address, and CRC must be disabled prior to issue this command.
P235-005 §4.17.1 In this case, the returned data will be a pattern instead of the contents of a mode register.
P235-006 §4.17.1 The timing of the read data return is the same as for an MRR or Read command, including the operation of the strobes (DQSL_t, DQSL_c, DQSU_t, DQSU_c).
P235-007 §4.17.1 The DRAM shall also support non-target ODT.
P235-008 §4.17.1 An alternate continuous burst mode is available and is configured with MRW to MR25:OP[3]=1.
P235-009 §4.17.1 Once this mode is configured, an MRR to MR31 on the target DRAM will start the pattern output and will automatically continue to output the appropriate pattern until it is stopped by either a system reset or issuing an MRW to MR25:OP[3]=0 command that reverts it to the “MRR command based (Default)” mode as shown in Figure 93.
P235-010 §4.17.1 An MRR to MR31 and an MRW to set MR25:OP[3}=0 are the only commands allowed after entering Read Training Continuous Burst Mode.
P235-011 §4.17.1 Issuing any MRR other than to MR31 is invalid and may result in unexpected DQ behavior, requiring the host to issue a Reset to exit Continuous Burst mode and restart the training with an MRR to MR31.
P235-012 §4.17.1 Once the MR25:OP[3]=0 “MRR command based (Default)” is registered by the DRAM, it will stop all pattern traffic by tCont_Exit.
P235-013 §4.17.1 Since there is no min time for tCont_Exit, the DRAM may stop the pattern prior to tCont_Exit, potentially truncating any current burst pattern.
P235-014 §4.17.1 To ensure that the DRAM’s state-machine doesn’t get into some meta-stability while turning off the output pattern, the host shall issue a second MRW with MR25:OP[3]=0 after waiting a minimum of ttMRW but less than a maximum of tCBME after the first MRW command with MR25:OP[3]=0, which will then start tCont_Exit_delay.
P235-015 §4.17.1 After tCont_Exit_delay has expired, any other valid command is then legal.
P235-016 §4.17.1 All Read Training Patterns (modes) are supported in continuous burst mode.
P235-017 §4.17.1 The host shall disable Read CRC, if enabled, prior to using continuous burst mode.
P235-018 §4.17.1 While in Read Training Pattern Continuous Burst Mode, the DRAM will set the ODT to DQS_RTT_OFF and RTT_OFF for DQS and DQ, respectively, for the duration of the continuous burst mode output.
P235-019 §4.17.1 However, if a non-target rank is intended to provide termination for the duration of the continuous burst mode output on the target rank, the nontarget rank will only terminate the DQ ODT to RTT_NOM_RD (MR35:OP[5:3]) during the BL16 associated with the MRR NT command.
P235-020 §4.17.1 Therefore, the host is responsible to ensure that RTT_PARK (MR34:OP[2:0] is programmed to align with RTT_NOM_RD (MR35:OP[5:3]), if so desired.
P235-021 §4.17.1 Upon exiting of Read Training Pattern Continuous Burst Mode, all RTT_PARK settings of the non-target rank needs programmed for normal operation if previously changed to align with RTT_NOM_RD.
P235-022 §4.17.1 Prior to utilizing either read training pattern mode (Continuous Burst Output mode or MRR Command Based mode), the initial seed value will need to be programmed in MR26-MR30 else the power on default values will be used.
P235-023 §4.17.1 The default value for the Read Pattern Data0/LFSR0 (MR26) register setting is: 0x5A and the default value for the Read Pattern Data1/LFSR1 (MR27) register setting is: 0x3C.
P235-024 §4.17.1 The Read Pattern Invert (MR28, MR29) register settings default to 0.
P235-025 §4.17.1 The Read LFSR Assignments (MR30) register setting default is 0xFE.
P235-026 §4.17.1 The DRAM will not store the current LFSR state when exiting the Continuous Burst Output Mode and may clear the pattern values stored in MR26-MR30, therefore any subsequent pattern reads will require the host to reprogram the seed, pattern, inversion and LFSR assignments in MR26-MR30.
P235-027 §4.17.1 The Read Training Pattern has 2 primary supported modes of operation.
P235-028 §4.17.1 One of the modes is referred to as the serial format.
P235-029 §4.17.1 The second mode is LFSR mode.
P235-030 §4.17.1 The LFSR mode is required due to the higher frequency bus operation for DDR5.
P235-031 §4.17.1 There is a secondary mode associated with the LFSR mode, which enables the generation of a simple high frequency clock pattern instead of the LFSR pattern.
P235-032 §4.17.1 The Read Training Pattern is a full BL16 pattern for each MRR command issued to the Read Training Pattern address.
P235-033 §4.17.1 The Read Training Pattern has a command burst length 16 regardless of the MR0 setting, and it should not be disturbed by an ACT command until the completion of training.
P236-001 §4.17.1 Table 94 shows the MR field and encodings for the Read Training Pattern format settings.
P236-007 §4.17.1 The default value for the Read Training Pattern Format register setting is: 0x0.
P236-008 §4.17.1 For Serial Read Training Pattern Format mode, the following Mode Registers are programmed with the data pattern.
P236-009 §4.17.1 There are two 8-bit registers to provide a 16 UI pattern length and two 8-bit registers to provide up to x16 data width for per-DQ-lane inversion.
P236-010 §4.17.1 The LFSR mode requires an 8-bit Mode Register to program the seed for the 8-bit LFSR.
P236-011 §4.17.1 The details of the LFSR polynomial and outputs are explained in the following section.
P236-012 §4.17.1 The Read Pattern Data0/LFSR0 and Read Pattern Data1/LFSR1 registers are re-purposed to program the LFSR seed when the Read Training Pattern Format is set to LFSR.
P236-015 §4.17.1 The default value for the Read Pattern Data0/LFSR0 register setting is: 0x5A.
P236-018 §4.17.1 The default value for the Read Pattern Data1/LFSR1 register setting is: 0x3C.
P236-019 §4.17.1 The values for the Read Pattern Data0/LFSR0 and Read Pattern Data1/LFSR1 registers may be restored to the default values under the following conditions:
P236-022 §4.17.1 Exiting Continuous Burst Output Mode If any of the above conditions occur, the Host will need to reprogram the contents of MR26 and MR27, prior to utilizing either read training pattern mode (Continuous Burst Output mode or MRR Command Based mode).
P236-023 §4.17.1 In both cases, when the Read Training Pattern Format is set to Serial mode or LFSR mode, the Read Pattern Invert - Lower DQ Bits and Read Pattern Invert - Upper DQ Bits settings will additionally invert the pattern, per DQ bit.
P236-024 §4.17.1 The Read Pattern Invert - Lower DQ Bits register will apply to x4, x8, and x16 devices.
P236-025 §4.17.1 The Read Pattern Invert - Upper DQ Bits register only applies to x16 devices, for the upper byte.
P236-028 §4.17.1 The default value for the Read Pattern Invert - Lower DQ Bits register setting is: 0x00.
P237-003 §4.17.1 The default value for the Read Pattern Invert - Upper DQ Bits register setting is: 0x00.
P237-004 §4.17.1 The values for both Read Pattern Invert - Lower and Upper DQ Bit registers may be restored to the default values under the following conditions:
P237-008 §4.17.1 If any of the above conditions occur, the Host will need to reprogram the contents of MR28 and MR29 if non-default values are desired, prior to utilizing either read training pattern mode (Continuous Burst Output mode or MRR Command Based mode).
P237-009 §4.17.1 A value of 0 in any bit location of the Read Pattern Invert - Lower DQ Bits or Read Pattern Invert - Upper DQ Bits registers will leave the pattern un-inverted for the associated DQ.
P237-010 §4.17.1 A value of 1 in any bit location of the Read Pattern Invert - Lower DQ Bits or Read Pattern Invert - Upper DQ Bits registers will invert the pattern for the associated DQ.
P237-011 §4.17.2 The LFSR is an 8-bit Galois LFSR.
P237-012 §4.17.2 The polynomial for the Galois LFSR is x8+x6+x5+x4+1.
P237-015 §4.17.2 There are two instances of the same LFSR polynomial.
P237-016 §4.17.2 These two instances will have unique seeds/states and supply patterns to any of the DQ outputs.
P238-001 §4.17.2 The seed location in the figure clarifies the mapping for the Read Pattern Data0/LFRS0 and Read Pattern Data1/LFSR1 mode registers relative to the LFSR logic.
P238-002 §4.17.2 The LFSR output is directed to any number of the DQ outputs, depending on the LFSR assignment programming.
P238-003 §4.17.2 These assignments between LFSR0 and LFSR1 to each DQ output will create a unique pattern sequence for better coverage of DQ to DQ crosstalk interactions.
P238-004 §4.17.2 The LFSR assignments are programmed according to Table 99.
P238-014 §4.17.2 The default value for the Read LFSR Assignments register setting is: 0xFE.
P238-015 §4.17.2 The values for the Read LFSR Assignments register may be restored to the default values under the following conditions:
P238-018 §4.17.2 Exiting Continuous Burst Output Mode If any of the above conditions occur, the Host will need to reprogram the contents of MR30 if non-default values are desired, prior to utilizing either read training pattern mode (Continuous Burst Output mode or MRR Command Based mode).
P238-019 §4.17.2 The LFSR output will change at the UI frequency, producing a new output value on every UI.
P238-020 §4.17.2 The LFSR will only change state to support the read data after the MRR to the specific (MR31) Read Training Pattern address.
P238-021 §4.17.2 When there are no MRR accesses to the (MR31) Read Training Pattern address, the LFSR will retain its previous state (from the end of the previous Read Training Pattern MRR access completion).
P238-022 §4.17.2 Therefore, the full state space of the LFSR may be traversed through a series of 16 MRR commands, each of which accesses 16 UI’s of LFSR output.
P238-023 §4.17.2 The BL for the LFSR data will always be BL16.
P238-024 §4.17.2 The state of the LFSR can also be changed by sending a new MRW command to reset the LFSR0 and LFSR1 seed mode registers (MR26 and MR27) or through the reset conditions listed for those registers.
P238-025 §4.17.2 A setting of 0x00 in either of the LFSR seed registers (MR26 and MR27) will not produce a pattern with any transitions to 1.
P238-026 §4.17.2 When set to this value the LFSR will produce a constant 0 pattern.
P238-027 §4.17.2 When the LFSR0 Pattern Option MR25:OP[1] is set to 1, the pattern that is supplied by the DRAM is a high frequency clock pattern, instead of the LFSR.
P238-028 §4.17.2 This clock pattern is sent only to the DQ signals that have a setting of 0 in the corresponding DQ Opcode location in the Read LFSR Assignments register.
P238-029 §4.17.2 The first UI of the pattern will have a value of 0.
P238-030 §4.17.2 The second UI will have a value of 1, and this will continue to toggle for each subsequent UI.
P238-031 §4.17.2 When the LFSR1 Pattern Option MR25:OP[2] is set to 1, the pattern that is supplied by the DRAM is a high frequency clock pattern, instead of the LFSR.
P238-032 §4.17.2 This clock pattern is sent only to the DQ signals that have a setting of 1 in the corresponding DQ Opcode location in the Read LFSR Assignments register.
P238-033 §4.17.2 The first UI of the pattern will have a value of 0.
P238-034 §4.17.2 The second UI will have a value of 1, and this will continue to toggle for each subsequent UI.
P238-035 §4.17.2 The state of the LFSR will not change when an MRR to MR31 occurs if the associated LFSR Pattern Option is set to 1 in MR25[1] for LFSR0 or MR25[2] for LFSR1, designating the clock pattern.
P238-036 §4.17.2 The state of both LFSR0 and LFSR1 will also not change when an MRR to MR31 occurs if the serial mode is selected by setting MR25[0] = 0.
P238-037 §4.17.2 The Read LFSR Assignments settings have no impact on whether or not the LFSR state progresses with each MRR to MR31.
P238-038 §4.17.2 Only the Read Training Pattern Format and LFSR Pattern Option settings determine whether the LFSR is actively computing next states.
P239-001 §4.17.3 Table 100 shows the bit sequence of the Read Training Pattern, for the following programming: Read Training Pattern Format = 0 (Serial) LFSR0 Pattern Option = 0 (These are don’t cares when in Serial Read Training Pattern Format) LFSR1 Pattern Option = 0 (These are don’t cares when in Serial Read Training Pattern Format) Read Pattern Data0/LFSR0 = 0x1C Read Pattern Data1/LFSR1 = 0x59 Read Pattern Invert - Lower DQ Bits = 0x55 Read Pattern Invert - Upper DQ Bits = 0x55
P240-001 §4.17.3 Table 101 shows the bit sequence of the Read Training Pattern, for the following programming: Read Training Pattern Format = 1 (LFSR) LFSR0 Pattern Option = 0 LFSR1 Pattern Option = 0 Read Pattern Data0/LFSR0 = 0x5A Read Pattern Data1/LFSR1 = 0x3C Read LFSR Assignments = 0xFE Read Pattern Invert - Lower DQ Bits = 0x00 Read Pattern Invert - Upper DQ Bits = 0xFF
P241-001 §4.17.3 Table 102 shows the bit sequence of the Read Training Pattern, for the following programming: Read Training Pattern Format = 1 (LFSR) LFSR0 Pattern Option = 0 LFSR1 Pattern Option = 1 (Clock Pattern Option) Read Pattern Data0/LFSR0 = 0x00 (When the LFSR seed is set to 0, this produces a constant 0 pattern) Read Pattern Data1/LFSR1 = 0x3C (This value is a don’t care when LFSR1 Pattern Option = 1) Read LFSR Assignments = 0x04 Read Pattern Invert - Lower DQ Bits = 0xFB Read Pattern Invert - Upper DQ Bits = 0xFB
P242-001 §4.17.4 The timing of the data return and strobe sequence should match that of a Read operation.
P242-002 §4.17.4 The timing of the Read Training Pattern will be similar to the MRR operation, with the exception that the MRR to the address that invokes the Read Training Pattern will be a full BL16 pattern.
P242-003 §4.17.4 The timing between MRR commands to access the Read Training Pattern is defined as tMRR_p, which supports back to back data patterns.
P242-004 §4.17.4 This is faster than a normal MRR to MRR condition which is defined as tMRR.
P242-005 §4.17.4 Figure 91 timing diagram shows the general timing sequence for an MRR that accesses the Read Training Pattern:
P242-022 §4.17.4 Note - The Read Training Pattern shall align to the DDR5 preamble timings.
P242-023 §4.17.4 The Read Training Pattern must also support back to back traffic, for any number of MRR commands sequenced every 8 tCK.
P242-038 §4.17.4 (Read Pattern) (Read Pattern) (Any Other) MRR
P242-047 §4.17.4 Note - The Read Training Pattern shall align to the DDR5 preamble timings.
P243-014 §4.17.4 NOTE 1 The Read Training Pattern shall align to the DDR5 preamble timings and will exit after the MRW (Continuous Exit encoding) has been received and but before tCont_Exit has expired.
P243-015 §4.17.4 During tCont_Exit, the data output may not follow the read pattern data.
P243-016 §4.17.4 NOTE 2 Host responsible for proper setting of RTT_PARK of the non-target rank is programmed to align with RTT_NOM_RD, if
P244-001 §4.18.1 Read preamble training supports read leveling of the host receiver timings.
P244-002 §4.18.1 This mode supports MRR transactions that access the Read Training Pattern, and cannot be used during any other data transactions.
P244-003 §4.18.1 Just like Read Training Pattern, Read Preamble Training needs to be entered with CRC disabled.
P244-004 §4.18.1 Read preamble training changes the read strobe behavior such that the strobes are always driven by the DRAM, and only toggle during a 1tCK preamble plus the actual burst of the read data.
P244-005 §4.18.1 There is no toggle during postamble time.
P244-006 §4.18.1 This mode enables the host to detect the timing of when the first data and associated strobe is returned after a read command.
P244-007 §4.18.2 The DRAM enters Read Preamble Training Mode by setting MR2:OP[0] = 1.
P244-008 §4.18.2 Read Preamble Training Mode is exited by setting MR2:OP[0] = 0.
P244-009 §4.18.2 Read Preamble Training should not be disturbed by an ACT command until the completion of the training.
P244-014 §4.18.3 Once the DRAM is placed in Read Preamble Training Mode, the only data transactions supported are MRR commands.
P244-015 §4.18.3 Other data transactions and some non-data transactions are not allowed during Read Preamble Training Mode.
P244-016 §4.18.3 Commands allowed or not allowed are as follows:
P244-017 §4.18.3 1. Allowed while in Preamble Training Mode: MPC functions allowed when current state is “Active”, MRW, MRR, REFab, REFsb, RFMab, RFMsb, NOP
P244-018 §4.18.3 2. Not allowed while in Preamble Training Mode: MPC functions only allowed when current state is “All Banks Idle”, ACT, WRP, WRPA, WR, WRA, RD, RDA, VrefCA, VrefCS, PREab, PREsb, PREpb, SRE, SREF, PDE, PDX
P244-019 §4.18.3 Once READ Preamble Training is enabled, the device will drive DQS_t LOW and DQS_c HIGH within tSDOn and remain at these levels until an MRR command is issued.
P244-020 §4.18.3 During read preamble training, a 1 tCK preamble will be used instead of the programmed DQS preamble setting.
P244-021 §4.18.3 Once the MRR command is issued, the device will drive DQS_t/DQS_c after CL-tRPRE (where tRPRE=1CK), like a normal READ burst with the Read DQS Offset setting programmed in MR40 applied.
P244-022 §4.18.3 In response to the MRR to the designated Read Training Pattern Address, the device must also drive the DQ pattern as per the Read Pattern configuration while in this mode.
P244-023 §4.18.3 The MRR commands may be sequenced to enable back to back bursts on the DQ bus.
P244-024 §4.18.3 Read preamble training mode is exited within tSDOff after setting MR2:OP[0].
P244-025 §4.18.3 Figure 94 shows the timing for the strobe driven differential low after Read Preamble Training Mode is enabled, and also shows the strobe timings including a 1tCK Preamble, after an MRR command to access the Read Training Pattern:
P245-019 §4.18.3 Read Preamble Training Mode = Enable: MR2[OP0] = 1 Read Preamble Training Mode = Disable: MR2[OP0] = 0
P245-026 §4.19.1 The CA Training Mode is a method to facilitate the loopback of a logical combination of the sampled CA[13:0] signals.
P245-027 §4.19.1 In this mode, the CK is running, and the CS_n qualifies when the CK samples the CA signals.
P245-028 §4.19.1 A loopback equation that includes all the CA signals results in an output value that is sent asynchronously on the DQ signals back to the host memory controller.
P245-029 §4.19.1 The host timings between CS_n, CK, and CA[13:0] signals can then be optimized for proper alignment.
P245-030 §4.19.1 When the DRAM is in this mode, no functional commands are executed in the DRAM.
P245-031 §4.19.1 The functional command interface is restored only after exiting this mode, which requires a CS_n assertion of two or more consecutive tCK.
P245-032 §4.19.1 Prior to entering the CA Training Mode, the CS_n signal must be aligned to the CK to meet the CS_n to CK timing specifications.
P245-033 §4.19.1 This assumes that CS Training has been completed to determine the correct CS_n timings on the host, so the timing window for the CS to be met to ensure enought setup or hold timing margin prior to entering Command/Address training mode.
P245-034 §4.19.1 Chip select training, through CSTM, can be used to accomplish this.
P245-035 §4.19.2 The CA Training Mode is enabled through an MPC command, with the opcode designated for CA Training Mode Entry.
P245-036 §4.19.2 Once this MPC command has executed no other commands will be interpreted by the DRAM.
P245-037 §4.19.2 Only the sampling of the CA signals, evaluation of the XOR result, and loop back to the DQ’s will occur.
P245-038 §4.19.2 While in CA Training Mode, the CS_n signal will only assert for a single tCK at a time.
P245-039 §4.19.2 The maximum sampling rate on the CA signals will be every 4tCK.
P245-040 §4.19.2 When the CA Training Mode has completed successfully, the CA Training Mode is disabled by asserting CS_n for 2 or more cycles in a row, while sending a NOP command on the CA bus.
P246-001 §4.19.3 In CA Training Mode, the CA values are sampled in the same way as for functional operation, where the CS_n qualifies which cycle the sampling occurs, and the sample is captured by the rising CK edge.
P246-002 §4.19.3 Unlike functional operation, there is no concept of multiple cycle commands in CA Training Mode.
P246-003 §4.19.3 Sampling of the CA signals ONLY occurs when CS_n is asserted.
P246-004 §4.19.3 Once the CA signals are sampled, the values are XOR’d to produce an output value.
P246-005 §4.19.3 This output value is driven on all the DQ pins, as a pseudo-static value.
P246-006 §4.19.3 These output values will be held until the next sample is captured on the CA bus, according to the CS_n assertion.
P246-007 §4.19.3 During CA Training Mode the CA ODT is enabled as for functional operation.
P246-008 §4.19.3 The VrefCA is Group According to the functional setting.
P246-009 §4.19.3 The timing requirements for the CA bus, CK_t, CK_c, and CS_n are the same as for functional operation.
P246-010 §4.19.3 The delay from when the CA signals are sampled during the CS_n assertion and when the output of the XOR computation is driven on the DQ pins is specified as tCATM_Valid, as shown in Figure 95.
P246-011 §4.19.3 CS_n shall be asserted every 4tCK or with greater than 4tCK separation between assertions, and thus the CA XOR output shall transition every 4tCK or greater.
P246-013 §4.19.3 The DRAM will exit CA Training Mode when the CS_n is asserted for 2 or more consecutive cycles but limited to 8 cycles.
P246-045 §4.19.3 NOTE 1 This timing parameter is applied to each DQ independently, not all-DQs valid window perspective.
P247-001 §4.19.3.1 The CATM Output is computed based on the CS_n assertion and the values of the CA inputs.
P247-006 §4.19.3.1 NOTE 1 The XOR function occurs after mirroring/inversion recovery, and only includes signals supported on the DRAM, i.e. may not include CA[13], depending on density (including stacking).
P247-007 §4.19.3.1 If CA[13] is not needed for the DRAM's density, the logical value shall be considered 0 for the XOR computation, though as indicated in the pin description table for MIR, the ball location associated with CA13's logical input (which switches with CA12) shall be connected to VDDQ.
P247-008 §4.19.3.2 Table 108 shows which signals will transmit the output of the CA Training Mode loopback equation.
P247-009 §4.19.3.2 These values are driven asynchronously as pseudo-static values, updating with a new output at a time tCATM_Valid after each CS_n assertion.
P248-001 §4.20.1 The CS Training Mode is a method to facilitate the loopback of a sampled sequence of the CS_n signal.
P248-002 §4.20.1 In this mode, the CK is running, and the CA signals are held in a NOP command encoding state.
P248-003 §4.20.1 Once this mode is enabled and the DRAM devices are selected to actively sample and drive feedback, The DRAM will sample the CS_n signal on the rising edge of CK.
P248-004 §4.20.1 Every set of four CK rising edge samples will be included in a logical computation to determine the CSTM Output result that is sent back to the host on the DQ bus.
P248-005 §4.20.1 Once sampling begins, the DRAM must maintain the consecutive grouping of the samples every 4 tCK.
P248-006 §4.20.1 When the CS_n Sample[0] and Sample[2] results in a logic 0 and the CS_n Sample[1] and Sample[3] results in a logic 1, the DRAM will drive a 0 on all the DQ signals.
P248-007 §4.20.1 There is no requirement to drive any strobes, and the output signal could transition as often as every 4 tCK.
P248-008 §4.20.2 The CS Training Mode is enabled when the host sends an MPC command with the opcode for CS Training Mode Entry.
P248-009 §4.20.2 Since CS Training shall occur prior to establishing alignment between CK and CS_n signals, the MPC command extends beyond multiple tCK cycles, during which the CS_n signal is asserted.
P248-010 §4.20.2 When the DRAM is in this mode, commands are still actively processed.
P248-011 §4.20.2 The only commands that should be sent by the host memory controller while CS Training Mode is enabled are the NOP command and the MPC to exit CS Training Mode.
P248-012 §4.20.2 Any other command may produce unreliable results.
P248-013 §4.20.2 Once the DRAM has CS Training Mode enabled, the DRAM begins sampling on every rising CK edge, with the 4-sample groups looping consecutively.
P248-014 §4.20.2 Depending on the value of the samples, the DQ signals are driven high or low.
P248-015 §4.20.2 Prior to entering CS Training Mode, the DQ signals are not driven by the DRAM and are terminated according to the default RTT_PARK setting.
P248-016 §4.20.2 After CS Training Mode is enabled, the DQ signal will begin driving the output values based on the CS Training Mode samples.
P248-017 §4.20.2 Once the DQ signals are driven by the DRAM, RTT_PARK termination will no longer be applied, similar to a READ operation.
P248-018 §4.20.2 To exit CS Training Mode, an MPC command shall be sent to disable CS Training Mode.
P248-019 §4.20.2 Even though the timing relationship between CS_n and CK is understood when exiting CS Training Mode, the host is required to send a Multi-Cycle CS_n assertion during the MPC command or a single tCK assertion while MR2:OP[4]=0B.
P248-020 §4.20.3 In CS Training Mode, the CS_n values are sampled on all CK rising edges.
P248-021 §4.20.3 Each group of 4 consecutive samples is evaluated in pairs, and then the two pairs are combined with a logical OR prior to sending to the DQ output.
P248-022 §4.20.3 The samples evaluation to determine the output is as follows:
P248-038 §4.20.3 *When there is no change on the CSTM Output from previous evaluation, DQ shall continue to drive same value continuously with no switching on the bus.
P249-001 §4.20.3 During CS Training Mode the CA ODT is enabled as for functional operation.
P249-002 §4.20.3 The VrefCA is Group According to the functional setting (through the VrefCA Command).
P249-003 §4.20.3 The delay from when the CS_n signals are sampled during the fourth CK rising edge (Sample[3]) to when the output of the sample evaluation is driven to a stable value on the DQ pins is specified as tCSTM_Valid, as shown in Figure 96.
P249-004 §4.20.3 The details of the tCSTM_entry, tCSTM_exit, and tCSTM_DQ_Window are also illustrated.
P249-064 §4.20.3 NOTE 1 See MPC Command for details on Setup, Hold and command register time.
P249-065 §4.20.3 NOTE 2 The CA bus must meet setup and hold times on any clock where it is possible that CS_n might be sampled low while in CS training mode.
P249-066 §4.20.3 When host trains CS_n timing for DDR5 by using CSTM, CS_n sampling timing for each DRAM could be different from each other because the variation of internal timing is different for each DRAM.
P249-067 §4.20.3 Therefore, even though the CS setup/hold time is appropriate for each DRAM, 4-tCK CS_n sampling window which may have different starting points could appear differently as shown in Figure 98.
P249-068 §4.20.3 Host should train CS_n timing based on asserting every edge of CS_n to cover multiple DRAMs without exiting CSTM.
P250-022 §4.20.3 NOTE 1 See MPC Command for details on Setup, Hold and command register time.
P250-030 §4.20.3 NOTE 1 This timing parameter is applied to each DQ independently, not all-DQs valid window perspective.
P251-001 §4.20.3.1 Table 113 shows which signals will transmit the output of the CS Training Mode loopback sample evaluation.
P251-002 §4.20.3.1 These values are driven asynchronously, but may switch as often as every 4tCK.
P252-002 §4.21.1 The fly-by topology has benefits from reducing number of stubs and their length, but it also causes flight time skew between clock and strobe at every DRAM on the DIMM.
P252-003 §4.21.1 This makes it difficult for the memory controller to set the timings of the WRITE DQS signaling according to the Write Latency timing specification at the DRAM.
P252-004 §4.21.1 Therefore, the DDR5 SDRAM supports a ‘write leveling’ feature to allow the controller to compensate for channel skew.
P252-005 §4.21.1 The DDR5 Write Leveling Training also allows for an unmatched path between CK and DQS within the DRAM, and thus supports an internal Write Leveling Training flow to account for the difference in internal delays.
P252-006 §4.21.1 The DDR5 DRAM also provides a programmable timing in its write logic, controlled by the Write Leveling Internal Cycle Alignment (WICA) mode register (MR3), which provides a means for improved performance of the device’s receiver.
P252-007 §4.21.1 The proper setting of this register shall be determined by the memory controller, either as described in the sections below, or by some other means.
P252-008 §4.21.1 This delay setting is specific to each DRAM, its Write Preamble setting, and the operating frequency being used.
P252-009 §4.21.1 Once the proper setting has been determined for a given WICA byte, Write preamble setting, and operating frequency, that setting may be subsequently restored to the DRAM after reset, power-cycle, or return to a previously used operating frequency.
P252-010 §4.21.1 The memory controller can use the ‘write leveling’ feature and feedback from the DDR5 DRAM to adjust the controller’s differential DQS (DQS_t - DQS_c) output delay to align to the phase and cycle that corresponds to the CAS Write Latency (CWL) delay after the WRITE command.
P252-011 §4.21.1 The memory controller involved in the leveling must have an adjustable delay setting on the DQS pins to align the rising edge of differential DQS with the timing at the receiver that is the pin-level Write Latency (External Write Leveling Training), and to align DQS with the internal DRAM Write Latency timing point (Write Leveling Internal Cycle Alignment).
P252-012 §4.21.1 The internal DRAM Write Latency timing point may be skewed from the pin-level Write Latency timing point.
P252-013 §4.21.1 The host will minimize this skew (tDQSoffset) through the Write Leveling Training flow.
P252-014 §4.21.1 Since the system and DIMM delays vary, the DRAM will support the ability for the host to align the DQS timings with a pin-level Write Latency differential CK (CK_t-CK_c) edge.
P252-015 §4.21.1 This alignment is referred to as External Write Leveling.
P252-016 §4.21.1 Once the DQS host timings are aligned at the DRAM Write Latency timing, the internal DRAM timings are optimized for lowest power and internal delay.
P252-017 §4.21.1 This is accomplished when the host enables the Internal Write Timing setting in MR2.
P252-018 §4.21.1 In order to compensate for the difference in delay, the host will execute an Internal Write Leveling Training sequence, which includes sweeping the Write Leveling Internal Cycle Alignment (WICA) settings in MR3 (Write Leveling Internal Cycle Alignment Operation) and then finalizing the differential DQS phase and offset (Write Leveling Internal Phase Alignment and Final Host DQS Timing Operation).
P252-019 §4.21.1 During Write Leveling Training (both External and Internal), the DQS pattern shall include the full programmed write preamble and only the first toggle of the normal data burst sequence.
P252-020 §4.21.1 The DRAM will sample the Internal Write Leveling Pulse relative to the first DQS toggle of the data burst sequence (last rising differential DQS edge sent by the host) and asynchronously feed back the result of this sample on the DQ bus, transitioning between tWLOmin and tWLOmax. If the DQ bus output is LOW at tWLOmax, the Internal Write Leveling Pulse signal was sampled while it was deasserted (LOW).
P252-021 §4.21.1 Likewise, if the DQ bus output is HIGH at tWLOmax, the Internal Write Leveling Pulse signal was sampled while it was asserted (HIGH).
P252-022 §4.21.1 The sampled feedback state will remain on the DQ bus until a subsequent Write Leveling sample changes the state, or until Write Leveling Training is exited.
P252-023 §4.21.1 The Internal Write Leveling Pulse is generated in response to a WRITE command, and held statically low otherwise.
P252-024 §4.21.1 To perform the Write Leveling training, the controller repeatedly sends a WRITE command, delays DQS, and monitors the DQ feedback after tWLOmax until a transition from 0 to 1 is detected, indicating alignment with the Internal Write Leveling Pulse.
P252-025 §4.21.1 During Internal Write Leveling Training flows, the host will apply an offset to the starting point or the final setting of the DQS signals.
P252-026 §4.21.1 The offsets are referred to as WL_ADJ_start and WL_ADJ_end.
P252-027 §4.21.1 This will minimize the tDQSoffset variation across different DRAM devices.
P252-029 §4.21.1 When External and Internal Write Leveling Training flows are complete and the final WL_ADJ_end offset has been applied to the DQS timings, the DQS is phase aligned and cycle aligned for write operations.
P252-030 §4.21.1 During the training sequence the DRAM in Write Leveling training mode will apply ODT to the strobes in the same way as for functional operation.
P252-031 §4.21.1 All non-target ranks (which will not be in Write Leveling Training Mode) will apply ODT as defined for functional operation.
P252-032 §4.21.1 Prior to executing the DDR5 Write Leveling Training Flow, the DRAM tWPRE value must be configured to the functional operation setting.
P252-033 §4.21.1 Note: DQS ODT is based on a DQS PARK Mode and is not enabled and disabled with DQ ODT timings.
P253-001 §4.21.2 The MR fields for Write Leveling Training, Internal Write Timing, and Write Leveling Internal Cycle Alignment are part of MR2 and MR3.
P253-002 §4.21.2 To enter Write Leveling Training Mode, the controller shall program MR2:OP[1]=1.
P253-003 §4.21.2 Likewise, to exit the Write Leveling Training Mode, the controller will program MR2:OP[1]=0.
P253-004 §4.21.2 Write Leveling Internal Cycle Alignment offers two nibbles to control the upper and lower DQ bytes.
P253-005 §4.21.2 The lower byte WL internal Cycle alignment is intended for x4, x8 and x16 configurations, while the upper byte is only for x16 configurations.The Internal Write Timing, once enabled (MR2:OP[7]=1), shall remain enabled through the Internal Write Leveling Training flow and for functional operation.
P253-006 §4.21.2 The host is responsible for incrementing the Write Leveling Internal Cycle Alignment setting until the Internal Write Leveling Pulse is pulled early enough to align with the differential DQS signals that was previously aligned with the pin-level Write Latency timing.
P253-007 §4.21.2 The Write Leveling Internal Cycle Alignment setting only applies when the Internal Write Timing is enabled.
P253-008 §4.21.2 Only BL16 Mode is supported in Write Leveling Training Mode.
P253-009 §4.21.3 When Write Leveling Mode is enabled (MR2:OP[1]=1) and the Internal Write Timing is disabled (MR2:OP[7]=0), the DRAM will have an Internal Write Leveling pulse that indicates how the DQS signals shall be aligned to match the pin-level Write Latency timings.
P253-010 §4.21.3 The rising edge of the Internal Write Leveling Pulse will align to the rising edge of the differential CK signal that coincides with CAS Write Latency (CWL), as shown in the following diagrams.
P253-011 §4.21.3 The differential DQS signals driven by the controller during leveling mode must be terminated by the DRAM based on ranks populated.
P253-012 §4.21.3 Similarly, the DQ bus driven by the DRAM must also be terminated at the controller.
P253-013 §4.21.3 All data bits shall carry the leveling feedback to the controller across the DRAM configurations x4, x8, and x16.
P253-014 §4.21.3 On a x16 device, both byte lanes shall be leveled independently.
P253-015 §4.21.3 Therefore, a separate feedback mechanism shall be available for each byte lane.
P253-016 §4.21.3 The upper data bits should provide the feedback of the upper diff_DQS(diff_UDQS) to Internal Write Leveling Pulse relationship whereas the lower data bits would indicate the lower diff_DQS(diff_LDQS) to Internal Write Leveling Pulse relationship.
P253-017 §4.21.3 When training a x16 device, the data bits of a byte will remain in the “don’t care” state until its applicable DQS signals are toggled.
P253-018 §4.21.3 Once the DQS signals are toggled, the DQs will follow the behavior previously described.
P253-019 §4.21.3 The following diagram shows the timing sequence to enter Write Leveling Training Mode, operation during Write Leveling Training Mode (with Internal Write Timings disabled), and the timing sequence to exit Write Leveling Training mode.
P253-020 §4.21.3 An MRW command is sent to enable Write Leveling Training Mode.
P253-021 §4.21.3 Prior to sending the MRW command to enable Write Leveling Training Mode, the host memory controller must drive the DQS signals differentially low.
P253-022 §4.21.3 After tWLPEN time, the controller can send a WRITE command, followed by strobe pulses.
P253-023 §4.21.3 The DQS signals shall always drive differentially low, except during the WRITE preamble and burst strobe sequence .
P253-024 §4.21.3 There is no restriction as to how early the strobe pulses are sent, so long as they are no earlier than CWL/2 after the WRITE command.
P253-025 §4.21.3 The DRAM will sample the Internal Write Leveling Pulse relative to the first DQS toggle of the data burst sequence and asynchronously feed back the result of this sample on the DQ bus, transitioning between tWLOmin and tWLOmax.
P253-026 §4.21.3 While in Write Leveling Training mode, the host controller may send ACT and PRE commands.
P253-027 §4.21.3 The DRAM will ignore these commands.
P253-028 §4.21.3 The address associated with the ACT and the WRITE commands may be any value.
P253-029 §4.21.3 The following timing diagrams demonstrate the timing requirements associated with Write Leveling Training Mode Entry, the Internal Write Leveling Pulse alignment during External Write Leveling Training, and the DQ sample feedback timing.
P254-035 §4.21.3 CMD WRITE MRW DES DES DES DES DES DES DES DES DES DES DES DES DES DES DES DES DES MRW DES DES
P255-001 §4.21.3 The Memory controller initiates Write Leveling mode of all DRAMs in a rank by setting MR2:OP[1]=1.
P255-002 §4.21.3 When entering write leveling mode, the DQ pins are in undefined driving mode.
P255-003 §4.21.3 Since the controller levels one rank at a time, all non-target ranks will set Write Leveling Mode to disabled.
P255-004 §4.21.3 The Controller may assert non-target ODT through the normal WRITE command protocol.
P255-005 §4.21.3 DQS_RTT_PARK termination will apply to the DQS_t and DQS_c signals.
P255-006 §4.21.3 The Controller shall drive DQS differentially LOW prior to sending the MRW command to enable Write Leveling Training Mode.
P255-007 §4.21.3 The WRITE command must occur after a delay of tWLPEN relative to when the MRW enabled Write Leveling Training Mode.
P255-008 §4.21.3 The DRAM will sample the Internal Write Leveling Pulse and asynchronously feed back the result of this sample on the DQ bus, transitioning between tWLOmin and tWLOmax. The controller can sample the state of any DQ bit after tWLOmax. Based on the sampled state, the controller decides to increment or decrement the DQS delay setting and launches the next WRITE command with associated DQS_t/DQS_c pulse (or pulse sequence) after some time, which is controller dependent.
P255-009 §4.21.3 Once a 0 to 1 transition on the DQ bus is detected, the controller locks DQS delay setting and external write leveling is achieved for the device.
P255-010 §4.21.3 Write Leveling Training may be executed by issuing consecutive Write commands after entering Write Leveling Mode has been enabled (no requirement to exit and reenter between each Write command).
P255-011 §4.21.3 The minimum Write to Write command spacing during Write Leveling Mode is tWL_Write and is defined as “max(CWL, last DQS differential toggle) + tWLO(max)+ 2nCK”.
P255-012 §4.21.3 An example of consecutive Write commands given during Write Leveling Training is shown in Figure 103:
P256-001 §4.21.4 After the external Write Leveling Training step is completed, and after the host DQS signals have been aligned to the pin-level Write Latency timing, the host will apply a negative offset (WL_ADJ_start) to the DQS timing.
P256-002 §4.21.4 The WL_ADJ_start offset moves the DQS timing prior to the Internal Write Leveling Pulse (causing LOW observed on the DQ pins), and it is dependent on the tWPRE setting (shown in table below).
P256-003 §4.21.4 This will be a reference point for aligning the iInternal Write Leveling Pulse via the WICA settings in MR3.
P256-004 §4.21.4 The Internal Cycle Alignment training begins when an MRW command is issued to enable Internal Write Timing in MR2 (keeping OP[1]=1 and setting MR2:OP[7]=1).
P256-005 §4.21.4 The host sweeps the Write Leveling Internal Cycle Alignment (WICA) delay settings in MR3, starting with the 0tCK offset default.
P256-006 §4.21.4 Increasing the WICA mode register offset setting speeds up the timing of the WRITE command by reducing the WRITE Command to Internal Write Pulse delay until the DQS is aligned with the internal Write Leveling Pulse assertion.
P256-012 §4.21.4 NOTE 1 For tWPRE = 4 tCK, CL is required to be ≥ 30 during Write Leveling Training Mode operation.
P256-013 §4.21.4 This is irrespective of the CL setting for tWPRE = 4 tCK during normal operation.
P256-014 §4.21.4 Due to potential measurement errors that can occur during the internal Write Leveling Training, the DRAM may include an optional 0.5 tCK adjustment for optimizing the Internal Cycle Alignment.
P256-016 §4.21.4 Figure 104 is an example of an Internal Write Leveling training flow with the addition of the +0.5tCK WICA adjustment (additional flow shown in blue).
P256-025 §4.21.4 Apply negative DQS timing adjustment (tWL_ADJ_start)
P256-030 §4.21.4 Note: Net effect is WICA = WICA + 0.5
P256-038 §4.21.4 Apply positive DQS timing adjustment tWL_ADJ_end
P256-039 §4.21.4 Set WICA value (MR3:OP[3:0] = WICA)
P256-042 §4.21.4 Note: Net effect is WICA = WICA - 0.5
P256-043 §4.21.4 WRITE Cmd WICA = WICA + 1
P256-045 §4.21.4 Exit WL (MR2:OP[1] = 0) (Keep MR2:OP[7]=1 to use the WICA value for normal writes
P257-001 §4.21.4 As previously noted, the DDR5 x16 devices allow for independent lower and upper byte Write Leveling Training and setting.Although unique values are preferred to optimize (minimize) tDQS offset, setting both the lower and upper bytes to use the same WICA offset is allowed since the internal paths for both bytes are expected to be closely matched, meaning Internal Write Leveling Training that results in a different byte settings per byte indicates the internal paths are on a boundary between the two settings.When choosing a common WICA offset value, the starting values of the lower and upper WICA offset should differ by no more than one.
P257-002 §4.21.4 The higher (smallest absolute value since the WICA values apply a negative offset) of the two values shall be programmed in both bytes of
P257-007 §4.21.4 When choosing a common 0.5tCK WICA offset value, the WICA value programmed in both bytes of the DRAM shall be the same before applying the 0.5tCK offset (if the byte values are different, the higher value shall be programmed in both bytes of the DRAM as noted above and the 0.5tCK offset step is skipped).
P257-008 §4.21.4 If the 0.5tCK training indicates that one byte requires the +0.5tCK offset but the other byte does not, this also indicates the two bytes are on a boundary between the two settings.
P257-009 §4.21.4 In this case, the +0.5tCK shall be applied to both bytes, resulting inthe higher (smallest absolute) value being used.
P257-014 §4.21.4 When using a common WICA offset value approach to the Internal Write Leveling training, both bytes shall do the final training sweep after applying the common WICA offset value (both the full cycle and 0.5tCK WICA, if applicable).
P257-015 §4.21.4 Figure 105 and Figure 106 timing diagrams demonstrate Write Leveling Training operation when Internal Write Timings are enabled and the Internal Cycle Alignment is set such that the Internal Write Leveling Pulse has not yet reached the host DQS toggles.
P258-030 §4.21.4 Figure 107 and Figure 108 timing diagrams demonstrate Write Leveling Training operation when Internal Write Timings are enabled and the Internal Cycle Alignment is set such that the Internal Write Leveling Pulse has completed the coarse alignment to the host DQS timing.
P259-016 §4.21.4 An example of Write Leveling Training internal Cycle Alignment executed by issuing consecutive Write commands after entering Write Leveling Mode has been enabled (no requirement to exit and reenter between each Write command) is shown in Figure 109:
P260-001 §4.21.5 Once the Internal Cycle Alignment is achieved, the host shall perform a final fine sweep of the DQS timings with Internal Write Timing enabled (MR2:OP[7]=1) to establish an even closer phase alignment to the rising edge of the internal Write Leveling Pulse.
P260-002 §4.21.5 Once this is complete, the host will then add a positive offset of WL_ADJ_end (dependent upon tWPRE) to the DQS timings.
P260-003 §4.21.5 This results in a tDQS offset between -0.5tCK and +0.5tCK with measurement adjustments of tWLS/tWLH, placing the rising edge of the Internal Write Leveling Pulse within the preamble.
P260-004 §4.21.5 If the optional half step WICA process is used during Write Leveling training, this results in a tDQS offset between -0.25tCk and +0.25tCK with measurement adjustments of tWLS/tWLH.
P260-005 §4.21.5 After the WL_ADJ_end offset has been applied, the host will disable Write Leveling Training Mode (MR2:OP[1]=0).
P260-006 §4.21.5 The Internal Write Timing will remain enabled and the Internal Cycle Alignment setting will retain the coarse setting that was trained.
P260-007 §4.21.5 After every reset, the host must either restore these settings or execute the full Write Leveling Training flow.
P260-008 §4.21.5 Figure 110 and Figure 111 show the timing relationships for the final placement of the host DQS timings relative to the Internal Write only for illustration.
P261-001 §4.21.5 Table 117 summarizes the timing parameter ranges associated with Write Leveling Training Mode:
P261-007 §4.21.5 NOTE 1 There is no Max limit for the tWL_Pulse_Width, but the Write Leveling Internal Pulse must begin at zero for each WRITE command.
P261-008 §4.21.6 When the DRAM is in Write Leveling Mode, the DQS_c/DQS_t termination (DQS_RTT_PARK) and the Command and Control termination (RTT_CA, RTT_CK, RTT_CS) will be the same as for functional operation.
P261-009 §4.21.6 The DQ signals will not be terminated in the DRAM, but instead will be driving values to the controller.
P261-010 §4.21.6 The host controller will apply termination for the DQ signals.
P261-014 §4.21.6 NOTE Termination for TDQS/DM is not included since TDQS and DM are disabled during Write Leveling Training Mode.
P262-001 §4.22.1 The DDR5 memory device supports a connectivity test (CT) mode, which is designed to greatly speed up testing of electrical continuity of pin interconnection on the PC boards between the DDR5 memory devices and the memory controller on the SoC.
P262-002 §4.22.1 Designed to work seamlessly with any boundary scan devices, the CT mode is required for all DRAM devices independent of density and interface width.
P262-003 §4.22.1 This applies to x4, x8, and x16 interface widths.
P262-004 §4.22.1 Contrary to other conventional shift register based test modes, where test patterns are shifted in and out of the memory devices serially in each clock, DDR5’s CT mode allows test patterns to be entered in parallel into the test input pins and the test results extracted in parallel from the test output pins of the DDR5 memory device at the same time, significantly enhancing the speed of the connectivity check.
P262-005 §4.22.1 Prior to entering CT Mode, RESET_n is registered to High.
P262-006 §4.22.1 The CT Mode is enabled by asserting the Test Enable (TEN) pin.
P262-007 §4.22.1 Once put in the CT mode by asserting the TEN pin, the DDR5 memory device effectively appears as an asynchronous device to the external controlling agent; after the input test pattern is applied, the connectivity test results are available for extraction in parallel at the test output pins after a fixed propagation delay.
P262-008 §4.22.1 A reset of the DDR5 memory device is required after exiting the CT mode.
P262-009 §4.22.2 Only digital pins can be tested via the CT mode.
P262-010 §4.22.2 For the purpose of connectivity check, all pins that are used for the digital logic in the DDR5 memory device are classified as one of the following types:
P262-011 §4.22.2 1. Test Enable (TEN) pin: when asserted high, this pin causes the DDR5 memory device to enter the CT mode.
P262-012 §4.22.2 In this mode, the normal memory function inside the DDR5 memory device is bypassed and the IO pins appear as a set of test input and output pins to the external controlling agent.
P262-013 §4.22.2 The TEN pin is dedicated to the connectivity check function and will not be used during normal memory operation.
P262-014 §4.22.2 2. Chip Select (CS_n) pin: when asserted low, this pin enables the test output pins in the DDR5 memory device.
P262-015 §4.22.2 When de-asserted, the output pins in the DDR5 memory device will be Hi-z.
P262-017 §4.22.2 3. Test Input: a group of pins that are used during normal DDR5 DRAM operation are designated as test input pins.
P262-018 §4.22.2 These pins are used to enter the test pattern in CT mode.
P262-019 §4.22.2 Most Test Input pins are input pins during normal operation.
P262-020 §4.22.2 The ALERT_n pin is the only output pin that will be used as a Test Input during CT mode.
P262-021 §4.22.2 The CK_t and CK_c pins are single-ended Test Input pins during CT Mode.
P262-022 §4.22.2 4. Test Output: a group of pins that are used during normal DDR5 DRAM operation are designated test output pins.
P262-023 §4.22.2 These pins are used for extraction of the connectivity test results in CT mode.
P262-024 §4.22.2 5. Reset: Fixed high level for RESET_n is required during CT mode, same as normal function.
P262-034 §4.22.2 NOTE 1 Test Outputs may contain the Upper and Lower label identification used with x16 devices.
P262-035 §4.22.2 In the case of x4/x8 devices, the lower (L) identification may be removed.
P262-036 §4.22.2 NOTE2 CAI and MIR input level do not affect to “Test Outputs” values.
P263-001 §4.22.3.1 MTx is an internal signal to be used to generate the signal to drive the output signals.
P263-002 §4.22.3.1 These internal signals are the same across all interface widths and densities.
P263-038 §4.22.3.2 NOTE Test Outputs may contain the Upper and Lower label identification used with x16 devices.
P263-039 §4.22.3.2 In the case of x4/x8 devices, the lower (L) identification may be removed.
P264-001 §4.23.1 The MPC command is used to initiate ZQ Calibration, which calibrates the output driver and command/address termination impedance across process, temperature, and voltage.
P264-003 §4.23.1 There are two ZQ Calibration modes initiated with the MPC command: ZQCal Start, and ZQCal Latch.
P264-005 §4.23.1 A ZQCal Start command may be issued anytime the DRAM is in a state in which it can receive valid commands.
P264-006 §4.23.1 There are two timing parameters associated with ZQ Calibration.
P264-007 §4.23.1 tZQCAL is the time from when the ZQCal Start MPC command is sent to when the host can send the ZQCal Latch MPC command.
P264-008 §4.23.1 tZQLAT is the time from when the ZQCal Latch MPC command is sent by the host to when the CA bus (and subsequently the DQ bus) can be used for normal operation.
P264-009 §4.23.1 A ZQCal Latch Command may be issued anytime outside of power-down after tZQCAL has expired and all DQ bus operations have completed.
P264-010 §4.23.1 The CA Bus must maintain a Deselect state during tZQLAT to allow CA ODT calibration settings to be updated.
P264-011 §4.23.1 Issuing consecutive ZQCAL Start commands is permitted, and subsequent commands not violating tZQCal of an ongoing ZQ calibration will initiate a new ZQ calibration operation.
P264-012 §4.23.1 ZQCal Start commands violating tZQCal may or may not initiate a new ZQ calibration operation.In this case, tZQCal should be counted from the last issued ZQCal start command.
P264-013 §4.23.1 A ZQCal Latch command issued before the expiration of tZQCal may latch or relatch the results of a previous ZQ Calibration operation that completed the calibration.
P264-014 §4.23.1 A ZQCal Latch command issued without rerunning the ZQ Calibration operation will relatch the results of the previous ZQ Calibration operation that completed.
P264-015 §4.23.1 The appropriate interval between ZQCal Start commands can be determined from the ‘ODT Voltage and Temperature Sensitivity’ tables and other application-specific parameters.
P264-016 §4.23.1 One method for calculating the interval between ZQCal Start commands, given the temperature (Tdriftrate) and voltage (Vdriftrate) drift rates that the DRAM is subject to in the application, is illustrated.
P264-017 §4.23.1 The interval could be defined by the following formula:
P264-020 §4.23.1 , where TSens = max(dRTTdT, dRondT) and VSens = max(dRTTdV, dRondV) define the DRAM temperature and voltage sensitivities.
P264-021 §4.23.1 For example, if TSens = 1.5% / °C, VSens = 0.15% / mV, Tdriftrate = 1 °C / sec and Vdriftrate = 15 mV / sec, then the interval between ZQCS commands is calculated as:
P265-004 §4.23.1 NOTE Amount of changes might be design guaranteed, and actual numbers would be vendor specific.
P265-008 §4.23.1 NOTE Amount of changes might be design guaranteed, and actual numbers would be vendor specific.
P265-009 §4.23.2 To use the ZQ calibration function, a 240 ohm +/- 1% tolerance external resistor must be connected between the ZQ pin and VSS.
P265-010 §4.23.2 If the system configuration has more than one rank, and if the ZQ pins of both ranks are attached to a single resistor, then the SDRAM controller must ensure that the ZQCals don’t overlap.
P265-011 §4.23.2 The total capacitive loading on the ZQ pin must not exceed the max external loading of 25 pF with the addition of the device ZQ pincap (5 pF) for a total capacitive loading of 30 pF.
P266-001 §4.24.1 The VrefCA setting must be set prior to training the CS_n and CA bus timings relative to CK.
P266-002 §4.24.1 In order to accomplish this, DDR5-SDRAMs will support a single UI command specifically for setting the VrefCA setting.
P266-003 §4.24.1 This avoids any timing and/or default VrefCA setting issues with sending a 2UI MRW command, by enabling the host to extend the setup and hold time for the CA signals.
P266-004 §4.24.1 In addition, the VrefCA command will support multiple cycles of CS_n assertion.
P266-005 §4.24.1 The multiple cycles of CS_n assertion ensures the DRAM will capture the VrefCA command during at least one rising CK_t/CK_c edge.
P266-010 §4.24.2 The command is sampled on every rising edge of CK_t/CK_c.
P266-011 §4.24.2 The host shall ensure that the CA signals are valid during the entire CS_n assertion time.
P266-012 §4.24.2 The timing of the CS_n assertion may not satisfy the setup/hold requirements around all CK_t/CK_c transitions, but it will satisfy the setup/hold requirements relative to at least one CK_t/CK_c rising edge.
P266-013 §4.24.2 Multi-cycle CS assertion is enabled or disabled by programming the mode register MR2:OP[4] bit.
P266-014 §4.24.2 This timing relationship can always be used by the host to send the VrefCA commands even after training has been completed for the interface.
P266-015 §4.24.2 For the DRAM to latch the VrefCA command in cases where the alignment between CS_n, CA, and CK may be unknown, the CA inputs shall reach the proper command state at least three cycles prior to CS_n transitioning from high to low, CS_n shall remain low for tVrefCA_CS, and CA shall remain in the proper command state for at least three cycles after CS_n transitions from low to high.
P266-037 §4.24.2 NOTE 1 Multiple cycles are used to avoid possible metastability of CS_n.
P266-038 §4.24.2 NOTE 2 At the end of CSTM and CATM, it is assumed that the host should be able to place the CS_n and the CA signals appropriately.
P266-039 §4.24.2 This applies only to Multi-Cycle VREFCA commands when MR2:OP[4]=0B.
P267-001 §4.25.1 The VrefCS setting should be set, if needed, prior to training the CS_n and CA bus timings relative to CK.
P267-002 §4.25.1 In order to accomplish this, DDR5-SDRAMs will support a single UI command specifically for setting the VrefCS setting (similar to VrefCA).
P267-003 §4.25.1 This avoids any timing and/or default VrefCS setting issues with sending a 2UI MRW command, by enabling the host to extend the setup and hold time for the CA signals.
P267-004 §4.25.1 In addition, the VrefCS command will support multiple cycles of CS_n assertion.
P267-005 §4.25.1 The multiple cycles of CS_n assertion ensures the DRAM will capture the VrefCS command during at least one rising CK_t/CK_c edge.
P267-006 §4.25.1 NOTE: The operation, functionality and timings for VrefCS are effectively the same as VrefCA with the exception of a different explicit command noted below and the fact that it modifies the VREF of the chip select pin vs the CA pins.
P267-011 §4.25.2 The command is sampled on every rising edge of CK_t/CK_c.
P267-012 §4.25.2 The host shall ensure that the CA signals are valid during the entire CS_n assertion time.
P267-013 §4.25.2 The timing of the CS_n assertion may not satisfy the setup/hold requirements around all CK_t/CK_c transitions, but it will satisfy the setup/hold requirements relative to at least one CK_t/CK_c rising edge.
P267-014 §4.25.2 Multi-Cycle CS assertion is enabled or disabled by programming the mode register MR2:OP[4] bit.
P267-015 §4.25.2 This timing relationship can always be used by the host to send the VrefCS commands even after training has been completed for the interface.
P267-016 §4.25.2 For the DRAM to latch the VrefCS command in cases where the alignment between CS_n, CA, and CK may be unknown, the CA inputs shall reach the proper command state at least three cycles prior to CS_n transitioning from high to low, CS_n shall remain low for tVrefCS_CS, and CA shall remain in the proper command state for at least three cycles after CS_n transitions from low to high.
P267-038 §4.25.2 NOTE 1 Multiple cycles are used to avoid possible metastability of CS_n.
P267-039 §4.25.2 NOTE 2 At the end of CSTM and CATM, it is assumed that the host should be able to place the CS_n and the CA signals appropriately.
P267-040 §4.25.2 This applies only to Multi-Cycle VREFCS commands when MR2:OP[4]=0B.
