P286-001 §4.29 DDR5 supports Fail Row address repair, PPR which allows a simple and easy repair method in a system.
P286-002 §4.29 Two methods are provided: Hard Post Package Repair (hPPR) for a permanent Row repair and Soft Post Package Repair (sPPR) for a temporary Row repair.
P286-003 §4.29 DDR5 also optionally supports MBIST PPR (mPPR) which is to be used in conjunction with the MBIST feature to automatically repair failling addresses based on the results of MBIST.
P286-004 §4.29 Entry into hPPR, sPPR, MBIST and mPPR is protected through a sequential MRW guard key to prevent unintentional PPR programming.
P286-005 §4.29 The sequential MRW guard key is the same for hPPR, sPPR, MBIST and mPPR.
P286-006 §4.29 The hPPR/sPPR/MBIST/mPPR guard key requires a sequence of four MRW commands to be issued immediately after entering hPPR/sPPR/MBIST/mPPR, as shown in Figure 135.
P286-007 §4.29 The guard key sequence must be entered in the specified order as stated and shown in the spec below and in Table 136.
P286-008 §4.29 Any interruptions of the guard key sequence from other MRW/R commands or non-MR commands such as ACT, WR, RD is not allowed.
P286-009 §4.29 Although interruption of the guard key entry is not allowed, if the guard key is not entered in the required order or is interrupted by other commands, the hPPR/sPPR/MBIST/mPPR mode will not execute and the offending command terminated the hPPR/sPPR/MBIST/mPPR entry may or may not execute correctly however the offending command will not cause the DRAM to lock up.
P286-010 §4.29 Offending commands which will interrupt hPPR/sPPR/MBIST/mPPR include: - Any interruptions of the guard key sequence from other MRW/R commands or non-MR commands - MRW with CW = high - 2 cycle commands with CS_n low on the 2nd cycle Additionally, when the hPPR/sPPR/MBIST/mPPR entry sequence is interrupted, subsequent ACT and WR commands will be conducted as normal DRAM commands.
P286-011 §4.29 If a hPPR/sPPR/MBIST/mPPR operation was prematurely interrupted and/or terminated, the MR23 OP[4:0] must be reset to "0" prior to performing another hPPR/sPPR/MBIST/mPPR operation.
P286-012 §4.29 The DRAM does not provide an error indication if an incorrect hPPR/sPPR/MBIST/mPPR guard key sequence is entered.
P286-021 §4.29 ACT is not required for MBIST entry
P286-022 §4.29 Any interruptions of the guard key sequence from other MRW/R commands or non-MR commands such as ACT, WR, RD is not allowed.
P287-008 §4.29 NOTE 1 Repair region is defined as the address space for which a single repair can be used.
P287-009 §4.29 A repair region is either a BG or bank, depending on vendor implementation.
P287-010 §4.29.1 With hPPR, DDR5 can correct at least one row address per Bank Group.
P287-011 §4.29.1 The hPPR resource designation (MR54,55,56,57) will indicate the hPPR resource availability, and can be read/checked prior to implementing a repair.
P287-012 §4.29.1 It is important to note that hPPR repairs are permanent; the Electrical-fuse cannot be switched back to un-fused states once it is programmed.
P287-013 §4.29.1 The controller should prevent unintended the hPPR mode entry and repair.
P287-015 §4.29.1 Entry into hPPR is through a register enable, ACT command is used to transmit the bank and row address of the row to be replaced in DRAM.
P287-016 §4.29.1 For the non-binary density, an ACT command with invalid row addresses, both MSB and MSB-1 address bits are “HIGH”, shall not be allowed since it may consume the PPR resource.
P287-017 §4.29.1 After tRCD time, a BL16 WRA command is used to select the individual DRAM through the DQ bits and to transfer the repair address to the DRAM.
P287-018 §4.29.1 After program time, and PRE, the hPPR mode can be exited and normal operation can resume.
P288-001 §4.29.1.1 1. Since the mode register address operand allows the user to execute hPPR resource, MRR of hPPR resource designation (MR 54, 55, 56, 57) needs to be read.
P288-002 §4.29.1.1 After user’s checking the hPPR resource availability of each bank from MRR, hPPR mode can be entered.
P288-003 §4.29.1.1 If the MRR of hPPR resource designation (MR 54, 55, 56, 57) appears to not be available, the host controller should not issue hPPR mode.
P288-004 §4.29.1.1 2. Before entering 'hPPR' mode, all banks shall be in a precharged and idle state, and CRC Mode must be disabled.
P288-007 §4.29.1.1 Each MRW command should be spaced by tMRD.
P288-009 §4.29.1.1 Write data is used to select the individual DRAM in the rank for repair.
P288-010 §4.29.1.1 For non 3DS DRAMs the CID bits need only be Valid, and are ignored by the DRAM.
P288-012 §4.29.1.1 The DRAM will ignore the address given with the WRA command.
P288-013 §4.29.1.1 7. After WL(WL=RL-2), DQ{3:0] of target DRAM must be LOW for 8tCK.
P288-014 §4.29.1.1 If HIGH is driven to DQ[3:0] of a DRAM for 8tCK, then DRAM does not conduct hPPR and retains data if REF/REFsb command is properly issued.
P288-015 §4.29.1.1 If more than one DRAM shares the same command bus, DRAMs that are not being repaired must have DQ[3:0] driven HIGH for 8tCK.
P288-016 §4.29.1.1 If all of the DQ[3:0] data bits are neither all LOW nor all HIGH for 8tCK, then hPPR mode execution is unknown.
P288-017 §4.29.1.1 For x8 and x16 devices, data bits other than DQ[3:0] are don’t cares.
P288-018 §4.29.1.1 Note that a previous versions of the spec required ALL DQs to be high or low, but this was changed to DQ[3:0] to accomodate ECC UDIMMs and SoDIMMs.
P288-020 §4.29.1.1 8. Wait tPGM_hPPRa or tPGM_hPPRb to allow DRAM repair at the target row address to occur internally, and then issue PRE command.
P288-021 §4.29.1.1 9. Wait tPGM_Exit(min) after PRE command to allow DRAM to recognize repaired row address.
P288-023 §4.29.1.1 11. DDR5 will accept any valid command after tPGMPST(min).
P288-024 §4.29.1.1 12. In the case of multiple addresses to be repaired, repeat Steps 3 to 10.
P288-025 §4.29.1.1 13. For a 3DS device, the target die for the hPPR is selected by the CID[3:0] bits in the ACT, WRA and PRE commands.
P288-026 §4.29.1.1 The CID bits must match in all three commands.
P288-027 §4.29.1.1 During hPPR mode, REF, REFsb commands are allowed, but array contents are not guaranteed.
P288-028 §4.29.1.1 Upon receiving a REF or REFsb command in hPPR mode, the DRAM may ignore the Refresh operation but will not disrupt the repair operation.
P288-029 §4.29.1.1 Other commands except REF/REFsb during tPGM can cause incomplete repair so no other command except REF is allowed during tPGM.
P288-030 §4.29.1.1 Once hPPR mode is exited with MR23:OP[0]=0 and tPGMPST, to confirm if target row is repaired correctly, host can verify by writing data into the target row and reading it back.
P289-044 §4.29.1.1 NOTE 1 Allow REFab/REFsb(1X) from WL+8tCK+tWR+tRP+1tCK after WR, but does not guarantee array contents are refreshed during hPPR
P289-045 §4.29.1.1 NOTE 2 Timing diagram shows possible commands but not all shown can be issued at same time; for example if REF is issued at Te1, DES must be issued at Te2 as REF would be illegal at Te2.
P289-046 §4.29.1.1 Likewise, DES must be issued tRFC prior to PRE at Tf0.
P289-047 §4.29.1.1 All regular timings must still be satisfied.
P289-048 §4.29.1.2 Repair requires additional time period to repair Fail Row Address into Spare Row Address and the following timing parameters are required for PPR.
P290-001 §4.29.2 Soft Post Package Repair (sPPR) is a way to quickly, but temporarily, repair one row address per Bank Group on a DDR5 DRAM device, contrasted to hPPR which takes longer but is permanent repair of a row address.
P290-002 §4.29.2 There are some limitations and differences between Soft Repair and a Hard Repair.
P290-003 §4.29.2 Entry into sPPR is through a register enable, ACT command is used to transmit the bank and row address of the row to be replaced in DRAM.
P290-004 §4.29.2 For the non-binary density, an ACT command with invalid row addresses, both MSB and MSB-1 address bits are “HIGH”, shall not be allowed since it may consume the PPR resource.
P290-005 §4.29.2 After tRCD time, a BL16 WR command is used to select the individual DRAM through the DQ bits and to transfer the repair address into an internal register in the DRAM.
P290-006 §4.29.2 After a write recovery time and PRE, the sPPR mode can be exited and normal operation can resume.
P290-007 §4.29.2 Care must be taken that refresh is not violated for the other rows in the array during soft repair time.
P290-008 §4.29.2 Also note that the DRAM will retain the soft repair information inside the DRAM as long as VDD remains within the operating region.
P290-009 §4.29.2 If DRAM power is removed or the DRAM is RESET, the soft repair will revert to the un-repaired state.
P290-010 §4.29.2 hPPR and sPPR may not be enabled at the same time.
P290-011 §4.29.2 sPPR must have been disabled, cleared and unlocked (if DRAM supports optional sPPR undo/lock) prior to entering hPP,R, MBIST or mPPR modes.
P290-012 §4.29.2 With sPPR, DDR5 can repair one Row address per BG (Default) or one row address per bank (Optional).
P290-013 §4.29.2 If the hPPR resources for a BG are used up, the bank group will have no more available resources for soft PPR.
P290-014 §4.29.2 If a repair sequence is issued to a BG with no repair resources available, the DRAM will ignore the programming sequence.
P290-015 §4.29.2 Note that MR54 through MR57 are NOT updated by an sPPR.
P290-016 §4.29.2 The host controller must remember which sPPR resources have been used since the last DRAM Reset.
P290-017 §4.29.2 The DRAM device may optionally support the sPPR Undo and sPPR Lock functions.
P290-018 §4.29.2 The sPPR undo command will undo a previous sPPR and cause the sPPR resource to be taken back to its unused condition.
P290-019 §4.29.2 The original row will appear back in the memory map at its original location following an sPPR undo.
P290-020 §4.29.2 The sPPR Lock function will lock the specific sPPR resource at its current location and not allow another sPPR or an sPPR undo to be performed to that resource.
P290-021 §4.29.2 A row that has been replaced by a spare row need not be refreshed by the DRAM.
P290-022 §4.29.2 Likewise a spare row that is not in the memory map, either from never being in the memory map, or from an sPPR undo need not be refreshed by the DRAM.
P290-023 §4.29.2 If moving a spare row in and out of the memory map, the host controller is responsible for sending enough Activate commands to any mapped out row to assure any required data retention.
P290-024 §4.29.2 The host controller is also responsible for any data copy operations between the original row and spare row.
P290-025 §4.29.2 The host controller should read MR23 OP2 to determine whether the sPPR undo and sPPR lock functions are supported.
P290-026 §4.29.2 0=unsupported, 1=supported.The two features are supported together.
P290-027 §4.29.2.1 The following is the procedure of sPPR with WR command.
P290-028 §4.29.2.1 Note that during the soft repair sequence, no refresh is allowed.
P290-029 §4.29.2.1 1. User should back up the data of the target row address for sPPR in the bank before sPPR execution.
P290-030 §4.29.2.1 The backup data should be one row per bank.
P290-031 §4.29.2.1 After sPPR has been completed, user restores the data in the repaired array.
P290-033 §4.29.2.1 sPPR resources are shared with hPPR.
P290-034 §4.29.2.1 The hPPR resource designation registers (MR 54, 55, 56, 57) should be checked prior to sPPR.
P290-035 §4.29.2.1 If the MRR of hPPR resource designation (MR 54, 55, 56, 57) shows that hPPR resources in the bank that is targeted for sPPR repair is not available, the host controller should not issue sPPR mode.
P290-036 §4.29.2.1 3. Before entering 'sPPR' mode, all banks shall be in a precharged and idle state.
P290-037 §4.29.2.1 and CRC mode must be disabled.
P290-039 §4.29.2.1 5. Issue Guard Key as four consecutive MRW commands each with a unique address field OP[7:0] Each MRW command shall be separated by tMRD.
P290-040 §4.29.2.1 The Guard Key sequence is the same as hPPR in Table 136.
P290-041 §4.29.2.1 6. Issue ACT command with the CID bits, Bank Group, Bank and Row Fail address, Write data is used to select the individual DRAM in the Rank for repair.
P290-042 §4.29.2.1 For non-3DS DRAMs, the CID bits need only be Valid, and are ignored by the DRAM.
P290-043 §4.29.2.1 7. A WR command is issued after tRCD, with valid column address.
P290-044 §4.29.2.1 The DRAM will ignore the column address given with the WR command.
P290-045 §4.29.2.1 8. After WL (WL=WL=RL-2), DQ[3:0] of the individual Target DRAM must be LOW for 8tCK.
P290-046 §4.29.2.1 If more than one DRAM shares the same command bus, DRAMs that are not being repaired must have DQ[3:0] driven HIGH for 8tCK.
P290-047 §4.29.2.1 If all of the DQ[3:0] data bits are neither all LOW nor all HIGH for 8tCK, then sPPR mode will be unknown.
P290-048 §4.29.2.1 For x8 and x16 devices, data bits other than DQ[3:0] are don’t cares.
P290-049 §4.29.2.1 Note that a previous versions of the spec required ALL DQs to be high or low, but this was changed to DQ[3:0] to accomodate ECC UDIMMs and SoDIMMs.
P290-051 §4.29.2.1 9. Wait tPGM_sPPR(min) for the internal repair register to be written and then issue PRE to the Bank.
P290-052 §4.29.2.1 10. Wait tRP after PRE command to allow the DRAM to recognize repaired Row address.
P291-001 §4.29.2.1 12. sPPR can be performed without affecting the hPPR previously performed provided a row is available in that repair region.
P291-002 §4.29.2.1 When more than one sPPR request is made to the same repair region, the most recently issued sPPR address would replace the early issued one associcated with given bank and row addresses.
P291-004 §4.29.2.1 During a soft repair, refresh command is not allowed between sPPR MRS entry and exit.
P291-005 §4.29.2.1 For a 3DS device, the target die for the sPPR is selected by the CID[3:0] bits in the ACT, WR, and PRE commands.
P291-006 §4.29.2.1 The CID bits must match in all three commands.
P292-001 §4.29.2.2 sPPR Undo is a method of setting the sPPR resource back to its unused state, as it was following Reset.
P292-002 §4.29.2.2 It follows the exact same protocol as the sPPR sequence, but with MR23 OP[2:1] set to 10.
P292-003 §4.29.2.2 The host controller must send the same CID bits, BG bits, Bank bits, and row address in the Activate command as it did for the most recent sPPR for this resource.
P292-004 §4.29.2.2 The DRAM may ignore the bank and row address bits if it so chooses, as the CID and BG bits may be enough to uniquely identify the sPPR resource depending on number of repair elements.
P292-005 §4.29.2.2 For non 3DS DRAMs the CID bits need only be Valid, and are ignored by the DRAM.
P292-006 §4.29.2.2 If the Bank and/or row address does NOT match that of the most recent sPPR to this resource, the DRAM may or may not perform the undo.
P292-007 §4.29.2.2 Any required copying of data is the host controller's responsibility.
P292-008 §4.29.2.2 Following an sPPR Undo, a later sPPR may be done to assign the resource to a new or the same location.
P292-009 §4.29.2.2 Data is retained in the sPPR resource, but it need not be refreshed by the DRAM.
P292-010 §4.29.2.2 If the host controller requires the data to remain valid, it must send enough ACT commands to the row while it is mapped in to guarantee the data.
P292-011 §4.29.2.2 This feature is optional.
P292-012 §4.29.2.2 The sPPR undo function should only be done to DRAMs in which MR23 bit 2 is read as a 1 upon an MRR.
P292-013 §4.29.2.3 sPPR Lock allows an sPPR resource to be locked in place.
P292-014 §4.29.2.3 Locks are done to sPPR resources individually.
P292-015 §4.29.2.3 Following an sPPR Lock, any sPPR or sPPR undo is blocked to that spare resource.
P292-016 §4.29.2.3 A hardware reset or power cycle must be done to undo the lock.
P292-017 §4.29.2.3 The hardware reset or power cycle must also be done before any hPPR operation can be done if any sPPR resources are locked.
P292-018 §4.29.2.3 The sPPR Lock uses the same protocol as the sPPR function except that MR23 OP[2:1] is set to 11.
P292-019 §4.29.2.3 The Activate command must contain the CID bits, BG bits, bank bits and row address of the most recent sPPR for that resource.
P292-020 §4.29.2.3 The DRAM may ignore the bank and row address bits if it so chooses, as the CID and BG bits may be enough to uniquely identify the sPPR resource depending on number of repair elements.
P292-021 §4.29.2.3 For non 3DS DRAMs the CID bits need only be Valid, and are ignored by the DRAM.
P292-022 §4.29.2.3 If the Bank and/or row address does NOT match that of the most recent sPPR to this resource, the DRAM may or may not perform the lock.
P292-023 §4.29.2.3 This feature is optional.
P292-024 §4.29.2.3 The sPPR lock function should only be done to DRAMs in which MR23 bit 2 is read as a 1 upon an MRR.
P293-001 §4.30 DDR5 devices can support optional Memory Built-In Self Test (MBIST) and Memory Built-In Self Test-Post Package Repair (mPPR) to help with hard failures such as single-bit or multi-bit failures in a single device so that weak cells can be scanned and repaired during the initialization phase.
P293-002 §4.30 There are two distinct associated phases, MBIST (Self-test phase) and mPPR (Self-repair phase).
P293-003 §4.30 During MBIST, the DRAM will use vendor specific patterns to test the memory array, detect hard failures.
P293-004 §4.30 During mPPR, addresses of hard-failures will be automatically repaired out.
P293-005 §4.30 MBIST and mPPR may only be entered from the All Banks Idle state.
P293-006 §4.30 MBIST may be ran any time after the device has been properly initialized according to Ch.
P293-007 §4.30 3.3.1 “Power-up Initialization Sequence”, but must be run prior to mPPR.
P293-008 §4.30 After MBIST completes, MR22 transparency must be read.
P293-009 §4.30 If transparency says that fails remain and the controller chooses to run mPPR, it must perform mPPR immediately after the DRAM transparency is read.
P293-010 §4.30 mPPR resources are separated from normal hPPR/sPPR resources.
P293-011 §4.30 mPPR resources will be used for initial scan and repair, and hPPR/sPPR resources still must satisfy the number of repair elements as specified in Table 137.
P293-012 §4.30 Once MBIST is done, DRAM will update the MBIST/mPPR transparency status in MR22:OP[2:0].
P293-013 §4.30 Detailed transparency status is described in Clause 3.5.24
P293-014 §4.30 There are two timings associated with MBIST/mPPR, tSELFTEST and tSELFREPAIR.
P293-015 §4.30 The time to test the array and detect failures is defined as tSELFTEST.
P293-016 §4.30 The time to repair failures detected in the previous MBIST run using mPPR is defined as tSELFREPAIR.
P293-017 §4.30 Multiple iterations of MBIST and mPPR may be required to repair all failures, and the transparency status will inform the host of this.
P293-018 §4.30 For 3DS devices, MBIST must be run on each logical rank in the 3DS package independently by configuring MR14:OP[3:0] prior to invoking MBIST.
P293-019 §4.30 After MBIST is run on a single logical rank, MBIST/mPPR transparency in MR22 must subsequently be read to determine whether mPPR is needed on that logical rank.
P293-020 §4.30 The controller may choose to run MBIST on all ranks before performing mPPR on all ranks sequentially, or it may perform MBIST on one rank followed by mPPR to the same rank, proceeding through each logical rank.
P293-021 §4.30.1 The controller is required to issue an MRW command to enter the MBIST operation.
P293-023 §4.30.1 Once the MBIST is completed, the DRAM drives ALERT_n to HIGH to notify the controller that this operation is completed.
P293-024 §4.30.1 MBIST/mPPR transparency will be updated in MR22, and will signify to the host whether mPPR must be performed to repair any found fails.
P293-025 §4.30.1 If mPPR is required, the controller will follow the mPPR sequence described in Clause 4.30.2, and transparency will be updated once mPPR is complete.
P293-026 §4.30.1 DRAM data will not be guaranteed after MBIST PPR operation.
P293-027 §4.30.1 During MBIST mode, only DESELECT command is allowed.
P293-028 §4.30.1 The DQ/DQS may either float (Hi-Z) or perform RTT_PARK/DQS_RTT_PARK termination during tSELFTEST depending on vendor specific implementation, while CA/CS/CK ODT will remain unchanged from its programmed state prior to MBIST.
P293-032 §4.30.1 NOTE 1 tSELFTEST applies per logical-rank.
P293-033 §4.30.1 MBIST procedure is detailed in Figure 138.
P294-001 §4.30.2 MBIST-PPR (mPPR) may be used after MBIST in order to repair failures found during the self-test phase.
P294-002 §4.30.2 mPPR may only be performed after MR22 MBIST/mPPR transparency is read, and MR22 MBIST/mPPR transparency must be read after mPPR completes in order to determine if an additional mPPR or MBIST is required.
P294-003 §4.30.2 If fails remain after the read of MR22 transparency, the mPPR sequence is described as follows:
P294-004 §4.30.2 1. All banks shall be in a precharged and idle state, and CRC mode must be disabled prior to entering mPPR mode.
P294-007 §4.30.2 Each MRW command must be spaced by tMRD.
P294-009 §4.30.2 The DRAM will ignore the address given with the ACT command.
P294-010 §4.30.2 Write data is used to select the individual DRAM in the rank for repair.
P294-011 §4.30.2 For 3DS devices, CID on the ACT must be set to the same value programmed in MR14:OP[3:0] which determined which logical rank MBIST was last run on.
P294-013 §4.30.2 The DRAM will ignore the address given with the WRA command.
P294-014 §4.30.2 For 3DS devices, CID on the WRA must be set to the same value programmed in MR14:OP[3:0] which determined which logical rank MBIST was last run on.
P294-015 §4.30.2 6. After WL (WL=RL-2), DQ[3:0] of the individual target DRAM must be LOW for 8tCK.
P294-016 §4.30.2 Only DRAMs in which most recently have had an MR22 transparency result of 001B may be considered “target DRAMs”.
P294-017 §4.30.2 If more than one DRAM shares the same command bus, DRAMs that are not being repaired must have DQ[3:0] driven HIGH for 8tCK.
P294-018 §4.30.2 If all of the DQ[3:0] data bits are neither all LOW or all HIGH for 8tCK, the mPPR mode execution is unknown.
P294-019 §4.30.2 For x8 and x16 devices, data bits other than DQ[3:0] are don’t cares.
P294-020 §4.30.2 Note that a previous versions of the spec required ALL DQs to be high or low, but this was changed to DQ[3:0] to accommodate ECC UDIMMs and SoDIMMs.
P294-022 §4.30.2 7. Wait tSELFREPAIR to allow DRAM to self repair the address(es) identified internally by MBIST, and then issue a PREab command.
P294-023 §4.30.2 8. Wait tPGM_exit after PREab command to allow DRAM to update MR22 transparency status.
P294-026 §4.30.2 10. Read MR22 transparency status.
P294-027 §4.30.2 11. If additional fails remain, the controller may repeat steps 2-10.
P294-028 §4.30.2 If no fails remain or the controller chooses not to perform additional mPPR, the DRAM may continue to the next planned operation.
P295-046 §4.30.2 Figure 140 contains a detailed flow chart of the MBIST/mPPR procedure.
P295-066 §4.30.2 011B (MBIST should be run again) 001B
P295-073 §4.30.2 NOTE 1 mPPR may only be performed after an MR22 transparency result of 001B.
P295-074 §4.30.2 NOTE 2 011B = “MBIST should be run again” indicates that some fails were repaired with mPPR, but MBIST should be run again to load internal fail addresses for mPPR.
P295-075 §4.30.2 NOTE 3 011B = “Unrepairable fails remain” indicates that there are not enough mPPR elements remaining to repair fail addresses latched during MBIST.
P295-076 §4.30.2 It may be updated in MR22 after either MBIST or mPPR, depending on vendor implementation.
P296-001 §4.31.1 At data rates >= 2933 MT/s, signal degradation due to Inter Symbol Interference (ISI) is expected to increase and the data eye at the DRAM ball is expected to be closed.
P296-002 §4.31.1 Since the memory channel is very reflective due to the many impedance mismatched points that exist along the memory subsystem, ISI due to reflections are expected to increase.
P296-003 §4.31.1 Traditional methods of characterizing the Receiver using the input eye mask is no longer sufficient.
P296-004 §4.31.1 DDR5 requires equalization to help improve (or open up) the data eyes after the data is latched by the receiver.
P296-005 §4.31.1 A 4-tap DFE is chosen to help equalize the DQ signals without amplifying the noise due to insertion loss and reflections, which is a common side effect of other equalization techniques (example CTLE).
P296-006 §4.31.1 Figure 141 shows an example of a memory subsystem with DFE circuit included on the DRAM.
P296-007 §4.31.1 At the 1980-2100MT/s data rates, the DRAM’s DFE shall be disabled.
P296-008 §4.31.1 Setting either the Global DFE Gain and Tap 1-4 Enable bits to “Disable” or setting all DFE Gain and Tap 1-4 Bias bits to “Step 0” will disable the DFE.
P296-018 §4.31.2 A reflection dominated channel such as those found in a memory subsystem is anticipated to have substantially reduced data eye at the DRAM ball due to the effects of insertion loss and reflections.
P296-019 §4.31.2 Figure represents how a pulse response of a very reflective channel might look like.
P296-020 §4.31.2 The attenuation as well as the ringing of the signal can cause the data eye to close at the DRAM ball.
P296-021 §4.31.2 Moreover, the ringing can impact future bits that are being sent into the channel, i.e., if the pulse response is for bit n, then the ringing from bit n can impact the signal integrity of future bits n+1, n+2, n+3, n+4, etc. Putting it another way, the signal integrity of any bit, for example bit n, can be impacted by the signals of the previous bits n-1, n-2, n-3, n-4, etc.
P297-001 §4.31.3 The 4-tap DFE subsystem consists of a gain amplifier, a DFE summer, 4 DQ slicers (also called Taps) with outputs that loop back to the DFE summer, and a coefficient multiplier for each Tap (Figure 143).
P297-002 §4.31.3 The gain control of the front end is used to ensure that the cursor or the current bit is in a congruent relationship with the ISI correction required for the channel.
P297-003 §4.31.3 The taps T1, T2, T3, T4 coefficients provide the corrections needed to the current bit by adding or subtracting the effects of ISI of the previous bits.
P297-030 §4.31.3 The Mode Registers shown in Table 142 and Table 143 are used by the memory controller to set the strengths of the gain amplifier and the strengths of the correction of the Taps to adapt the ISI cancellation in accordance with the channel performance.
P297-031 §4.31.3 Optimal values used for the strengths of the gain amplifier and of the Taps are system dependent, and are usually obtained through a combination of simulations, platform characterizations, and other methods.
P297-040 §4.31.3 NOTE 1 All parameters are defined over the Vref ranges from 0.5*VDDQ to 0.9*VDDQ
P297-041 §4.31.3 NOTE 2 DFE Gain Bias are for all voltage and temperature range
P297-042 §4.31.3 NOTE 3 These values are defined over the entire voltage and temperature range
P297-043 §4.31.3 NOTE 4 These parameters are suggested to evaluate relative ratio of DFE gain bias settings, and absolute values of all parameters are not subject to silicon validation nor production test.
P298-016 §4.31.3 NOTE 2 Values are defined for the entire voltage, temperature and the Rx Vref range from 0.5*VDDQ to 0.9*VDDQ.
P298-017 §4.31.3 NOTE 3 Values are identical for Taps 1-4.
P298-018 §4.31.3 NOTE 4 These parameters are suggested to evaluate relative ratio of DFE Taps 1~4, and absolute values of all parameters are not subject to silicon validation nor production test.
P298-019 §4.31.3 NOTE 5 For the pulse response shown in Fig 118 (...000010000...
P298-020 §4.31.3 pulse pattern), a positive value corrects a negative post-cursor by setting the DFE tab bias sign bit (MR113~116, OP[6]) to ‘0’ to apply a positive correction.
P298-021 §4.31.3 NOTE 6 For the pulse response shown in Fig 118 (...000010000...
P298-022 §4.31.3 pulse pattern), a negative value corrects a positive post-cursor by setting the DFE tab bias sign bit (MR113~116, OP[6]) to ‘1’ to apply a negative correction.
P298-023 §4.31.3 For example, in a memory channel where the ISIduring the first post-cursor is dominated by bandwidth loss, the expected tap-1 bias sign bit will be set to ‘1’.
P298-024 §4.31.3 NOTE 7 Users refer to DRAM supplier’s datasheet to check the multiplier which is applied to the DFE Tap Bias setting (MR113, MR114, MR115, etc.) for total DFE feedback swing implemented in hardware.
P299-001 §4.31.3 The method to measure INL and DNL for both DFE gain and DFE taps will be the subject of future ballots.
P300-001 §4.31.3 The DRAM may implement 1-way interleave, 2-way interleave, or 4-way interleave 4-tap DFE memory circuitry.
P300-002 §4.31.3 The 1-way interleaved 4-tap DFE architecture (Figure 145) requires a strobe multiplier, which is at Nyquist rate, and the output of the DQ slicer runs at same speed as received data.
P300-029 §4.31.3 A 2-way interleaved 4-tap DFE architecture (Figure 146) can use the strobe as is.
P301-001 §4.31.3 A 4-way interleaved 4-tap DFE architecture (Figure 147) requires a divided clock.
P301-005 §4.31.3 See Mode Registers for information on Gain Adjustment and Taps 1-4 Bias Programming
P302-001 §4.32 As voltage and temperature change on the SDRAM die, the DQS clock tree delay will shift and may require re-training.
P302-002 §4.32 The DDR5- SDRAM includes an internal DQS clock-tree oscillator to measure the amount of delay over a given time interval (determined by the controller), allowing the controller to compare the trained delay value to the delay value seen at a later time.
P302-003 §4.32 The DQS Oscillator will provide the controller with important information regarding the need to re-train, and the magnitude of potential error.
P302-004 §4.32 The DQS Interval Oscillator is started by issuing an MPC [Start DQS Osc] command with OP[0000 0111B] set as described in the MPC Operation section, which will start an internal ring oscillator that counts the number of times a signal propagates through a copy of the DQS clock tree.
P302-005 §4.32 The DQS Oscillator may be stopped by issuing an MPC [Stop DQS Osc] command with OP[0000 0110B] set as described in the MPC Operation section, or the controller may instruct the SDRAM to count for a specific number of clocks and then stop automatically (See MR45,46, and 47 for more information).
P302-006 §4.32 If MR45 is set to automatically stop the DQS Oscillator, then the MPC [Stop DQS Osc] command shall not be used.
P302-007 §4.32 When the DQS Oscillator is stopped by either method, the result of the oscillator counter is automatically stored in MR46 and MR47 after the oscillator is stopped and within tOSCOA for the automatic mode and tOSCOM for the manual mode.
P302-008 §4.32 MRW commands to MR45 during an ongoing DQS Interval Oscillator operation are not permitted.
P302-009 §4.32 MR45 may be reprogrammed tMRD after the oscillator is stopped by the automatic stop.
P302-010 §4.32 The DRAM shall respond in one of two ways if an MPC Start DQS Osc is issued during an ongoing DQS Interval Oscillator operation (automatic or manual).
P302-011 §4.32 One option is the DRAM will ignore the concurrent start command, in which case the DQS Interval Oscillator operation will proceed as normal with all timing constraints referencing the original start command.
P302-012 §4.32 The other option is for the DRAM to restart the DQS Interval Oscillator operation, in which case all timing constraints will reference the most recent (subsequent) start command.
P302-013 §4.32 The host must account for the worst-case option in the event that a concurrent start command is issued.
P302-014 §4.32 If issuing a concurrent MPC [Start DQS Osc] results in a loss of run time tracking, the results stored in MR46 and MR47 should be ignored and the full operation restarted.
P302-015 §4.32 Entering Self Refresh during an ongoing DQS Interval Oscillator operation is permitted.
P302-016 §4.32 Upon exiting Self Refresh, an operation started in automatic mode shall be allowed to complete naturally based upon the specified number of clocks (cumulative before and after Self Refresh).
P302-017 §4.32 If a manual operation started prior to entering Self Refresh is not stopped prior as well, the operation shall be manually stopped upon Self Refresh exit.
P302-018 §4.32 The results stored in MR46 and MR47 for a DQS Interval Oscillator operation that spans Self Refresh entry/exit should be ignored and the full operation restarted.
P302-019 §4.32 The controller may adjust the accuracy of the result by running the DQS Interval Oscillator for shorter (less accurate) or longer (more accurate) duration.

P303-008 §4.32 The result of the DQS Interval Oscillator is defined as the number of DQS Clock Tree Delays that can be counted within the “run time,” determined by the controller.
P303-009 §4.32 The result is stored in MR46 and MR47.
P303-010 §4.32 MR46 contains the least significant bits (LSB) of the result, and MR47 contains the most significant bits (MSB) of the result.
P303-011 §4.32 MR46 and MR47 are overwritten by the SDRAM when an MPC-1 [Stop DQS Osc] command is received.
P303-012 §4.32 The SDRAM counter will count to its maximum value (=2^16) and stop.
P303-013 §4.32 If the maximum value is read from the mode registers, then the memory controller must assume that the counter overflowed the register and discard the result.

P303-015 §4.32 The interval oscillator matching error is defined as the difference between the DQS training circuit (interval oscillator) and the actual DQS clock tree across voltage and temperature.
P305-004 §4.32 The OSCMatch is the matching error per between the actual DQS and DQS interval oscillator over voltage and temp.
P305-006 §4.32 NOTE 2 This parameter will be characterized or guaranteed by design.

P305-008 §4.32 NOTE 4 The runtime of the oscillator must be at least 200ns for determining tDQSOSC(V,T) Runtime tDQSOSC(V,T) = 2 * Count

P305-011 §4.32 NOTE 7 These parameters are defined per channel.
P305-012 §4.32 NOTE 8 tRX_DQS2DQ(V,T) delay will be the average of DQS to DQ delay over the runtime period.

P307-001 §4.33 As temperature and voltage change on the SDRAM die, the DQS clock tree will shift and may require retraining.
P307-002 §4.33 The oscillator is usually used to measure the amount of delay over a given time interval (determined by the controller), allowing the controller to compare the trained delay value to the delay value seen at a later time.
P307-003 §4.33 The tRX_DQS2DQ offset due to temperature and voltage variation specification can be used for instances when the oscillator cannot be used to control the tRX_DQS2DQ.
P307-010 §4.33 It includes the VDDQ and VDD AC noise impact for frequencies >20MHz and max voltage of 45mVpk-pk from DC -20MHz at a fixed temperature on the package.
