P361-001 §5.4 The DDR5 DRAM includes ODT (On-Die Termination) termination resistance for CK_t, CK_c, CS and CA signals.
P361-002 §5.4 The ODT feature is designed to improve signal integrity of the memory channel by allowing the DRAM controller to turn on and off termination resistance for any target DRAM devices via MR setting.
P361-009 §5.4 The ODT termination resistance during power up will be set to the default values based on MR32 and MR33.
P361-010 §5.4 The ODT resistance values can be configured by those same registers.
P361-011 §5.4 On-Die Termination effective resistance RTT is define by MRS bits.
P361-012 §5.4 ODT is applied to CK_t, CK_c, CS and CA pins
P362-001 §5.4.1 On-die termination effective Rtt values supported are 480, 240,120, 80, 60, and 40 ohms
P362-022 §5.4.1 NOTE 1 The tolerance limits are specified after calibration with stable voltage and temperature.

P362-024 §5.4.1 NOTE 2 Pull-up ODT resistors are recommended to be calibrated at 0.8*VDDQ.
P362-025 §5.4.1 Other calibration schemes may be used to achieve the linearity spec shown above, e.g. calibration at 0.5*VDDQ and 0.95*VDDQ.
P362-028 §5.4.1 NOTE 5 Without ZQ calibration ODT effective RTT values have an increased tolerance of +/- 30%
P362-029 §5.4.1 NOTE 6 CK ODT RZQ/2 (120), CS ODT RZQ/2 (120), and CA ODT RZQ/2 (120) are only supported at >5200 MT/s data rates.

P369-002 §7.3.1 The times at which the differential crossing points of the clock occur are defined at t1, t2, …, tn-1, tn,..., tK.
P369-003 §7.3.1 The UI at index “n” is defined as shown in Figure 205 (with n=1,2,…) from an arbitrary time in steady state, where n=0 is chosen as the starting crossing point.
P369-004 §7.3.1 Mathematical definition of UI is shown in Figure 205 and Figure 206.
P369-007 §7.3.1 For the differential clock, the unit interval time starts when the CK_t and CK_c intersect (see Figure 206).
P369-011 §7.3.2 If a number of UI edges are computed or measured at times t1, t2, …, tn-1, tn,..., tK, where K is the maximum number of samples, then the UI jitter at any instance “n” is defined in Figure 207, where T = the ideal UI size.
P369-013 §7.3.2 In a large sample with random Gaussian-like jitter (therefore very close to symmetric distribution), the average of all UI sizes usually turns out to be very close to the ideal UI size.
P369-014 §7.3.2 The equation described in Figure 207 assumes starting from an instant steady state, where n=0 is chosen as the starting point.
P369-015 §7.3.2 1 UI = one bit, which means 2 UI = one full cycle or time period of the forwarded strobe.
P369-016 §7.3.2 Example: For 6.4 GT/s signaling, the forwarded strobe frequency is 3.2 GHz, or 1 UI = 156.25 ps.
P370-001 §7.3.2 Deterministic jitter is analyzed in terms of the peak-to-peak value and in terms of specific frequency components present in the jitter, isolating the causes for each frequency.
P370-002 §7.3.2 Random jitter is unbounded and analyzed in terms of statistical distribution to convert to a bit error rate (BER) for the link.
P370-003 §7.3.3 UI-UI (read as “UI to UI”) jitter is defined to be the jitter between two consecutive UI as shown in Figure 208.
P370-005 §7.3.4 Accumulated jitter is defined as the jitter accumulated over any consecutive “N” UI as shown in Figure 209.
P370-010 §7.3.4 where UI is defined in the equation shown in Figure 210.

P375-001 §8.3.1 The clock is being driven to the DRAM either by the RCD for L/RDIMM modules, or by the host for U/SODIMM modules (Figure 216).
P376-001 §8.3.2 The Random Jitter (Rj) specified is a random jitter meeting a Gaussian distribution.
P376-002 §8.3.2 The Deterministic Jitter (Dj) specified is bounded.
P376-003 §8.3.2 Input clock violating the min/max jitter values may result in malfunction of the DDR5 SDRAM device.
P376-018 §8.3.2 NOTE 1 f0 = Data Rate/2, example: if data rate is 3200MT/s, then f0=1600
P376-019 §8.3.2 NOTE 2 Rise and fall time slopes (V / nsec) are measured between +100 mV and -100 mV of the differential output of reference clock
P376-020 §8.3.2 NOTE 3 On-die noise similar to that occurring with all the transmitter and receiver lanes toggling need to be stimulated.
P376-021 §8.3.2 When there is no socket in transmitter measurement setup, in many cases, the contribution of the cross-talk is not significant or can be estimated within tolerable error even with all the transmitter lanes sending patterns.
P376-022 §8.3.2 When a socket is present, such as DUT being DRAM component, the contribution of the cross-talk could be significant.
P376-023 §8.3.2 To minimize the impact of crosstalk on the measurement results, a small group of selected lanes in the vicinity of the lane under test may be turned off (sending DC), while the remaining Tx lanes send patterns to the corresponding Rx receivers so as to excite realistic on-die noise profile from device switching.
P376-024 §8.3.2 Note that there may be cases when one of Dj and Rj specs is met and another violated in which case the signaling analysis should be run to determine link feasibility
P376-025 §8.3.2 NOTE 4 Duty Cycle Error defined as absolute difference between average value of all UI with that of average of odd UI, which in magnitude would equal absolute difference between average of all UI and average of all even UI.

P398-001 §8.11 During CT Mode, input levels are defined below.
P398-005 §8.11 Prior to the assertion of the TEN pin, all voltage supplies must be valid and stable.
P398-006 §8.11 Upon the assertion of the TEN pin, the CK_t and CK_c signals will be ignored and the DDR5 memory device will enter into the CT mode after time tCT_Enable.
P398-007 §8.11 In the CT mode, no refresh activities in the memory arrays, initiated either externally (i.e., auto-refresh) or internally (i.e., self-refresh), will be maintained.
P398-008 §8.11 The TEN pin may be asserted after the DRAM has completed power-on, after RESET_n has de-asserted, the wait time after the RESET_n de-assertion has elapsed, and prior to starting clocks (CK_t, CK_c).
P398-009 §8.11 The TEN pin may be de-asserted at any time in the CT mode.
P398-010 §8.11 Upon exiting the CT mode, the states of the DDR5 memory device are unknown and the integrity of the original content of the memory array is not guaranteed; therefore, the reset initialization sequence is required.
P398-011 §8.11 All output signals at the test output pins will be stable within tCT_valid after the test inputs have been applied to the test input pins with TEN input and CS_n input maintained High and Low respectively.
P399-001 §8.11.1 Following input parameters will be applied for DDR5 SDRAM Input Signals during Connectivity Test Mode.
P399-010 §8.11.1 It should be limited by the Absolute Maximum DC Ratings.
P399-012 §8.11.1 It should be limited by Absolute Maximum DC Ratings.
P400-008 §8.11.2 NOTE 1 After RESET_n is registered LOW, RESET_n level shall be maintained below VIL(DC)_RESET during tPW_RESET, otherwise, SDRAM may not be reset.
P400-009 §8.11.2 NOTE 2 Once RESET_n is registered HIGH, RESET_n level must be maintained above VIH(DC)_RESET, otherwise, SDRAM operation will not be guaranteed until it is reset asserting RESET_n signal LOW.
P400-010 §8.11.2 NOTE 3 RESET is destructive to data contents.
P400-011 §8.11.2 NOTE 4 This definition is applied only for “Reset Procedure at Power Stable”.
P400-013 §8.11.2 It should be limited by the Absolute Maximum DC Ratings.
P400-015 §8.11.2 It should be limited by Absolute Maximum DC Ratings

P412-019 §9.9 NOTE 1 On-die noise similar to that occurring with all the transmitter and receiver lanes toggling need to be stimulated.
P412-020 §9.9 When there is no socket in transmitter measurement setup, in many cases, the contribution of the cross-talk is not significant or can be estimated within tolerable error even with all the transmitter lanes sending patterns.
P412-021 §9.9 When a socket is present, such as DUT being DRAM component, the contribution of the cross-talk could be significant.
P412-022 §9.9 To minimize the impact of crosstalk on the measurement results, a small group of selected lanes in the vicinity of the lane under test may be turned off (sending DC), while the remaining TX lanes send patterns to the corresponding RX receivers so as to excite realistic on-die noise profile from device switching.
P412-023 §9.9 Note that there may be cases when one of Dj and Rj specs is met and another violated in which case the signaling analysis should be run to determine link feasibility

P418-024 §9.10.2 NOTE 6 Vref noise level to DQ jitter should be adjusted to minimize DCD See Clause 7 for details on the minimum BER requirements
P418-025 §9.10.2 NOTE 7 See Clause 7 for details on UI, NUI and Jitter definitions
P418-027 §9.10.2 NOTE 9 Duty Cycle of the DQ pins must be adjusted as close to 50% as possible using the Duty Cycle Adjuster feature prior to running this test
P418-028 §9.10.2 NOTE 10 The Mode Registers for the Duty Cycle Adjuster are MR43 and MR44
P418-029 §9.10.2 NOTE 11 Spread Spectrum Clocking (SSC) must be disabled while running this test
P418-030 §9.10.2 NOTE 12 These parameters are tested using the continuous clock pattern which are sent out from the dram device without the need for sending out continuous MRR commands.
P418-031 §9.10.2 The MR25 OP[3] is set to “1” to enable this feature.
P418-033 §9.10.2 NOTE 14 The max value of tTx_DQ_Rj_1UI_NoBUJ and tTx_DQ_Rj_NU_NoBUJ can be 6mUI RMS
P419-001 §9.11 Tx DQ stressed eye height and eye width must meet minimum specification values at BER=E-9 and confidence level 99.5%.
P419-003 §9.11 In order to support different Host Receiver (Rx) designs, it is the responsibility of the Host to insure the advanced DQS edges are adjusted accordingly via the Read DQS Offset Timing mode register settings (MR40 OP[3:0]).
P420-016 §9.11.1 NOTE 2 Refer to the minimum Bit Error Rate (BER) requirements for DDR5
P420-017 §9.11.1 NOTE 3 The validation methodology for these parameters will be covered in future ballot(s)
P420-018 §9.11.1 NOTE 4 Mismatch is defined as DQS to DQ mismatch, in UI increments
P420-019 §9.11.1 NOTE 5 The number of UI’s accumulated will depend on the speed of the link.
P420-020 §9.11.1 For higher speeds, higher UI accumulation may be specified.
P420-021 §9.11.1 For lower speeds, N=4,5 UI may not be applicable
P420-022 §9.11.1 NOTE 6 Duty Cycle of the DQ pins must be adjusted as close to 50% as possible using the Global and Per Pin Duty Cycle Adjuster feature prior to running this test
P420-023 §9.11.1 NOTE 7 The Mode Registers for the Duty Cycle Adjuster are MR43 and MR44.
P420-024 §9.11.1 Also the Mode Registers for the Per Pin DCA of DQS are MR103-MR110, the Mode Registers for the Per Pin DCA of DQLx are MR(133+8x) and MR(134+8x), where 0≤x≤7, and the Mode Registers for the Per Pin DCA of DQUy are MR(197+8y) and MR(198+8y), where 0≤y≤7.
P420-025 §9.11.1 NOTE 8 Spread Spectrum Clocking (SSC) must be disabled while running this test
P420-026 §9.11.1 NOTE 9 These parameters are tested using the continuous PRBS8 LFSR training pattern which are sent out on all DQ lanes off the dram device without the need for sending out continuous MRR commands.
P420-027 §9.11.1 The MR25 OP[3] is set to “1” to enable this feature.
P420-029 §9.11.1 NOTE 11 Matched DQS to DQ would require the DQs to be adjusted by 0.5UI to place it in the center of the DQ eye.

P420-031 §9.11.1 Generally, for XUI mismatch the DQ must be adjusted XUI + 0.5UI to be placed in the center of the eye.

P423-002 §10 Future Speed Bins, (which are just placeholders) are defined as:
P439-010 §10.14 1. Minimum timing parameters are defined according to the rules in the Rounding Definitions and Algorithms section.
P439-011 §10.14 2. The translation of all timing parameters from ns values to nCK values shall follow the Rounding Algorithm.
P439-012 §10.14 The translation of tAA to CL shall follow the explicit combinations listed in the Speed Bin Tables.
P439-014 §10.14 When selecting tCK(avg), requirements from the CL setting as well as requirements from the CWL setting shall be fulfilled.
P439-015 §10.14 4. ‘Reserved’ settings are not allowed.
P439-016 §10.14 The user shall program a different value.
P439-017 §10.14 5. This column shows the intended native speed bin timings to be replaced and supported when down clocking.
P439-018 §10.14 This column does not necessarily show the actual minimum speed bin timings allowed and supported when down clocking because the timings could be faster according to the Rounding Algorithm, depending on the specific speed bin and down clock frequency combination.
P439-019 §10.14 6. DDR5-3200 AC timings apply if the DRAM operates slower than the 2933 MT/s data rate.
P439-020 §10.14 This is not limited to only the Speed Bin Table timings.
P439-021 §10.14 7. Parameters apply from tCK(avg)min to tCK(avg)max. 8.
P439-022 §10.14 tRC(min) shall always be greater than or equal to tRAS(min) + tRP(min), and when using the appropriate rounding algorithms, nRC(min) shall always be greater than or equal to nRAS(min) + nRP(min).
P439-024 §10.14 tCK(avg).max of 1.010 ns (1980 MT/s data rate) is defined to allow for 1% SSC down-spreading at a data rate of 2000 MT/s according to JESD404-1.
P439-025 §10.14 10. Each speed bin lists the timing requirements that need to be supported in order for a given DRAM to be JEDEC standard.
P439-026 §10.14 The JEDEC standard does not require support for all speed bins within a given speed.
P439-027 §10.14 The JEDEC standard requires meeting the parameters for a least one of the listed speed bins.
P439-028 §10.14 11. Any speed bin also supports functional operation at slower frequencies as shown in the table which are not subject to Production Tests but are verified by Design/Characterization.
P439-029 §10.14 12. The CL Algorithm can be used to mathematically determine the valid CAS Latencies listed in the Speed Bin Tables.
P439-032 §10.14 Because the DDR5 SDRAM specification only supports even CAS Latencies, odd CAS Latencies are rounded up to the next even CAS Latency.
P439-034 §10.14 If tAA(corrected) or tRCDtRP(corrected) are violated, the CL Algorithm uses a slower combination of tAA(target) and tRCDtRP(target) to return slower valid CAS Latencies.
P439-035 §10.14 The DDR5 SDRAM can support up to four valid CAS Latencies, CL(AN), CL(B), CL(BN), and CL(C), for a given frequency.
P439-036 §10.14 tAA(corrected) and tRCDtRP(corrected) are calculated by reducing tAA(min), tRCD(min), and tRP(min) by the Rounding Algorithm correction factor.
P439-037 §10.14 The proper setting of CL shall be determined by the memory controller, either by using the Speed Bin Tables, or by using the CL Algorithm, or by some other means.
P439-038 §10.14 Refer to the Rounding Definitions and Algorithm section for more information.
P439-039 §10.14 When CRC is enabled, CL is increased according to the Read CRC Latency Adder.
P439-040 §10.14 When CRC is enabled, there is no Write CRC Latency Adder, so CWL is still calculated based on the CL value during CRC disabled.
P439-041 §10.14 // Variables already defined in other areas of the DDR5 SDRAM specification CorrFact = 0.30 // (%) Rounding Algorithm correction factor ScaledCorrFact = 997 // Scaled correction factor (1000*(1-0.30%)) tCKreal =1011-952, 682-238 // (ps) Real application tCK(AVG) (1980-2100MT/s, 2933-8400MT/s) tAAmin MONO=14000-17500, 3DS=16000-20000 // (ps) From Speed Bin Tables and DIMM SPD bytes 30-31 tRCDtRPmin MONO=14000-17500, 3DS=14000-17500 // (ps) From Speed Bin Tables and DIMM SPD bytes 32-33 (tRCD=tRP) tAAcorr = TRUNC(tAAmin*ScaledCorrFactor/1000) // (ps) Corrected tAA(min) per the Rounding Algorithm rules tRCDtRPcorr = TRUNC(tRCDtRPmin*ScaledCorrFactor/1000) // (ps) Corrected tRCD(min), tRP(min) per the Rounding Algorithm FUNC[RA(targ)] = TRUNC((targ*ScaledCorrFact/tCKstd+1000)/1000) // (nCK) Use Rounding Algorithm to convert bin target timing to nCK // Round tCKreal down to the next faster standard frequency (tCK in ps) IF (TRUNC(2000000/(2000*99%))>=TRUNC(tCKreal)>=TRUNC(2000000/(2000*105%))) // Check for 1980-2100 nominal data rates tCKstd=TRUNC(2000000/2000) // Assign standard 2000 tCK (ps) ELSE IF (TRUNC(2000000/(2000+7*(133+1/3)))>=TRUNC(tCKreal)>=TRUNC(2000000/3200)) // Check for 2933-3200 nominal data rates tCKstd=TRUNC(2000000/3200) // Assign standard 3200 tCK (ps) ELSE FOR (DataRateNom=3200; DataRateNom<=8000; DataRateNom=DataRateNom+400) // Check for >3200-8400 nominal data rates IF (TRUNC(2000000/DataRateNom)>TRUNC(tCKreal)>=TRUNC(2000000/(DataRateNom+400))) tCKstd=TRUNC(2000000/(DataRateNom+400)) // Assign standard 3600-8400 tCK (ps) ELSE tCKstd=RESERVED // No valid data rate found
P440-001 §10.14 // Timing targets (ps) that have been used to define the Speed Bin Tables // MONO targets 3DS targets BinAN_tAAtarg = 14000 BinAN_tAAtarg = 16000 // tAA target for AN bins BinB__tAAtarg = 16000 BinB__tAAtarg = 18500 // tAA target for AN, B bins BinBN_tAAtarg = 16000 BinBN_tAAtarg = 18500 // tAA target for AN, B, BN bins BinC__tAAtarg = 17500 BinC__tAAtarg = 20000 // tAA target for AN, B, BN, C bins BinAN_tRCDtRPtarg = 14000 BinAN_tRCDtRPtarg = 14000 // tRCD, tRP target for AN bins BinBN_tRCDtRPtarg = 16000 BinBN_tRCDtRPtarg = 16000 // tRCD, tRP target for AN, B, BN bins BinC__tRCDtRPtarg = 17500 BinC__tRCDtRPtarg = 17500 // tRCD, tRP target for AN, B, BN, C bins IF (TRUNC(2000000/3600)>tCKstd) // tRCD, tRP target for B bins is frequency dependent BinB__tRCDtRPtarg = 16000 BinB__tRCDtRPtarg = 16000 // tRCD, tRP target for AN, B bins data rates faster than 3600 ELSE // 16250=(2000000/3200)*EVEN(TRUNC((BinB__tRCDtRPtarg*ScaledCorrFact/(2000000/3200)+1000)/1000)) BinB__tRCDtRPtarg = 16250 BinB__tRCDtRPtarg = 16250 // tRCD, tRP target for AN, B bins for data rates 3600 and slower
P440-002 §10.14 // CL Algorithm using variables defined above // Up to four valid CL’s can be returned for a specific freq: CL(AN), CL(B), CL(BN), CL(C), depending on tAAmin, tRCDmin, tRPmin // The B and BN bins return the same CL // Only even CL’s (not odd CL’s) are valid per the DDR5 SDRAM specification // nRCD, nRP are only even at standard native frequencies for the AN, BN bins (can be even or odd at intermediate frequencies) // nRCD, nRP may be even or odd at standard native frequencies for the B, C bins (can be even or odd at intermediate frequencies) IF (TRUNC(2000000/2000)=tCKstd) // CL22 is the only valid CL for 1980-2100 data rates CL(AN)=22 // Valid even CL for AN bins CL(B )=22 // Valid even CL for AN, B, bins CL(BN)=22 // Valid even CL for AN, B, BN bins CL(C )=22 // Valid even CL for AN, B, BN, C bins ELSE IF (TRUNC(2000000/3200)>=tCKstd>=TRUNC(2000000/8400)) // Valid CL for 2933-8400 data rates IF ((EVEN(RA(BinAN_tAAtarg))*tCKstd>=tAAcorr)AND(EVEN(RA(BinAN_tRCDtRPtarg))*tCKstd>=tRCDtRPcorr)) // nRCD, nRP even only CL(AN)=EVEN(RA(BinAN_tAAtarg)) // Valid even CL for AN bins CL(B )=EVEN(RA(BinB__tAAtarg)) // Valid even CL for AN, B bins CL(BN)=EVEN(RA(BinBN_tAAtarg)) // Valid even CL for AN, B, BN bins CL(C )=EVEN(RA(BinC__tAAtarg)) // Valid even CL for AN, B, BN, C bins ELSE IF ((EVEN(RA(BinB__tAAtarg))*tCKstd>=tAAcorr)AND( (RA(BinB__tRCDtRPtarg))*tCKstd>=tRCDtRPcorr)) // nRCD, nRP even, odd CL(AN)=RESERVED // Valid even CL for AN bins CL(B )=EVEN(RA(BinB__tAAtarg)) // Valid even CL for AN, B bins CL(BN)=EVEN(RA(BinBN_tAAtarg)) // Valid even CL for AN, B, BN bins CL(C )=EVEN(RA(BinC__tAAtarg)) // Valid even CL for AN, B, BN, C bins ELSE IF ((EVEN(RA(BinBN_tAAtarg))*tCKstd>=tAAcorr)AND(EVEN(RA(BinBN_tRCDtRPtarg))*tCKstd>=tRCDtRPcorr)) // nRCD, nRP even only CL(AN)=RESERVED // Valid even CL for AN bins CL(B )=RESERVED // Valid even CL for AN, B bins CL(BN)=EVEN(RA(BinBN_tAAtarg)) // Valid even CL for AN, B, BN bins CL(C )=EVEN(RA(BinC__tAAtarg)) // Valid even CL for AN, B, BN, C bins ELSE IF ((EVEN(RA(BinC__tAAtarg))*tCKstd>=tAAcorr)AND( (RA(BinC__tRCDtRPtarg))*tCKstd>=tRCDtRPcorr)) // nRCD, nRP even, odd CL(AN)=RESERVED // Valid even CL for AN bins CL(B )=RESERVED // Valid even CL for AN, B bins CL(BN)=RESERVED // Valid even CL for AN, B, BN bins CL(C )=EVEN(RA(BinC__tAAtarg)) // Valid even CL for AN, B, BN, C bins ELSE // No valid CL found (tAAmin, tRCDmin, tRPmin are too slow) CL(AN)=RESERVED // Valid even CL for AN bins CL(B )=RESERVED // Valid even CL for AN, B bins CL(BN)=RESERVED // Valid even CL for AN, B, BN bins CL(C )=RESERVED // Valid even CL for AN, B, BN, C bins ELSE // No valid data rate found CL(AN)=RESERVED // Valid even CL for AN bins CL(B )=RESERVED // Valid even CL for AN, B bins CL(BN)=RESERVED // Valid even CL for AN, B, BN bins CL(C )=RESERVED // Valid even CL for AN, B, BN, C bins
P440-003 §10.14 13. tRAS(max) shall always be less than or equal to 5*tREFI1(max) during Normal Refresh Mode and less than or equal to 9*tREFI2(max) during Fine Granularity Refresh Mode, and when using the rounding algorithms, nRAS(max) shall always be less than or equal to 5*nREFI1(max) during Normal Refresh Mode and less than or equal to 9*nREFI2(max) during Fine Granularity Refresh Mode.

P453-001 §11.1 In this chapter, IDD, IPP, and IDDQ measurement conditions such as test load and patterns are defined.
P453-003 §11.1 IDD currents (such as IDD0, IDDQ0, IPP0, IDD0F, IDDQ0F, IPP0F, IDD2N, IDDQ2N, IPP2N, IDD2NT, IDDQ2NT, IPP2NT, IDD2P, IDDQ2P, IPP2P, IDD3N, IDDQ3N, IPP3N, IDD3P, IDDQ3P, IPP3P, IDD4R, IDDQ4R, IPP4R, IDD4RC, IDD4W, IDDQ4W, IPP4W, IDD4WC, IDD5F, IDDQ5F, IPP5F, IDD5B, IDDQ5B, IPP5B, IDD5C, IDDQ5C, IPP5C, IDD6N, IDDQ6N, IPP6N, IDD6E, IDDQ6E, IPP6E, IDD7, IDDQ7, IPP7, IDD8, IDDQ8, IPP8 and IDD9, IDDQ9, IPP9) are measured as time-averaged currents with all VDD balls of the DDR5 SDRAM under test tied together.
P453-004 §11.1 Any IDDQ or IPP current is not included in IDD currents.
P453-005 §11.1 IDDQ currents are measured as time-averaged currents with all VDDQ balls of the DDR5 SDRAM under test tied together.
P453-006 §11.1 Any IDD or IPP current is not included in IDDQ currents.
P453-007 §11.1 IPP currents are measured as time-averaged currents with all VPP balls of the DDR5 SDRAM under test tied together.
P453-008 §11.1 Any IDD or IDDQ current is not included in IPP currents.
P453-009 §11.1 Attention: IDDQ values cannot be directly used to calculate IO power of the DDR5 SDRAM.
P453-010 §11.1 They can be used to support correlation of simulated IO power to actual IO power as outlined in Figure 246.
P453-011 §11.1 For IDD, IPP and IDDQ measurements, the following definitions apply:
P453-012 §11.1 “0” and “LOW” is defined as VIN <= VILAC(max).
P453-013 §11.1 “1” and “HIGH” is defined as VIN >= VIHAC(min).
P453-014 §11.1 “MID-LEVEL” is defined as inputs are VREF = 0.75 * VDDQ.
P453-015 §11.1 Basic IDD, IPP and IDDQ Measurement Conditions are described in Table 301.
P453-016 §11.1 Detailed IDD, IPP and IDDQ Measurement-Loop Patterns are described in Chapter 11.2 through Chapter 11.11.
P453-017 §11.1 IDD Measurements are done after properly initializing and training the DDR5 SDRAM.
P453-018 §11.1 This includes but is not limited to setting TDQS_t disabled in MR5; CRC disabled in MR50; DM disabled in MR5; 1N mode enabled and set CS assertion duration (MR2:OP[4]) as 1B in MR2, unless otherwise specified in the IDD, IDDQ and IPP patterns’ conditions definitions;
P453-019 §11.1 Attention: The IDD, IPP and IDDQ Measurement-Loop Patterns need to be executed at least one time before actual IDD, IDDQ or IPP measurement is started, with the exception of IDD9 which can be measured any time after the DRAM has entered MBIST mode.
P453-021 §11.1 For all IDD, IDDQ and IPP measurement loop timing parameters, refer to the timing parameters defined in the spec to calculate the nCK required.

P473-001 §13.1 Figure 247 represents the effective reference load of 50 ohms used in defining the relevant AC timing parameters of the device as well as output slew rate measurements.
P473-002 §13.1 Ron nominal of DQ, DQS_t and DQS_c drivers uses 34 ohms to specify the relevant AC timing parameter values of the
P473-006 §13.1 The nominal reference level of an Output signal can be approximated by the following:
P473-010 §13.1 level or midpoint of an output signal is at the widest part of the output signal’s eye.
P473-011 §13.1 Prior to measuring AC parameters, the reference level of the verification tool should be set to an appropriate level.
P473-012 §13.1 It is not intended as a precise representation of any particular system environment or a depiction of the actual load presented by a production tester.
P473-013 §13.1 System designers should use IBIS or other simulation tools to correlate the timing reference load to a system environment.
P474-001 §13.2 Software algorithms for calculation of timing parameters are subject to rounding errors from many sources.
P474-002 §13.2 For example, a system may use a memory clock with a nominal frequency of 2200 MHz (4400 MT/s) for the DDR5-4400 speed bin, which mathematically yields a nominal clock period tCK(AVG) of 0.454545...
P474-004 §13.2 In most cases, it is impossible to express all digits after the decimal point exactly, and rounding must be used.
P474-006 §13.2 Rules for rounding must be defined to allow optimization of device performance without violating device parameters.
P474-007 §13.2 All timing parameters specified in the time domain (ns, ps, etc.) which must then be converted to the clock domain (nCK units) shall be defined to align with these rules.
P474-008 §13.2 The key point is, minimum and maximum timing parameter values shall generally use the same rounding rules used to define tCK(AVG)min and tCK(AVG)max. The resulting rounding algorithms rely on results that are within correction factors of device testing and specification to avoid losing performance due to rounding errors.
P474-010 §13.2 DEFINING TIMING PARAMETER VALUES: Minimum and maximum timing parameter values, including tCK(AVG)min and tCK(AVG)max, are rounded down and to be defined to 1 ps of accuracy in the DDR5 SDRAM specification based on the non-rounded nominal tCK(AVG) for a given speed bin.
P474-011 §13.2 If the nominal timing parameter values require more than 1 ps of accuracy, they can be rounded down (faster) to the next 1 ps according to the rounding algorithms, and the DDR5 SDRAM is responsible for absorbing the resulting small parameter extensions.
P474-013 §13.2 For example, this extends the DDR5-4400 tCK(AVG)min definition to be exactly 0.454 ns which is slightly smaller (faster) than the nominal memory clock period of 0.454545...
P474-015 §13.2 CALCULATING THE REAL MINIMUM TIMING PARAMETER VALUES: For minimum timing parameters, other than tCK(AVG)min, to avoid losing performance due to additional erroneous nCKs and to calculate the true real minimum values, their nominal values listed in the DDR5 SDRAM specification must be reduced (faster) by the maximum %error (correction factor) used to define tCK(AVG)min. The DDR5 SDRAM is responsible for absorbing the resulting small parameter extensions.
P474-016 §13.2 For example, tWRmin has a nominal value of 30.000ns, however, applying the 0.30% correction factor allows a more aggressive timing (for example, 29.910ns) to be supported, which allows the intended smaller (faster) nCK value to be maintained when rounding tCK(AVG)min down to the next 1 ps.
P474-017 §13.2 Note, parameter values defined to be 0 ps do not need to be reduced by a correction factor, and therefore don’t require these rounding algorithms.
P474-018 §13.2 CALCULATING THE REAL MAXIMUM TIMING PARAMETER VALUES: For maximum timing parameters, including tCK(AVG)max, to avoid losing performance due to excluding erroneous nCKs and to calculate the true real maximum values, their nominal values listed in the DDR5 SDRAM specification must be increased (slower) by the maximum %error caused by rounding tCK(AVG) down to the next 1 ps.
P474-019 §13.2 The DDR5 SDRAM is responsible for absorbing the resulting small parameter extensions.
P474-020 §13.2 For example, tREFImax has a nominal value of 3.9 us.
P474-023 §13.2 So the true real tREFImax value is 3.916386...
P474-025 §13.2 ROUNDING ALGORITHM FOR MINIMUM TIMING PARAMETER VALUES: Round down only integer number math is commonly used in the industry to calculate nCK values.
P474-026 §13.2 This rounding algorithm for minimum timing parameters uses scaling by 1000 to allow use of integer math.
P474-027 §13.2 Nominal minimum timing parameters like tWRmin, tRCDmin, etc. which are programmed in systems in numbers of clocks (nCK) but expressed in units of time(ps), are rounded down to the next 1ps, multiplied by the scaled inverse correction factor (1000-3=997) prior to division by the application memory clock period rounded down to the next 1ps, and adding 1 scaled by 1000 to that result effectively adds 1nCK.
P474-028 §13.2 Division by 1000 undoes the scaling effects, resulting in a number of clocks as the final answer which has been effectively rounded up to the next 1 nCK by adding 1nCK in the previous step and then rounding down to the next 1 nCK.
P474-029 §13.2 The caveat is, effectively adding 1 prior to rounding down is mostly equivalent to rounding up except when the result is equal to an integer in which case the result won’t be rounded down as intended, and therefore performance would be lost.
P474-030 §13.2 To address this, the maximum 0.28% correction factor needed for 3600 MHz (0.277ns/ 0.2777777...ns-100%) operation has been increased slightly to 0.30% in this rounding algorithms.
P474-031 §13.2 This accounts for all integer boundary conditions, except for the specific case when the nominal minimum timing parameter value is defined to be 0 ps.
P474-032 §13.2 No rounding is required for parameter values equal to 0 ps.
P474-033 §13.2 This rounding algorithm is not optimized for parameter values that are less than or equal to 0ps, and may result in unintended lost performance if used for parameter values that are less than or equal to 0ps.
P475-004 §13.2 ROUNDING ALGORITHM FOR MAXIMUM TIMING PARAMETER VALUES: Round down only integer number math is commonly used in the industry to calculate nCK values.
P475-005 §13.2 This rounding algorithm is used for maximum timing parameters.
P475-006 §13.2 Nominal maximum timing parameters like tREFImax, etc. which programmed in systems in numbers of clocks (nCK) but expressed in units of time (ps), are rounded down to the next 1 ps prior to division by the application memory clock period rounded down to the next 1 ps, resulting in a number of clocks as the final answer which is rounded down to the next 1 nCK.
P475-007 §13.2 No rounding is required for parameter values equal to 0 ps, but this rounding algorithm can still be used for parameter values equal to 0ps and no performance will be lost.
P475-008 §13.2 This rounding algorithm is not optimized for parameter values that are less than 0 ps (negative parameter values), and may result in violating the parameter specification if used for parameter values that are less than 0 ps.
P475-010 §13.2 CL ALGORITHM FOR STANDARD SPEED BINS: The math rounding algorithms shall be used for all timing parameters when converting from the time domain (ns, ps, etc.) to the clock domain (nCK units), except for when converting tAA to CL.
P475-011 §13.2 If these rounding algorithms are used to convert tAA to CL, they’ll return invalid CL’s for some cases when down clocking (and the DIMM SPD CL Mask doesn’t protect against all of these cases).
P475-012 §13.2 The proper setting of CL shall be determined by the memory controller, either by using the speed bin tables, or by using the CL algorithm, or by some other means.
P475-013 §13.2 Refer to the Speed Bins and Operations section for more information.
P475-015 §13.2 CL ALGORITHM FOR CUSTOM SPEED BINS: If the DDR5 SDRAM supports non-standard tCK, tAA, tRCD, and tRP speed bin timings, the CL algorithm will still only return valid CL’s as defined in the speed bin tables, which may not be the intended CL’s for non-standard speed bins.
P475-016 §13.2 In these cases, the rounding algorithms may need to be used to convert tAA to CL, instead of the CL algorithm.
P475-017 §13.2 The CL returned by the rounding algorithms shall be incremented up to the next supported CL according to the DIMM SPD CL Mask.

P487-001 §14.1 To achieve efficient module power supply design for JEDEC-standard DDR5 DIMMs, minimum timings as well as limitations in the number of DRAMs are provided for Refresh, and Write operations occurring on a single module.
P487-002 §14.1 As well, since these modules are organized as two independent 36-bit or 40-bit channels (32 bits for non-ECC DIMMs), additional restrictions apply in order to limit localized power delivery noise on the module.
P487-003 §14.1 To provide best performance, the different channels may initiate commands on the same cycle provided the rank to rank timings are met, the maximum number of DRAMs in a given activity is not exceeded, and the applicable component timings shown elsewhere in this specification are met.
P487-004 §14.1 The timing and operational relationships for DDR5 DIMMs are shown in Table 326.
P487-018 §14.1 NOTE 1 Any combination of commands with up to the maximum of die per channel and per DIMM, per condition is allowed.
P487-019 §14.1 NOTE 2 Refresh commands to different channels do not require stagger.
P487-020 §14.1 NOTE 3 tRFC_dlr must be met for refresh commands to different logical ranks within a package rank on the same channel.
P487-021 §14.1 NOTE 4 Any DRAM is considered to be in Refresh mode until tRFC time has been met.
P487-022 §14.1 NOTE 5 tCCD parameters must be met for Write and Write-Pattern commands to different logical ranks or physical ranks within the same channel; no overlapping write data bus activity is allowed on two physical or logical ranks within the same channel.
P487-023 §14.1 NOTE 6 Write and Write-Pattern commands to different channels do not require stagger.
P487-024 §14.1 NOTE 7 Each rank consists of one group of DRAMs making up a 36 or 40 bit channel (32 bits for non-ECC DIMMs).
P487-025 §14.1 NOTE 8 These restrictions only apply to explicit all-banks refresh commands (REFab) and not to self-refresh entry or exit
P487-026 §14.1 NOTE 9 Restrictions apply to DIMMs built with JEDEC PMICXXXX, but may not apply to DIMMs built with higher current capacity PMICs
P488-002 §15.1.1 This limits the number of failure patterns seen by the memory controller such that correction of many failures can be reliably performed in DIMMs with one ECC device.
P488-003 §15.1.1 Many internal DRAM failures may impact only a portion of the data from a fetch.
P488-004 §15.1.1 The likelihood of a specific component failing may also vary between process generations and DRAM vendors.
P488-005 §15.1.1 These guidelines can be used by DDR5 DRAM’s to bound failures from the components most likely to fail.
P488-006 §15.1.1 These design guidelines can only address failures that impact a portion of the data from a 128-bit fetch.
P488-007 §15.1.1 Failures that impact all the data in a 128-bit fetch from a device (e.g., device failure, bank failure) cannot be bounded as described here.
P488-008 §15.1.2 In an x8 device bounded failures are defined by the following qualities:
P488-009 §15.1.2 A bounded fault will not impact more than 32 bits of data in a 128-bit fetch
P488-010 §15.1.2 The data bits impacted by a bounded failure will be confined to at most 2 DQs (i.e., the failures will be DQ aligned)
P488-011 §15.1.2 The DQs transmitting data impacted by the failure will both be in either the first nibble or the second nibble of a burst.
P488-012 §15.1.2 That is, the impacted DQs will both be in the set of the first 4 DQs (DQ0, DQ1, DQ2, DQ3) or both in the set of the last four DQs (DQ4, DQ5, DQ6, DQ7)
P488-014 §15.1.2 Device on the left has a bounded fault in lanes DQ0 and DQ1 in the first nibble.
P488-015 §15.1.2 Device on the right has a bounded fault in lanes DQ4 and DQ6 in the second nibble.
P489-001 §15.1.2 An x4 device has similarly defined qualities for the failure boundaries, with the noted exception of the number of bits impacted by a bounded failure.
P489-002 §15.1.2 In a DRAM device in a 9x4 configuration bounded failures should not impact more than 16 bits.
P489-003 §15.1.2 However, in a 10x4 device the bounded failure may impact up to 32 bits.
P489-004 §15.1.2 In x4 devices, bounded failures are defined by the following qualities:
P489-005 §15.1.2 A bounded failure in a DRAM device in a 9x4 configuration shall not impact more than 16 bits of data in a 128-bit prefetch.
P489-006 §15.1.2 A bounded failure in a DRAM device in a 10x4 configuration shall not impact more than 32 bits of data in a 128-bit prefetch.
P489-007 §15.1.2 For a device in a 9x4 configuration, the data bits impacted by a bounded failure will be confined to one DQ.
P489-008 §15.1.2 For a device in a 10x4 configuration, the data bits impacted by a bounded failure will be confined to at most two DQs.
P490-001 §15.2 To maintain the bounded fault design guidelines, miscorrections by the on-die ECC must be restricted when a bounded fault causes a multi-bit error.
P490-002 §15.2 In devices with bounded fault it is suggested that the DRAM vendor uses an on-die ECC code that maintains the fault boundaries.
P490-003 §15.2 That is, if an error is contained in one boundary, then the on-die ECC should not spread the error into a second boundary by miscorrection.
P490-004 §15.2 Note that if faults are not bounded in the device, then the on-die ECC does not need to have these properties.
P490-005 §15.2 To restrict miscorrection in devices following bounded fault design guidelines, the data may be divided into blocks aligned with the bounded fault and miscorrection should be restricted in the case an error is contained in a single data block.
P490-006 §15.2 The 128 data bits used to compute a set of 8 check bits may be divided into data blocks up to 32 bits in size.
P490-007 §15.2 These data blocks are to be determined by the memory vendor to best align with internal DRAM faults.
P490-008 §15.2 For example, a common component fault may impact 32 bits per 128 data bits, then the vendor may choose to divide the 128 data bits into four 32-bit blocks each of which correspond to the bits impacted by one of the components failing.
P490-009 §15.2 If a multi-bit error occurs on a read and is limited to one data block, then the on-die ECC SEC code should do one of the following:
P490-012 §15.2 Detect the error (a SEC code may detect some multi-bit errors, but detection of these errors is not guaranteed)
P490-013 §15.2 The selection of block size and data blocks is determined by the vendor to best suit the device architecture and possible modes of fault.
P491-001 §15.3 The DRAM device may optionally support the suppressing of writeback for x4 devices and ECS writebacks.
P491-002 §15.3 DRAM may implement the feature in MR9 or MR15.
P491-003 §15.3 DDR5 SPD Byte 14 Bits[2:1] indicates if feature is supported and will also indicate whether to use MR9 or MR15 for enabling the modes.
P491-004 §15.3 If MR9 OP1=1 or MR15 OP7=1, then x4 DRAM’s on writes will perform an internal ‘read-modify-write’ operation for BL16.
P491-005 §15.3 BL32 mode does not requires an internal ‘read modify write’ operation.
P491-006 §15.3 The DRAM will correct any single bit errors that result from the internal read of 128 bit data before merging the incoming 64 bit data and then re-compute 8 ECC Check bits.
P491-007 §15.3 Note that ECC check bits are computed after merging of the incoming data with the corrected data from the array.
P491-008 §15.3 DRAM will then write the incoming 64b data to the array along with recomputed 8 ECC Check bits.
P491-009 §15.3 DRAM will suppress the writeback of 64 bit data that was fetched from the array irrespective of whether the 64 bit data needed any correction or not.
P491-010 §15.3 Suppression of writeback (where applicable due to BC8 or DM usage) is not supported on x8/x16 devices; MR9:OP[1] or MR15:OP[7] must be set to “0B”.
P491-011 §15.3 Suppression of writeback may or may not
P491-012 §15.3 occur when BC8 mode is used on x4 devices.
P491-013 §15.3 If MR9 OP0=1 or MR15 OP6=1, then DRAM will suppress writeback of data and ECC Check bits during ECS operation for x4, x8, and x16 DDR5.
P491-014 §15.3 DRAM will continue to count errors to provide transparency.
P491-018 §15.3 NOTE 1 DDR5 SPD Byte 14 Bits[2:1] indicates if feature is supported and will also indicate whether to use MR9 or MR15 for enabling the modes.
P491-019 §15.3 This byte defines support for SDRAM fault handling features and for wide on-die temperature sensing range (see MR4).
P492-002 §16.1 Note that some of the methodologies in this standard may reference qualitative means (for example, “slowly”, “a lot”).
P492-003 §16.1 In such cases this standard attempts to give some guidance as to what quantitative term should be assigned to those qualitative statements.
P492-004 §16.1 However, it must be noted that the numbers assigned are not absolute.
P492-005 §16.2 Most of the voltage and timing measurements are performed using real time scopes and/or Bit Error Rate (BER) testers.
